# SKA formalization

Lean 4 (v4.33.1, no dependencies).  The aim: the SKA compiler and its IR
interpreter, as the Nock inside a steel pill, agree with Nock.  The compiler
is not ported to Lean and no port is trusted.

## Layout

| Directory | Content |
| --- | --- |
| `core/` | The trusted part, with no proofs: the Nock specification, `cue`, the pill as an atom, and the statements. It imports nothing from `lemmas/`. |
| `lemmas/` | Everything else: executable Nock, tactics, the pill's nouns as Lean definitions, models and proofs. |
| `pill/steel.pill` | The pill that `core/Pill.lean` and `lemmas/Pill/` are generated from. |
| `tools/` | The generators and the memory cap. |

## Build

```sh
cd formalization
tools/capped.sh -m 3G lake build core              # 3 s
tools/capped.sh -m 3G lake build                   # about 100 s from scratch, 2.7 GB peak
tools/capped.sh -m 3G lake env lean lemmas/Audit.lean  # axioms of the main theorems
```

`tools/gen/` holds the Python scripts that generate proof variants from the
text of existing proofs (`fraguj.py` writes `CompFragUJGen.lean`, `sevenv.py`
writes `CompSevenV.lean`, `fivev.py` writes `CompFiveV.lean`, `fourv.py`
writes `CompFourV.lean`, `tenv.py` writes `CompTenV.lean`, `runlc.py` writes
`CompRunLC.lean`, `runlcm.py` writes `CompRunLCM.lean` (from `CompRunLC.lean`), `kernlt.py` prints `kernBoth_lt` and `kernLoop_lt` of
`CompWholeKern.lean` (from `CompUnproxyRegs.lean`), `walkdi.py` prints
`walkLazy_okDI` of `CompWholeKern.lean` (from `CompCollapseGate.lean`), `monotry.py` cuts the trap of
`+mono-try-call` for `CompRunDirectMono.lean`, `monoblk.py` cuts `crD7Nm` for `CompRunDirectMonoB.lean`; run each from
`formalization/`, every extraction and replacement asserts its match count).
`mrops.py` writes the 25 lemmas `mr_op_<tag>` and `mr_op` of
`CompMapRegs.lean` between the lines `-- BEGIN mrops` and `-- END mrops`.
`pairlsx_fsx.py STMT HEAD` writes `CompFrontSX.lean` from `frontS_pair` of
`CompFrontS.lean` (`STMT` the new statement, `pairlsx_fsx_stmt.lean`, `HEAD` the
file's header, `pairlsx_fsx_head.lean`), `pairlsx_fronts.py` writes
`CompPairLSXFronts.lean` (from `CompPairLSSem.lean`), `pairlsx_x.py` writes
`CompPairLSXCore.lean` and `CompPairLSXSem.lean` (from `CompPairLSCore.lean`,
`CompPairLSSem.lean`), `pairlsx_ok.py` writes `CompPairLSX.lean` (from
`pairLS_ok` of `CompPairLS.lean`).  `apartsx.py` writes `CompLOKSXUnary.lean`
(the `ApartS` lemmas of `[4 p]`, `[3 p]`, the indirect call and `[5 p q]`, from
the openings of `lokS_four_run`, `lokS_three_run`, `lokS_indirect_runF`,
`lokS_five_run`).

`tools/capped.sh` runs a command under a memory cap (8 GB unless `-m SIZE`).
Use it for anything that makes the kernel evaluate: an uncapped check of `cue`
on the whole pill took the machine down.

`tools/gen/` holds two helpers for writing links by hand, run under the cap
like the rest.  `nouns.py` parses the generated nouns of `lemmas/Pill`
(`slot`, `show`, `lean` for a Lean literal, `arms` of a battery,
`contains_atom`, `cord`), and `runcases.py 0,1,7` prints the cases of the gate
`+run` of the door of `+comp` as the definitions `crSn`, `crK0`, ... of
`Compiler/CompRunLink.lean`, the named cases written out and the others read
from the pill.  `split.py OUTDIR` cuts the long bodies `crK5`, `crK10`,
`crKc` of `CompRunLink.lean` into the named parts of
`Compiler/CompRunCases.lean` (`cr5Head`, `cr10Tail`, `crcPick`, ...), each
later part named inside the earlier ones so that `crK5 = cr5Head` is a
`kernel_rfl` fact; its `after_push` takes apart the Nock of one `=^`,
`[7 [0 1] [8 A [7 E [7 F REST]]]]`.
`arms.py door` (any other argument: the core `K`) lists the arms of the door
of `+comp` (or of `K`) that carry a `%fast` hint, with their battery axes,
for example `446 comp-need-ord-alloc-regs` and `1014 comp-collapse-shape`.

## core

| File | Content |
| --- | --- |
| `Nock.lean` | Nock 4K, rule for rule: nouns, `Wut`, `Lus`, `Tis`, `Fas`, `Hax` and the relation `Nock`. |
| `Cue.lean` | `cue : Nat → Option Noun`, after `+cue` of hoon.hoon. Written to be read, not run. |
| `Pill.lean` | Generated: the pill file as an atom, in 1024-byte chunks. The same bytes as vere's `steel.c`, which `tools/pill2core.py --check pill/steel.pill steel.c` confirms. |
| `Statement.lean` | `Loads`, `NockEquivalence` (if Nock computes `p`, `+eval-top` returns `[%0 p]` with any scry gate; if `+eval-top` with the scry gate `~` returns `[%0 p]`, Nock computes `p`), `MockEquivalence` (`+mock` returns a toon exactly when `+eval-top` then `+eval-mook` do, for any scry gate), `Halts`, `Full`, and the few definitions they need. Start here. |

The statements quantify over every core that the pill's core can become by
pokes and by runs of the interpreter, except through a `%jets` poke that names
a jet.  Nothing in `core/` is proved.  `lemmas/Compiler/Connect.lean` ties
`Top`, `Mook`, `Run` and `Mock` to the models, and `lemmas/Assembly.lean`
proves the statements from the hypotheses it lists.

Every run in the statements is a run of `Nock`, the specification.  There is
no second relation for hints: since ea6413d `+mink` runs a dynamic hint whose
tag is a cell, and since f7e95af the compiler compiles one, as Nock reduces
it.

The proofs do not work with `Nock`, `Fas` and `Hax` directly but with
`Eval`, `Slot` and `Edit` of `lemmas/Spec.lean` (axes as `Nat`, opcode 6 as
two rules).  `lemmas/SpecEquiv.lean` proves them the same relations
(`nock_iff_eval`, `fas_iff_slot`, `hax_iff_edit`).

What a reader of `core/` has to take on trust beyond the Lean kernel:

1. `Nock` is Nock, and `cue` is `+cue`.
2. `Pill.lean` is the pill file (`tools/pill2core.py --check`).
3. The axes in `Statement.lean` are those of the arms they are named after.
   Each is the formula Hoon compiles the arm's name to against the library.

## lemmas

| File | Content |
| --- | --- |
| `Spec.lean`, `SpecEquiv.lean` | `Slot`, `Edit` and `Eval`, the form of the specification the proofs use, and their equality with `Fas`, `Hax` and `Nock` of `core/` (`fas_iff_slot`, `hax_iff_edit`, `nock_iff_eval`, `nock_eq_some_iff_spec`). |
| `StatementEval.lean` | The definitions of `core/Statement.lean` with `Eval`, `Slot`, `Edit` or `nock` for `Nock`, `Fas`, `Hax` (`pull_iff_nock`, `slam_iff_nock`, `fromPill_iff_eval`, `top_iff_slot`, `mook_iff_slot`, `mock_iff_slot`, `nockEquivalence_iff_eval`, both implications). |
| `Fuel.lean` | `slot?`, `edit?` and the fuelled `evalFuel`. |
| `Sound.lean` | `slot?_iff`, `edit?_iff`, and `evalFuel_sound`: a fuelled run is a derivation of `Eval`. |
| `Run.lean` | `nock`, the evaluator as a partial function, with one rewrite rule per opcode, and `nock_eq_some_iff : nock s f = some p ↔ Eval s f p` (`eval_of_nock`, `nock_of_eval`); `Eval.deterministic`. |
| `Exec.lean` | Symbolic execution with `simp only [nock_exec]`, and simprocs that compute `slot?` and `edit?` at a literal axis.  An axis whose path runs through a variable `v` becomes `slot? rest v`, so a hypothesis about `v` applies. |
| `CueSpec.lean` | `cue` of `core/` checked by the kernel on jams made by `tools/nouns.py`. |
| `CueRel.lean` | `Dec a KV b n e i i'`: the bits of `a` from `b` to `e` encode `n`, where `KV` lists the nouns that references point back to, in the order in which their decoding ends, and `i`, `i'` count the entries decoded before and after.  `cue_of_dec`: `cue` returns the noun of any derivation from bit `0` (proved once, against `cueAt` and its table).  The token lemmas `tokA`, `tokAK`, `tokC`, `tokCK`, `tokR` check one token each with a boolean on a window, a literal that holds 16384 bits of the atom (`WinOK`), which the kernel evaluates with GMP. |
| `CueTac.lean`, `CuePill.lean` | The tactic `cue_dec`, which builds a derivation of `Dec` from the bits of the windows and the generated nouns and leaves every check to the kernel, and the commands `cue_windows` and `cue_nouns`, which define the windows and the nouns of `Pill/Cue/` from the bits of the pill.  `pillList`, the chunks of `pill` as its definition writes them, and `winOK_pill`. |
| `Pill/Cue/` | Generated by `tools/pill2cue.py`: the windows (`Win.lean`), the nouns that references point to and the table `KV` (`J000.lean`, ..., `KV.lean`), the theorems `d<b> : Dec pill KV b n e i i'`, each by `cue_dec` on up to 2000 tokens of the jam and the theorems below it (`D<b>.lean`), and `cue_pill : cue pill = some root` (`Root.lean`), where the kernel compares the noun of the derivation with `root`. |
| `CueWords.lean`, `CueExample.lean` | An earlier `cue` on 32-bit words (namespace `Nock.Words`, apart from the `cue` of `core/`), built for the kernel, checked on a 150-cell jam. |
| `Pill/Arm/<Family>.lean` | Generated: the named nouns of one arm family (`dec.arm`, `dec.battery`, `dec.loop`, ..., and the axis constant `dec.axis`), as a Lean module whose nouns are public but whose bodies are not exposed. |
| `Pill/Nouns/P000.lean`, ... | Generated: the rest of the pill's noun and of the SKA core (definitions named by a hash of their content), as a chain of modules.  A few named nouns live here too: the library core `lib`, the batteries `ca.battery` and `so.battery`, and the arms `dif` of `+ca` and `huge` of `+so`, which quote a noun holding the hoon core, and the analysis core `ana` (the `|%` of `+ska-callgraph`, two cores above `lib`), whose arms `+patch-prod`, `+knit-anno`, `+msg-sock`, `+msg-anno`, `+safe-nomm`, `+safe-fol-fol` have their own arm files. |
| `Pill/Facts.lean` | Generated, kernel-checked: the pill's formula on its trap evaluates to the core, and the arms sit at their axes (`x.arm_at`). |
| `Hoon/Basic.lean` | Flags (`loob`), lists as nouns (`listN`, `appendN`), and `Below`, the predicate on the context of a second-layer arm that reads the first layer. |
| `Hoon/Dec.lean` | `+dec`: `dec_gate (c) (n) (h : 0 < n) : nock [dec.battery n c] dec.battery = some (n - 1)`, and `(dec 0)` crashes (no `Eval` run, `dec_gate_zero_eval`). |
| `Hoon/Arith.lean` | `+add`, `+sub`, `+mul`, `+lth`, `+lte`, `+gte`, `+gth`, `+max`, `+min`, `+dvr`, `+div`, `+mod` compute their `Nat` operations; `+sub` crashes below zero and `+dvr`, `+div`, `+mod` on a zero divisor. |
| `Hoon/MugSpec.lean` | `MugSpec`, what the arms that hash need from `+mug`. |
| `Hoon/Order.lean` | `+dor`, `+gor`, `+mor` compute `dorF`, `gorF`, `morF`, given `MugSpec`. |
| `Hoon/MapModel.lean` | The Lean models `dorF`, `gorF`, `morF`, `putF`, and the map invariant `IsMap`. |
| `Hoon/Treap.lean` | Pure Lean: the three orders are total orders, and `putF` inserts into an `IsMap`. |
| `Hoon/PutBy.lean` | `put_by`: `+put:by` ends on an `IsMap`, keeps the invariant, and holds the new pair and the old pairs under other keys. |
| `Hoon/Lists.lean` | `+lent`, `+weld`, `+flop`, `+snag`, `+scag`, `+slag` against `List Noun`; `+turn`, `+roll`, `+levy`, `+lien` given what their gate argument computes on the elements. |
| `Hoon/Units.lean` | `+need`, `+fall` and `+biff` on units. |
| `Hoon/Maps.lean` | `+get:by`, `+has:by`, `+got:by`, `+gut:by` on an `IsMap` (`+got:by` on a missing key has no `Eval` run). |
| `Hoon/Apt.lean` | `apt_by`: the pill's `+apt:by` says yes exactly on `IsMap` nouns and on the lone nodes `[x ~ ~]` with an atom `x`. |
| `Hoon/TapBy.lean`, `Hoon/TapIn.lean` | `+tap:by` and `+tap:in` (the same noun): the list of the pairs of a map, of the keys of a set. |
| `Hoon/DelBy.lean`, `Hoon/GasBy.lean`, `Hoon/JabBy.lean` | `+del:by` (with the merge of two treaps), `+gas:by`, `+jab:by` on maps. |
| `Hoon/RepBy.lean` | `+rep:by` and `+rep:in` (one noun): a fold over the nodes in pre-order. |
| `Hoon/MapFolds.lean`, `Hoon/SetFolds.lean` | `+key:by`, `+run:by`, `+any:by`, `+wyt` of `+by` and `+in` (one noun); `+gas:in`, `+any:in`, `+run:in`, `+del:in`. |
| `Hoon/Canon.lean` | Pure Lean: treaps are canonical.  Two `IsMap` nouns with the same pairs are equal (`isMap_ext`), and two `IsSet` nouns with the same keys (`isSet_ext`, through `toMap_inj`).  `+tap:by` lists the pairs in strictly decreasing `+gor` order of the keys (`tapList_pairwise`), which determines the list (`tapList_unique`); the order of `+rep` is the pre-order of the canonical treap (`preorder_ext`, `mem_preorder`). |
| `Hoon/DifIn.lean` | `+bif:in` and `+dif:in` against `bifInF` and `difInF` (`bif_gate`, `dif_in_gate`); on sets, `+bif` returns a node with root `b` whose subtrees are sets of the keys before and after `b` in `+gor` order (`bifInF_spec`), and `+dif` returns a set of the keys of `a` that `b` does not hold (`difInF_spec`, `dif_in`).  The merge of `+dif` is `mergeInF` of `+del:in`.  The generator names no noun of `+bif`, so its arm is defined there (`bifArm`, `bif_arm_at` by the kernel). |
| `Hoon/DifBy.lean` | `+bif:by` and `+dif:by` against `bifByF` and `difByF` (`bif_by_gate`, `dif_by_gate`; the nouns of `+bif:by` cut by hand, `bif_by_arm_at`: axis `342` of the door); on maps, `+dif:by` is a map with the pairs of `a` whose keys `b` does not hold (`difByF_spec`, `dif_by`).  Its merge is `mergeF` of `+del:by`; the `?>  ?=(^ c)` of a cell type are `%toss` hints. |
| `Hoon/UniIn.lean` | `+uni:in` (`in.uni.*`): its loop is not structurally recursive (an outer call takes the product of an inner one as a set), so its model is a relation `UniR`, one constructor per branch, and `uni_loop`, `uni_in_gate` run the Nock on every derivation.  On two sets a derivation exists and ends in a set with the members of both and at most as many nodes (`uniP_of_sets`, by induction on the nodes: while the root of `a` comes first in `+mor` order the loop walks down `b` with it at the root, `uni_domA`, and symmetrically `uni_domB`), so the gate returns such a set (`uni_in`). |
| `Hoon/Ju.lean` | The doors `+ju` (get, put, has, del, gas) and `+ja` (get, add). |
| `Hoon/RollUni.lean` | `+roll` with a gate that may crash (`roll_loop_M`, `roll_gate_M`: the fold stops at the first crash); with the gate itself as the model, `+roll` over a list of one element is the gate on that element and the first accumulator (`roll_gate_one`); the arm `uni` of a door `+by` builds its gate (`uni_by_arm`).  `+uni:by` itself is not linked. |
| `Hoon/ListsMore.lean` | `+skim`, `+skip`, `+murn`, `+rear`, `+snip`. |
| `Hoon/ListsSort.lean` | `+welp`, `+zing`, `+snoc`, `+skid`, `+spin`, and `+sort` against the quicksort `qsort`, which permutes its list. |
| `Hoon/Axis.lean` | `+cap`, `+mas`, `+peg`, and what they mean for `slot?` (`slot_pegF`, `slot_cap_mas`). |
| `Hoon/Sets.lean` | `+put:in` and `+has:in`, with a set read as the map of its keys to `~`. |
| `Hoon/Rip.lean` | `+rip` on a bite of step at least 1: the blocks of an atom, least significant first (`ripF`). |
| `Hoon/Bits.lean` | `+bex`, `+lsh`, `+rsh`, `+end`, `+met`, `+cut`, `+cat`, `+can`, and `+mix`, `+dis`, `+con` as `^^^`, `&&&`, `|||`. |
| `Hoon/Muk.lean` | The arms `sit`, `sum`, `rol` of the door `+fe`, and `+muk` against the model `mukF`. |
| `Hoon/Mug.lean` | `mug_spec`: `+mug` computes `mugF` on every noun, which proves `MugSpec mugF`. |
| `Hoon/Layer1.lean`, `Hoon/Layer2.lean` | The pill's layers satisfy the predicates of their arms, and `+gor`, `+mor` and the map and set theorems hold on `layer2` with no assumption.  The only theorem files that import `Facts.lean` and the bulk. |
| `Compiler/Ca.lean`, `Compiler/So.lean` | The pill's Nock of the arms `hed`, `tel`, `con`, `all`, `int`, `uni`, `lack`, `app` of `+ca` and `hed`, `tel`, `jibe`, `knit`, `purr` of `+so` computes the models of `Ska/Sock.lean` on every cape and sock.  The arms that read the library core assume only what it gives at the named axes `ca.lib.axis` and `so.lib.axis`.  These are plain files, because the models are. |
| `Iter.lean` | `nockW call`, Nock whose opcodes `2` and `9` hand the formula they compute to `call`, and `iterN k`, Nock with calls at most `k` deep: `nock = nockW nock`, and `nock` returns `p` exactly when some `iterN k` does.  An induction on `k` meets a loop's calls to itself at `iterN k`. |
| `Hoon/Mink.lean` | The gates `frag` and `edit` of the `|^` core of `+mink` are `slot?` and `edit?` as units (`mink_frag_gate`, `mink_edit_gate`), with `+cap` and `+mas` from the context (`MinkIn`). |
| `Compiler/Mink.lean` | `+mink` against `minkF`: one symbolic run of its loop (`mink_loop_unfold`) gives `NStep`, the loop on nouns with its calls as parameters; `NStep` with `nock` is `minkStep` encoded (`nstep_exact`); `mink_gate_iff`: the gate returns `x` exactly when `Mink` returns a tone that `x` encodes, given `MinkIn` and `SlamIsNock lib`. |
| `Compiler/Monk.lean` | `+monk` and `+mack` from `+mink`: `+monk` drops the trace, `+mack` keeps the product of a `%0` (`monk_gate_iff`, `mack_gate_iff`). |
| `Compiler/MinkPill.lean` | The same in the pill's layer 4, where `MinkIn`, `MonkIn`, `MackIn` hold (`mink_pill_iff`, `mink_pill_call`, `monk_pill_iff`, `mack_pill_iff`). |
| `Compiler/Mook.lean` | `+mook` and `+mock` against `mookF` and `Mock`: the test of the tone, `+skip` (`mook_skip`), the `+turn` of `rend` over the trace, `rend` on a `%mean` of a cell through `+mack` and `(soft tank)` (`rend_mean`), and the branches of `rend` that format with constants, `+rip` and `+scow %p` of `+mug` (`rend_leaf`, `rend_hand`).  `mook_gate_iff`, `mock_gate_iff`. |
| `Compiler/MockPill.lean` | The same in layer 4 (`mock_pill_iff`).  What stays assumed of `Lib`, each a statement about the pill's Nock for one field: `(soft tank)` (`SoftTankIs`), the cord of `+skip` (`SkipMsgIs`), `+scow %p` (`HandIs`), and `rend` on `%hunk` of a cell and on `%spot` (`RendRestIs`); and, on `Lib` alone, the frames that `rend` formats with constants and `+rip` (`RendLeafIs`). |
| `Compiler/Pi.lean` | `cons`, `hed`, `tel`, `slot` (on an axis of at least `1`) and `compose` of the provenance core `+pi` compute B's models of `Ska/Pi.lean`; `hed`, `tel`, `slot` read `+peg` (and `+cap`, `+mas`) of layer 1 through the library core (`PegOf`, `AxisOf`). |
| `Compiler/PiEdit.lean` | `edit` and `prune` of `+pi`, `+distribute` and `+double-int` against B's models (`pi_edit_gate`, `pi_prune_gate`, `distribute_gate`, `double_int_gate'`), and on the pill's library core with no assumption (`*_pill`).  The upward loop of `edit` is the noun of the upward loop of `+darn:so` (`pi_edit_up`); `prune` computes `distribute` with its arguments swapped. |
| `Compiler/Inlineable.lean` | `+inlineable` computes `inlineable` of `Ska/FolLoop.lean` on every noun and in every context (`inlineable_gate'`); it reads nothing from its context. |
| `Compiler/Safe.lean` | `+safe-nomm` and `+safe-fol-fol` of the analysis core `ana` compute `safeNomm` and `safeFolFol` of `Ska/FolLoop.lean` on `Nomm.toNoun` of every nomm (`safe_nomm_gate`, `safe_fol_fol_gate`, and `*_pill` on `ana`).  `+safe-nomm` reads nothing from its context, `+safe-fol-fol` reads `+safe-nomm`. |
| `Compiler/Anno.lean` | `+knit-anno`, `+patch-prod`, `+msg-sock` and `+msg-anno` of `ana` against `knitAnno`, `patchProd`, `msgSock` (with its crash as `none`) and `msgAnno` of `Ska/CallGraph.lean`, on every sock and spring (`*_gate`), and on `ana` with no assumption (`*_pill`).  They reach `+so` and `+pi` through `lib` at `knit_anno.lib.axis` of `ana`. |
| `Compiler/CapeSize.lean`, `Compiler/GitG.lean`, `Compiler/Mi.lean` | Callees of `fol-loop` in `ana`: `+cape-size` counts the leaves of any noun (`cape_size_gate`, against `capeSize`, written here after the Hoon, and `leafCount`); `+git-g` is `Ana.gitG` on call graphs of `FinMap`, the bunt datum being the noun of `FDatum.bunt` (`git_g_gate`); `gut` and `put` of the core `+mi` are `Ana.gutMi` and `Ana.putMi` on memos of `FinMap` (`mi_gut_gate`, `mi_put_gate`). Each reads `+by` (or `+add`) of the library's layers at an axis of `ana` (`8191` for layer 2, `16383` for layer 1, through `lib` by `slot_spine`), and holds on `ana` with no assumption (`*_pill`). |
| `Compiler/GitMi.lean` | `+git:mi` against `Ana.gitMi`, crashes included (`mi_git_gate`, `mi_git_pill`): `gut`, then `+tap:by` of the library's second layer gives `Treap.tap` of the inner map (`tap_inner`), and the loop is `Ana.gitLoop` with `hitTest` (`mi_git_loop`), through `+huge:so`, `+app:ca` of the pill's library core at axis `7` of the context and `+patch-prod` at axis `22` (`MiGitIn`). |
| `Compiler/HeSock.lean` | `+he-sock` against `heSock`, written there after the Hoon, line by line (`he_sock_gate`, `he_sock_pill`): the gate never crashes on two socks, so the model is a `Bool`, and `Env.he` of `Ska/Analysis.lean` is instantiated with it (`heEnv`, with `he-threshold` `32`).  It reads `+lth` and `+add` of the library's first layer at axis `16383` of its context, `+cape-size` at `22495`, and `+hed:so`, `+tel:so` through the pill's library core at `7` (`HeSockIn`); the proof is by induction on the size of the two capes. |
| `Compiler/RecCall.lean` | `+recursive-call` against `recursiveCall heSock` of `Ska/CallGraph.lean` on call graphs of `FinMap` read through `gCore` (`recursive_call_gate`, and `recursive_call_pill` with the threshold `32` read at axis `510` of `ana`, `ana_threshold`), crashes of `+huge:so` and `+msg-sock` included.  The count is the `+roll` of layer 2 with the gate `recursive_call.cnt` (`rc_count`), and the loop is `recLoop` (`rc_loop`), through `+git-g`, `+he-sock` and `+msg-sock` of the context (`RcIn`). |

The statement of `al_start`, for phase 16: `theorem al_start {g : Opt.AGen (TreapMap mugF)} {GEN : Noun} (hg : GenRel g GEN) (hb : Opt.BrzInv g) (hn : Opt.RevNodup g.rev) (nargs : Nat) (BL REV T : Noun) (o : Nat) (preH : List Nat) (hd : preH.Nodup) (hm : ∀ x, x ∈ preH ↔ x ∈ g.rev o) {info : Opt.AInfo} {imms : Opt.AImms} (hi : (if g.rev o = [] then some (Opt.entryInfo nargs) else Opt.aliasStart g o (g.rev o)) = some info) (hj : Opt.aliasStartImms g (g.rev o) = some imms) (K : Noun) : ∃ GEN' INFO IMMS, GenRelL g info imms GEN' ∧ InfoMapRel INFO info ∧ ImmsRel IMMS imms ∧ nock (C (listNoun A preH) (alWS GEN (A nargs) BL REV (C (A o) T))) (alStartF K) = nock (C IMMS (C INFO (C (listNoun A preH) (alWS GEN' (A nargs) BL REV (C (A o) T))))) K` (`preH`, `hd`, `hm` as `al_pre` gives them; `hb`, `hn` from `aliasM_inv`; `hi`, `hj` as `aliasBlock` binds them). |

| `Compiler/OptLinkAlParsTrap.lean`, `Compiler/AuditAlPars.lean` | Phase 12 of `+alias`, the parameter trap: `alPTC` (its core), the gate of `ins` (`al_pain`, `UInfoRel` to `aliasIn`), the gate that drops an argument (`al_patl`), **the trap is `aliasPars`** (`al_part`, by induction on the parameters: `[(flop out) gen]`, `info-local` `InfoMapRel` to the model's facts); `GenRelL.of_aInfoEq`; **`al_pars_perm`** (phase 12 on the Hoon's `pre`) and **`al_pars`** (moved to `g.rev o` by `aliasPars_edges_perm`); the audit lists their axioms.  The statement of `al_pars`: `{g : Opt.AGen (TreapMap mugF)} {loc : Opt.AInfo} {imml : Opt.AImms} {GEN : Noun} (hg : GenRelL g loc imml GEN) (hr : Opt.RevNodup g.rev) (hn : Opt.NoLti g.new) (IMMS INF NA BL REV T : Noun) (o : Nat) (pre : List Nat) (hd : pre.Nodup) (hm : ∀ x, x ∈ pre ↔ x ∈ g.rev o) (b : Blob) (edges : List (Nat × List (Option Nat))) (loc' : Opt.AInfo) (he : Opt.aliasEdges g o (g.rev o) = some edges) (hp : Opt.aliasPars g b.par edges loc = some loc') : ∃ GEN', GenRelL g loc' imml GEN' ∧ nock (C b.toNoun (C IMMS (C INF (C (listNoun A pre) (alWS GEN NA BL REV (C (A o) T)))))) alPars = some (C (listNoun A b.par) GEN')` (the subject is the walk's after `=/  bob`; `pre`, `hd`, `hm` from `al_pre`; `al_pars_perm` is the same on any `pre` with `he` on `pre` and without `hr`, `hd`, `hm`). |
| `Compiler/OptLinkAlFin.lean` | Phase 14 of `+alias` (Gaps 13), the end of a block: the subject `alFS` (`fin.bob` at `59`, `gen` at `510`, the walk core `alWS` at `127`), the arm `rn` as a gate (`al_rn_arm`, `al_rn_gate`); the ends that only rename, each returning `[fin-new gen]` with `gen` unchanged (`alF_hop` by `lf_turn`, `alF_jmp`, `alF_jmf` by `mr_turn_ren`, `alF_jsp`, `alF_jsf`, `alF_don`, `alF_bom`); **`+del:ju` on a `JugRel` jug is the filter at the key** (`alF_jug_del`; `jugRel_congr`, `jugRel_delJu`: under `RevNodup`, `Opt.delJu`); `alF_got` (`+got:by` on `info-local`, an `InfoRel` noun); `lb_atomImm`, `lb_none` (what `InfoRel` fixes of `is-loob`); **`AlFinOK FC fin`**, the statement of an end on a `GenRelL` state with `RevNodup`; `alF_eqq`. |
| `Compiler/OptLinkAlFinClq.lean`, `Compiler/OptLinkAlFinBrn.lean`, `Compiler/OptLinkAlFinBrz.lean` | Phase 14 of `+alias`, the ends with a test: the Nock of each on any `gen` noun, the facts of the test register an `infoN` noun and the `+del:ju` given (`alF_clq_run`, `alF_brn_run`, `alF_brz_run`, by cases on `is-cell`, `is-loob`, `has-imm` and on `there.o = there.z`), then **`AlFinOK`** of each (`alF_clq`, `alF_brn`, `alF_brz`): the folded branch a `%hop` with the dropped edge deleted from `rev` unless both targets agree, `%brn` on a non-loobean immediate `[%bom ~]` with both edges deleted (the second `+del:ju` on the product of the first) and `folded` kept. |

| `Compiler/OptLinkAlBodyLoc.lean` | Phase 13 of `+alias`: `+got:by` and `+jab:by` on `info-local` (`loc_got`; `loc_jab`, a lens mapping the noun at the key to a related noun, through `jab_gate_v`, a copy of `jab_gate_at` of `CompSect.lean`, and `jab_arm_node`, the arm on a map that is a node, `loc_node`), `sawRel_single` (the set `[a ~ ~]`); the cases `opOK_cel`, `opOK_lob` (the test reads `is-loob ∨ loobImm`, which `InfoRel` fixes), `opOK_inc`, `opOK_hed`, `opOK_tal`, each for every `pick`. |
| `Compiler/OptLinkAlBodyImm.lean` | Phase 13 of `+alias`, the two cases that pick a member of a set, for `pick = Ana.pickR FinMap` (the root of the set's treap, `sawRel_root`): `iml_get`, `iml_put` (`+get:ju`, `+put:ju` on `imms-local`, `mem_insN`), `opOK_imm`; the lenses of `%con` (`alLensHed`, `alLensTel`, `al_lens_hed`, `al_lens_tel`: `+put:in` of the new register on `hed-of`, `tel-of`, read in the lens's context), `saw_insN`, `opOK_con`. |
| `Compiler/OptLinkAlBodyGate.lean` | Phase 13 of `+alias`, the gate and the roll: `alCase` (the case of the `?-` for each op), **`opOK_all`** (every case links for `Ana.pickR FinMap`), `al_bodyG_case` (the gate `alBodyG` sets `gen` to `gen-init` and runs `alCase op`), `al_bodyG`; `bodyStep`, `body_fold` (the `+roll` of `bodyStep`, which conses the kept op, is `aliasBody` reversed), `BodyAcc`; **`al_body`** (the phase `alBody` on a related state returns the noun of the model's ops and a related state; the roll starts from the bunt `[*pole ~ gen]`, `poleBunt`, `list_mb`, and ends with the `+flop`); **`al_body_gen`** (the same with `GenRelL` on the walk's subject `alWS`). |
| `Compiler/AuditAlBody.lean` | `#print axioms` of the main theorems of phase 13 of `+alias`. |
| `Compiler/OptLinkAlWFin.lean`, `Compiler/OptLinkAlWFinClq.lean`, `Compiler/OptLinkAlWFinBrn.lean`, `Compiler/OptLinkAlWFinBrz.lean`, `Compiler/OptLinkAlWFinAll.lean` | Phase 14 of `+alias` as the walk imports it: copies of `OptLinkAlFin*.lean` (the same declarations, `al_fin` included) whose first file imports `OptLinkAlBody.lean` and takes `alRnArm`, `alBat_rn` from it instead of declaring them again, so that phase 14 can be imported beside phases 9, 12 and 13 (`OptLinkAlStartRef.lean`, `OptLinkAlBodyGate.lean` import `OptLinkAlBody.lean`); no file imports both these and the originals.  Once `OptLinkAlFin.lean` imports `OptLinkAlBody.lean` and drops its two copies, these five files can go. |
| `Compiler/OptLinkAlWalk.lean` | Phase 15 of `+alias` (Gaps 13): the walk cut after the unreachable test (`alWalkC`, `alW1` to `alW6`, `alWalk_cut` by `rfl`); `store_put` (`+put:by` on a map of labels whose values are related by any `R`: `InfoStoreRel`, `ImmsStoreRel`); `alStored` (the model's state after the three stores); **`al_tail`** (on `gen` `GenRelL g loc imml`, `alTail` stores `[par body fin]`, `info-local`, `imms-local` at `o` and runs the walk on `t.topo` from `gen` `GenRel (alStored g loc imml o b)`). |
| `Compiler/OptLinkAlWalkHyp.lean` | The phases the turn of the walk of `+alias` takes from other files, as `Prop`s: `alStartW` (a copy of `alStartF`), `AlStartLink` (phases 9 and 10: the statement of `al_start`), `AlParsLink` (phase 12: `al_pars` with `NoLti` of `g.new`), `AlBodyLink` (phase 13: the statement of `al_body_gen` for `Ana.pickR FinMap`); `noLti_opt`, `noLti_of_opt` (`NoLti` is `Opt.NoLti`). |
| `Compiler/OptLinkAlWalkTurn.lean` | One turn of the walk of `+alias` (Gaps 13, phases 7 to 15 composed): `alW1_start` (the branch of a reached block is `alStartW [8 alBob alW4]`), `al_reach_comp` (the Nock from `bob` to `alTail` from the product of each phase), `al_walk_step` (the walk on `o :: t` is `alUnB` or `alW1` by the test `al_unt` on the Hoon's `pre`), `pre_nil_iff`; **`al_turn`** (on `gen` `GenRel g` with `RevNodup`, `BrzInv`, `NoLti` of `g.new`, where `aliasBlock (Ana.pickR FinMap)` returns `g1`, the walk on `o :: t` is the walk on `t` from `gen` `GenRel g1`; the unreachable block by `al_unreach`, a reached one by `al_pre`, `AlStartLink`, `al_bob`, `AlParsLink`, `AlBodyLink`, `al_fin`, `al_tail`). |
| `Compiler/OptLinkAlWalkBody.lean` | The three hypotheses of the turn, proved: `al_start_link` (`al_start`, with `alStartW_eq`), `al_pars_link` (`al_pars`), `al_body_link` (`al_body_gen`). |
| `Compiler/OptLinkAlWalkGate.lean`, `Compiler/AuditAlWalk.lean` | Phase 16 of `+alias`: **`al_walk`** (the walk `alWalk` on `topo` is `aliasWalk`, by induction on `topo` from `al_end` and `al_turn`, carrying `RevNodup`, `BrzInv`, `NoLti` by `revNodup_block`, `brzInv_block`, `noLti_block`), **`al_gate`** (the gate body `aliasBody` read from the pill is `aliasM (Ana.pickR FinMap)`: `aliasBody_eq`, `al_prelude`, `genRel_start`, `al_walk`), `al_link_of` (from the three hypotheses), **`al_link`: `AliasLink P` for every `P` with `P.alias = aliasM (Ana.pickR FinMap)`, no hypothesis left**, **`opt_gate_alias`** (`opt_gate_sw` with `al_link`: the pill's `+optimize` is `optimizeF` for those passes, given `P.trim = trimTraceHints` and `P.switch = switchChains sfuel`); the axiom audit of phases 15 and 16. |
| `Compiler/AuditOptLink.lean` | `#print axioms` of the main theorems of `Compiler/OptLink*.lean`; `OptLinkBase.lean` also has `+rep` forward (`rep_gate_fwd`) and against a relation (`rep_gate_rel`). |
| `Ska/Types.lean` | Models of the IR types (`Bell`, `Sock`, `Cape`, `Pole`, `Termin`, `Blob`, `Straight`, `Tone`, ...), each with its Hoon noun layout. |
| `Ska/Mink.lean`, `Ska/MinkTrace.lean` | Models of `+mink`, `+mook` and `+mock`; what Nock computes, `+mink` returns (`mink_of_evalH`, with `EvalH` the same as `Eval`: `EvalH.toEval`, `Eval.toEvalH`), also on a dynamic hint whose tag is a cell (`eval_cell_tag`); with no scry gate, what `+mink` returns as `[%0 p]` Nock computes (`evalH_of_minkF_nil`, `eval_of_minkF_nil`). |
| `Ska/Interp.lean`, `Ska/Steps.lean` | A model of the Hoon interpreter (`+eval-top`, `+eval-blob`, `+eval-pole`, `+eval-mook`), with the compiler as a parameter that answers pokes. The registers are a `Map (Option Noun)`: `some v` a value, `none` poison, the mark of a parameter that a hop passed `~`. The proofs use them through `read_eq_some`, `read_store_self`, `read_store_other`, `read_poison_self`, `read_poison_other`, `move_of_read` (a move of a value is a store of it) and `read_move`. |
| `Ska/Contract.lean`, `Ska/ContractExamples.lean` | The contract: what the code answered for a bell must do, given the same of its callees. It is met by a tail call and by an indirect `[2 b c]`. |
| `Ska/Theorems.lean`, `Ska/Statements.lean` | From the contract alone: `meek` (over `Eval`) and `meek_conv` (with no scry gate, `[%0 p]` from the interpreter is an `Eval` run to `p`), `weak`, `converse` for the models, on every reachable compiler state (`*_reach`); `meek_cell_tag`, `meek` on `[11 [[1 0] 1 0] 1 42]`. |
| `Ska/Sock.lean`, `Ska/SockFacts.lean`, `Ska/CapeFacts.lean` | Models of the `+ca` and `+so` cores, and what their operations do to the nouns a sock describes. |
| `Ska/NommSem.lean` | `nomm`, a semantics for it, and the invariant `Annot` the analysis must give it.  `Annot.n11c`: a dynamic hint whose tag is a cell has the nomm `[%11 [%$ h] f]` of the hint under `%$` (f7e95af). |
| `Ska/NommAgree.lean` | `nomm_agree`: a nomm satisfying `Annot`, with a code table satisfying `CodeOK`, returns exactly the tones `+mink` returns on the formula.  `minkF_ctag`: `+mink` runs a hint whose tag is a cell as the hint under `%$`, at every fuel and from every trace (`stopTag 0` is false). |
| `Ska/Pi.lean` | Models of the provenance core `+pi`, of `+distribute` and `+double-int`, and of `sock-anno`. |
| `Ska/FolLoop.lean`, `Ska/FolLoopFacts.lean` | A model of `fol-loop` for one function, with direct calls answered from a table, and of `+inlineable`, `+safe-nomm`, `+safe-fol-fol`.  The case `Shape.op11c` (f7e95af): on `[11 [[x y] h] f]` with `h` a cell the loop runs again on `[11 [0 h] f]`.  `annot_ctag`, `mink_ctag_iff`: what holds of the hint under `%$` holds of the hint with a cell tag. |
| `Ska/FolLoopSound.lean` | `folLoop_good`: given a sound table, the nomm of `fol-loop` satisfies `Annot` and its product sock holds of every product; `+safe-fol-fol` and `saf` give what the inline and memo cases assume. |
| `Ska/CallGraph.lean` | Models of the call graph's arms (`+patch-prod`, `+msg-anno`, `+git:mi`, `+recursive-call`), and what a converged graph gives. The fixed point itself is not proved: `WantSound` and the soundness of the table on products are hypotheses.  `GenLe` (generalization by forgetting) is defined here, for `Answers.hitGen`: a hit at a sock `s` that generalizes `s0`, patched for `s0`, which is what the `%rec` branch of `resolve` gives after `%he` steps.  `RunEntry`: the code requirement of an entry is `app:ca (uni:ca want w) more` for some cape `w` (the old entry's cape, `+run` since 1813f09); `lessOK_of_run`. |
| `Ska/Prov.lean`, `Ska/ProvFacts.lean` | The provenance invariant on products (`ProvR`, `ProvO`): a part with axis provenance is that axis of the function's subject. Nock 0, cons, Nock 10, the join of two branches and a direct call through `compose:pi` keep it; `+patch-prod` is sound under it. |
| `Ska/WantMono.lean` | `want` only grows along a run of `fol-loop`. |
| `Ska/AppPath.lean` | `app:ca` below a covered path; the static conditions `StatS` and `ShapeOK`; `known_formula`: on subjects of the code requirement, a fully known formula-formula is that formula. |
| `Ska/Stat.lean` | The static condition `StatOK` (the product sock generalizes what the subject sock knows at each axis provenance), kept by every sock operation of `fol-loop`. |
| `Ska/AppMask.lean` | `+app:ca` under a mask, and the subject half of a call site, `call_sub_fits`: on the code requirement, a call's subject fits the callee's requirement. |
| `Ska/FolLoopRel.lean` | `folLoop_rgood`: the soundness of `fol-loop` relative to the code requirement, every case; `folLoop_wantSound`, `folLoop_relProd`. |
| `Ska/Converge.lean` | `converged_agree`: the analysis output of every entry of a converged graph agrees with `+mink` on its code requirement. `ConvergeProd.lean` removes its assumption `ProdRel`.  `fits_app_uni`: a noun that fits `app:ca (uni:ca want w) s` fits `app:ca want s` on a well-formed `s` (from `app_mono_cape` and `uni_has`), which is how every consumer of `RunEntry` and its variants gets from the entry's code requirement, larger since 1813f09, to the subjects of `WantSound` and `folLoop_wantSound`; `entry_ok_of_run` (moved here from `CallGraph.lean`, with `WellFormed e.more`). |
| `Ska/MinkFuel.lean` | `+mink` taken apart with its fuel: a run with fuel `k+1` has its parts, and the callee of Nock 2, with fuel `k`. |
| `Ska/FolLoopProd.lean` | `folLoop_rprod`: the product half of `folLoop_rgood` for runs with fuel `K+1`, from a table right on callee runs with fuel `K`. |
| `Ska/ConvergeProd.lean` | `prodRel_of_converged`: `ProdRel` at convergence, by induction on the fuel. `agree_at_convergence` assuming `RunEntryM`: the stored product is the merge (`+msg-anno` or `+double-int`) of the last run's with some older one, which is what `+run` stores. `merge_no_fixed_point`: the two merges have no common fixed point.  The code requirement of `RunEntryM` is `app:ca (uni:ca want w) more` (1813f09), taken down to the run's `app:ca want more` by `fits_app_uni`. |
| `Ska/CompSim.lean` | The relation `+comp` must establish for nomm without calls and hints (`CompDone`, `CompRel` per block and goal), which gives `BellContract`; composition for `%7` and `%6`; the leaves `[1 k]`, `[0 1]`, `[0 0]` meet the contract. `+comp` itself is not modelled. |
| `Ska/WellFormedFacts.lean` | Every sock operation of the analysis keeps `WellFormed`; `folLoopTop_wf`: on a well-formed subject sock, products are well formed and the table is read only at well-formed subject socks. |
| `Ska/RunStep.lean` | A model of `+run` after `fol-loop` (`runDatum`, with `less-code` the run's `want` united with the old entry's cape, `darkOf old.less`, then masked on `bus`, as the Hoon since 1813f09); the writes of `+ska-callgraph` keep the graph well formed (`GStep.wf`); the poke's code table `codeOf`; `ConvergedGraph`. The loop that makes the writes (`resolve`, `+analyze`, the SCC iteration) is not modelled. |
| `Ska/ConvergeGraph.lean` | `agree_poke`: agreement with `+mink` from the final graph of a poke, each run with its own table, with well-formedness proved from the poke's sock. It assumes the graph converged, and it does not cover memo hits from earlier pokes.  Each use of an entry's code requirement goes through `fits_app_uni` (the requirement is `want` united with the old cape since 1813f09). |
| `Ska/CompSimOps.lean` | The block relation of `+comp` through cons (`%next` with a pair need), `[3 p]`, `[4 p]`, `[5 p q]`, from the blocks each case emits. |
| `Ska/Analysis.lean` | A state-passing model of `+ska-callgraph` (`fol-loop` with the whole `gen`, `resolve`, `+analyze`, `+run`, the pass and member loops, `+dirty`, `+sweep`, `+above`, `+cull`, `+pop`), `+prune-callgraph`, `+memo-final-entry` and the analysis part of `+ska-poke`, arm by arm and fuelled. Maps and sets are the parameter `Treap`; `+he-sock`, `+soft-spot` and `+register-fast` are the parameter `Env`. This is the model the pill is to be compared with; the fixed-table model of `FolLoop.lean` is a special case.  The `resolve` loop carries the identity it began with (`orig`, the call's site sock and formula) and asks `+git:mi` with the formula `orig.2` and the current identity's sock, also after a `%he` step, as the Hoon does since 1df76d0 (`(git:mi done.st.gen fol-new more.id-there)`); `resolveR` is the loop begun with the call's own identity, and the invariants of the loop (`ResPL`, `ResP2L`) hold on every current identity whose sock comes from `orig.1` by `%he` steps.  The `%rec` branch patches the product with `orig.1`, as the Hoon does, so `ResP2L` concludes with an answer at `orig` (`AnsW` at `orig.1`), given that the current sock comes from `orig.1` by `%he` steps (`HeChain` of `ConvInv.lean`, with `HeChain.lift` and `HeChain.genLe`); the `%rec` answer is `AnsW.hitGen`, and a memo hit, an answer at the current identity, is lifted to `orig` by `HeChain.lift`. |
| `Ska/FolRel.lean` | `FolR`: `fol-loop` with answers that change during a run, and its soundness (`folR_mono`, `folR_wf`, `folR_rgood`, `folR_rprod`). |
| `Ska/ConvergeRel.lean` | Agreement from the final graph with each last run in `FolR` (`ConvergedGraphR`, `GStepR`, `agree_pokeR`); the older statements as special cases; `RunEntryR.pop`.  `RunEntryR` has the code requirement `app:ca (uni:ca want w) more` (1813f09), and its consumers go through `fits_app_uni`. |
| `Ska/Bridge.lean` | The state-passing `fol-loop`, projected, is in `FolR` (`bridge_step`, `folLoop_goOK`, `run_folTopR`), under an invariant that `resolve` keeps, which is assumed; the write of `+run` is a `GStepR.run`. |
| `Ska/MemoPoke.lean` | Memo hits from earlier pokes: `MemoEntryOK`, the hit entry as a third kind (`ConvergedGraphM`, `agree_pokeM`), and the memo invariant kept by every poke (`MemoInv`, `memoInv_poke`).  The run entries' code requirements go through `fits_app_uni` (1813f09). |
| `Ska/CompModel.lean` | A model of `+comp`'s `run` for nomm without calls, hints and forks (`[^ *]`, `%0`, `%1`, `%3`, `%4`, `%5`, `%7`) and its helpers (`emit`, `from`, `split`, `copy`, `mede`, `collapse-lazy-atom`, `forl`); `HoonSet` abstracts the sets. |
| `Ska/CompSem.lean` | `RunOK`, the relation for one run of `run` against `execBlob` (goals with looks and jump arguments, the necessity of the looks). |
| `Ska/CompRun.lean`, `Ska/CompLook.lean`, `Ska/CompMede.lean`, `Ska/CompUnary.lean` | `%0`, `%1`, `%7`, `%4` (`%done`, `%pick`) and `%3` (`%pick`) meet `RunOK`; `+sure-require-look`; `+mede`. |
| `Ska/CompFrag.lean` | `run_ok`: the model of `run` meets `RunOK` on the fragment `Frag`, by induction, with no assumption about `+comp`. |
| `Ska/CompTop.lean` | A model of the end of `+comp` (`+need-ord-alloc-regs`, `+kern-need`, `+coerce-lazy` without forks, `+to-straight`'s renaming path with `+chase`) and of the final pass `finish`. `+shape-collapse` and `+bake-need` are parameters. |
| `Ska/CompStraight.lean`, `Ska/CompEntry.lean` | The renaming and chasing of `+to-straight` keep what the code does (`sim_block`), and entering the result does what the entry block did (`toStraight_entry`), for blocks whose jumps go down in some rank of the labels (`DownRk`, `toStraight_entryR`); `Down` is the identity rank. |
| `Ska/CompTopOK.lean` | `finish_bellContract`: on the fragment `Frag`, the final pass meets `BellContract` for every shape on which it does not crash, when nothing is baked and the bell's cape has no `[| |]`. Its hypotheses `RunDown` and `CoerceSpec` are discharged in `CompFinal.lean`. |
| `Ska/AnaInv.lean` | The construction invariant of the state-passing analysis (`SInv`, `Keep`). Every arm writes the graph by `GStepR` only and leaves the stack below it alone (`allP`, by induction on the fuel).  `ResPL orig`: what the `resolve` loop begun with `orig` keeps on every current identity (the memo lookup is at the current sock, so a hit is well formed by `gitMi_wf`). `skaCallgraph_steps`: the graph of `+ska-callgraph` is made by `GStepsR` from the empty graph. |
| `Ska/StaleWitness.lean` | A scheduling bug that this work found in `+analyze` and reproduced on the real compiler: the first pass at a root did not run a member left on the stack by a nested pass loop that exited early, and `+pop` could finalize it stale. Fixed in the Hoon by the cull before the first pass (fefb95d); the formulas are kept as a regression test. |
| `Ska/CompDown.lean` | `run_down`: every block the model of `run` makes jumps only to smaller labels, and the jump it returns has no parameters, for every nomm. |
| `Ska/CompCoerce.lean` | What the ops of `+kern-need` and of the sure loop do (`kernLoop_spec`, `sureLoop_spec`), and so what `+coerce-lazy` does (`coerceSpec`). |
| `Ska/CompFinal.lean` | `finish_bellContract'`: the final pass meets `BellContract` on the fragment `Frag` with no assumption about the model of `+comp`. It still assumes that nothing is baked and that the bell's cape has no `[| |]`. |
| `Ska/CompUnaryMore.lean`, `Ska/CompCollapse.lean` | `[3 p]` for `%done`; `+collapse-lazy-atom` (`collapse_ok`) and `[4 p]` for `%next`. |
| `Ska/CompForl.lean` | `+forl` (`forl_ok`) and `[3 p]` for `%next`. |
| `Ska/CompClean.lean` | `run_clean`: no need that `run` returns has a subterm `pair none none` when the goal's has none, so `cons-need` never drops a cell check. |
| `Ska/CompCopy.lean`, `Ska/CompBinary.lean` | `+copy-need-make-ops` (`copyNeed_spec`) and the block of `+copy`; `binary_ok`, the shape shared by `%5` and cons. |
| `Ska/CompN5.lean`, `Ska/CompSplit.lean`, `Ska/CompCons.lean` | `[5 p q]`, `+split` and cons, for every goal. With these the fragment `Frag` is every nomm built from cons, `%0`, `%1`, `%3`, `%4`, `%5` and `%7`, under every goal, and `finish_bellContract'` covers it. |
| `Ska/SockNorm.lean` | Normal capes (no `[| |]`; `NormCape`, `NormSock`) are kept by every cape and sock operation of the analysis. Not yet lifted to every bell of the graph. |
| `Ska/BridgeD.lean` | The bridge between the state-passing run and `FolR`, with answers judged at the end of the run. |
| `Ska/ConvInv.lean`, `Ska/ConvArms.lean`, `Ska/ConvPass.lean`, `Ska/ConvAna.lean` | Convergence of `+ska-callgraph`: the invariant `CInv` and every arm keeping it. `skaCallgraph_frozen`: at the end the stack is empty and every entry of the final graph was written by its last run with answers that are answers of the final entries, with no assumption on scheduling. `skaCallgraph_convergedM` gives `ConvergedGraphM`.  `CInv.clo`: under `MemoNoCallees` of the memo, the graph is closed under callees and finalized entries call only finalized identities (used by `GraphClose.lean`).  `ResP2L` (`ConvArms.lean`): what the `resolve` loop begun with `orig` keeps, on every current identity whose sock comes from `orig.1` by `%he` steps (`HeChain`); since 1df76d0 a memo hit is an answer at the current identity, lifted to `orig` by `HeChain.lift`. |
| `Ska/CompIntoLook.lean`, `Ska/CompInto.lean`, `Ska/CompN10.lean` | `+gep` against `+peg`; the looks of `+into-sure`; `into_ok`, what the `+into` block does, and `into_okK`, the same with the block keeping every register outside the write set of `into_wr` (the donor's and the recipient's needs included, which the keep clause of `into_ok` does not cover when they lie in the goal's need or were allocated by `+into`), from the strengthened keep clause of `IntoOK`; `[10 [a v] q]` meets `RunOK` for every goal, so the fragment includes `%10`. The model also has `+hinted` and `+past` now, with `past_pure`: on a pure nomm `+past` is the identity. |
| `Ska/FolNorm.lean` | `folR_norm`: `fol-loop` keeps normal products. With `CInv.norm` and `PokeInv.bellNorm`, every bell has a normal cape. |
| `Ska/PokeConv.lean` | The poke's table from the pruned graph: `+prune-callgraph` is closed under callees; the datums and the memo of `pokeMemo`. `skaPoke_sound`: when the model of the analysis part of `+ska-poke` returns, every bell of its datums agrees with `+mink` on every subject that fits it, and the invariant `PokeInv` (the memo and the cumulative code table) is kept for the next poke. It assumes only the `Treap` laws and a root sock that is well formed, normal and not `%void`, as `&+sub` is. Termination is "some fuel is enough". Baking is not covered. |
| `Ska/Bake.lean`, `Ska/Know.lean` | Models of `+uni-sock`, `+norm-sock`, the bake of a callee and `+know`. A subject fitting `less.c` and a site's sock fits the site's variant (`bakeOf_fits`, `rebake_fits`, `pick_fits`). A bake need not imply `less.c` (`uniSock_drops_less`). |
| `Ska/CompLazy.lean`, `Ska/CompLazyArms.lean` | `Lazy`, the full `need-lazy` with forks and bonds, and the arms that walk them (`+lazy-prune`, `+none-equivalent`, `+lazy-big`, `+soft-less`, `+past-fork`, `+walk-lazy`, `+from`, `+split`, `+into`, `+copy-lazy`), each equal to its fork-free model on a need without forks. |
| `Ska/CompLazyRun.lean`, `Ska/CompLeap.lean`, `Ska/CompSixNext.lean` | `runL`: the model of `run` over full lazy needs, with `%6` for every goal. For `%next` it is `sixNextL`: `+leap` and the case again, or `+kerf` and the case again, or the fork branch (`+fork`, `+sect`), or the simple one (`+fork-sure`, `+sect`), with `+pass`, `+whole`, `+need-cut`, `+lazy-leap`, `+insert-hop`, `+fork-need`. Nothing is proved yet about what the code of `%6` under `%next` does. Runs of the model found that `+comp` threads through a join a register to which an inner join passed `~` (see the doc of `sixNextL`). That is no bug: such a register is poisoned and only moved. The interpreter used to delete it, and 85 of 162 such formulas crashed it before `+optimize`; with the poisoned registers all 162 run right before and after `+optimize`. |
| `Ska/CompSixFuel.lean` | The fuel `3` of `sixNextLC`: `LeapTwice laz q r` (`+leap` of the rest that `+leap` returned is `~`; proved: `leap_twice`, and `leapTwice_noAx0` on branches with `Bake.NoAx0`; it had held on 726 formulas of `tools/hoon/leaptwice.py`), and under it any larger fuel gives the same (`sixNextLC_fuel`), so the model is the Hoon's unbounded recursion; on a goal with no fork and no bond any fuel from `1` is phase S (`sixNextLC_fork_free`). |
| `Ska/CompLeapTwice.lean` | **`leap_twice`**: `LeapTwice laz q r` holds for every lazy need when the parts of `passBoth q r` have nonzero product axes. The invariant of a round, `QuietL p s` (every block's part at `p` is behind a register, or `%none` with every look under `p` going to `by` as axis `1`), is exactly an empty `by` of `+lazy-leap p s` (`quiet_empty`, `empty_quiet`); a pass at `p` leaves the rest `QuietL p s` (`quiet_rest`), a pass elsewhere keeps it (`quiet_keep`, through `needCut_keep` and `needCut_open`), so after the loop the rest is quiet at every pass in any order (`leapLoop_quiet`) and the second loop is `~` (`leapLoop_none`). Pure model, generic in the set type. |
| `Ska/CompLeapTwiceCheck.lean` | The test of `LeapTwice` on the executable model run before the proof (a computable set type, random lazy needs with nested forks and bonds, pass lists from `passBoth` of 20 branches or random), kept as one `#guard` of 3000 cases. |
| `Ska/CompLeapTwiceRun.lean` | `leapTwice_noAx0` (`LeapTwice` from `Bake.NoAx0` of the branches, by `pbt_gate`), and the theorems that took `LeapTwice` restated without it: `sixNextLC_fuel_tw`, `comp_run_n6_l_tw`, `res_n6_l_tw`, `comp_run_n6_l_nf_tw`, `comp_run_n6_l_k_tw`. |
| `Ska/CompLazyAgree.lean`, `Ska/CompLazyFinal.lean` | `runL_toL`: on a pure nomm without `%6`, `runL` is `run`. `+coerce-lazy` in full; `finishL_bellContract`: the earlier contract theorem carried over to the model with forks. Nothing is proved yet about `%6` itself. |
| `Ska/CompSix.lean` | `[6 c a b]` under `%done`, with a test and branches that have no `%6` of their own: the final pass meets the contract (`finishL_bellContract6`), and the compiled code crashes exactly when the nomm does (`six_crash_iff`), so it never crashes on a part of the subject that only the branch not taken reads. A nested `%6`, a `%6` as a test, and a `%6` under a `%next` goal are not proved. |
| `Ska/CompStraightMap.lean`, `Ska/CompCond.lean`, `Ska/CompFinishF.lean` | `toStraightF`, the whole of `+to-straight` with the proxy test and the map path; `finishLF`, the final pass with it. On the proved fragment no proxy register is made (`runL_cond_frag6`), so the earlier contract theorems carry over (`finishLF_bellContract6`, `six_crash_iffF`). What the code does when `+lazy-shut` makes proxies is not proved; the pieces for it are in `CompUnproxy.lean`. |
| `Ska/CompSixNextCond.lean` | `[6 c a b]` under a fork-free `%next` makes no proxy register (`sixNextSimple_cond`), given branches and a test that make none and few lazy blocks: `+sect` then threads nothing (`keys_difM_self`, `sect_apart`, `sect_cond`), and `+fork-sure` and `+copy-lazy` never write `cond` (`kc_forkSure`, `kc_copyL`). |
| `Ska/CompCopyFresh.lean` | `+copy-lazy` of lazy needs whose block labels are apart merges nothing (`copyLazyL_apart`). |
| `Ska/CompForkNeed.lean` | `+fork-need`: two copies of the need from distinct fresh registers (`NCopy`, `forkNeedGo_spec`), the fork blocks and the join (`forkSure_spec`); entering a fork block with the copy held enters the goal's block with the need held and no other register changed, since every argument is `some r` (`evalPassLoop_copy`, `goJ_fork`); the threading of `+sect` puts every block back when the branches made no proxy (`sectThread_same`). |
| `Ska/CompSixNextRun.lean`, `Ska/CompSixNextOK.lean` | `RunOKN`: the relation for a run for a fork-free goal `%next` whose result has lazy blocks, met by `Frag` (`runOKN_frag`). `sixNext_okN`: `[6 c a b]` under a fork-free `%next` meets it from `RunOKP` of the test and `RunOKN` of the branches; `runN_ok` on `FragN` (`%6` nested in branches and tests to any depth under fork-free `%next` goals, at most 255 `%6`). Both relations grow the state by `GrowU`, from a start with `UpBelow` (`growU_forkSure`: the fork blocks hop up to the join). |
| `Ska/CompGrowU.lean` | `GrowU`: `Grow` in which a new block may hop up, to a label whose block does not hop up, and every jump down to a block that hops up to `t` comes from a label above `t` (`JumpsU`). `UpBelow g`: every block that hops up hops below `g.bo`; `GrowU` keeps it (`GrowU.up`), and from it every `Grow` is a `GrowU` (`Grow.toU`). `downRk_of_growU`, `downRk_gen0`: the blocks of a state grown from `gen0` go down in the rank `upRk` (`3 t + 1` at a block that hops up to `t`, `3 k` at any other label `k`), which `toStraight_entryR` takes. `Fresh` now also says the jump passes no arguments, so a run for `%done` returns such a jump. |
| `Ska/CompSevenN.lean` | Parents of `%6` under `%next`: `runOKP_n7`, `[7 p q]` for `%done` with `q` in `Frag` and `p` in `FragN`, `runOKP_n4` and `runOKP_n3`, `[4 p]` and `[3 p]` for `%done` with `p` in `FragN` (through `unaryP_ok`). Each takes `NextOK p`, the run lemma for fork-free `%next` goals whose jump has no arguments, which `runN_ok` gives on `FragN`. `FragT`: `FragP` for `%done` and these three. `finishLF_bellContractT` and `crash_iffT`: the top theorems on `FragT` with at most 255 `%6`, from `finishLF_compDoneR` (`CompNestFinalP.lean`), which takes any run that meets `RunOKP`. For example `[7 [6 [0 2] [6 [0 3] [1 1] [1 2]] [0 3]] [4 [0 1]]]`. `CompFragM.lean` supersedes it. |
| `Ska/CompFragM.lean` | `FragM`: `Frag`, `[6 c a b]`, `[4 p]`, `[3 p]` and `[7 p q]` (with `q` in `Frag`) nested in any order, in the tests of `%6` too, so every `%next` goal a `%6` meets is fork-free. `runM_ok`: on it, every run for `%done` and for a `%pick` whose jumps have no arguments meets `RunOKP` (`OKP`), and every run for a fork-free `%next` whose jump has no arguments meets `RunOKN` (`NextOK`), by induction, with the parent cases `runOKN_n4`, `runOKN_n3`, `runOKN_n7` (through `unaryN_ok`, after `+collapse-lazy-atom` and `+forl`), `runOKP_n4p`, `runOKP_n3p`, `runOKP_n7g` (through `unaryG_ok`), and `sixP_ok` (`CompNestPick.lean`, the `%6` case of `runP_ok` with the runs of the test and branches as hypotheses) and `sixNext_okN` for `%6`. `finishLF_bellContractM`, `crash_iffM`: the top theorems on `FragM` with at most 255 `%6`. `fragM_of_fragT`: `FragM` contains `FragT` (`frag_any`: `Frag` does not depend on the goal). For example `[4 [6 [0 2] [3 [6 [0 6] [1 1] [0 7]]] [4 [0 3]]]]` and `[6 [4 [6 [0 2] [1 0] [1 1]]] [0 2] [0 3]]` are in `FragM`; `[7 [6 [0 2] [1 1] [1 2]] [6 [0 2] [0 3] [0 1]]]` (the first `%6` runs for the forked need of the second) and `[[6 [0 2] [0 3] [0 1]] [0 1]]` (cons) are not. Cons over `FragM` is in `CompFragX.lean`; not yet: `[5 p q]` and `[10 [a v] q]` over `FragM`, and goals with entries. |
| `Ska/CompBinaryN.lean` | `binaryN_ok`: the shape of cons and `%5` when both operands run for fork-free `%next` goals and meet `RunOKN`, so their needs have lazy blocks.  The lazy blocks of `p` were allocated after those of `q`, so `+copy-lazy` merges only the sure needs (`copyL_apart_some`, from `copyLazyL_apart`), the entries of the result are those of the two (`entries_side_perm`), and the `AgreeP` of the whole gives that of each part (`AgreeP.part`): the fill ops of `q`'s lazy blocks read the input registers, which the code of `p` keeps.  `RunOKG`, the relation for any goal, is `RunOKN` for `%next` (`RunOKG.toN`) and gives `RunOKP` for `%done` and `%pick` (`RunOKG.toP`).  The sure needs of the operands must be clean, as for `binary_ok`. |
| `Ska/CompConsN.lean` | Cons over operands with `%6`: `runOKN_cons` (fork-free `%next`, after `+split`), `runOKP_consD` (`%done`, through `%next` of a register a `%don` block returns, with `unaryG_ok`), `runOKP_consP` (`%pick`: a cell is no loobean, the block is a `%bom`), from `NextOK` and `CleanN` of the operands. |
| `Ska/CompFragX.lean` | `FragX`: `FragM` with cons `[p q]`, `[5 p q]` and `[10 [a v] q]` of two nomms of `FragX`, and `[7 [0 axe] q]` with `2 ≤ axe` and `q` in `FragX`, in any order and in the tests of `%6`.  `runX_clean`: on `FragX` every run for a goal with a clean need returns a clean sure need (`CleanA`, through `clean_copyL`, `clean_lazyShut`, `clean_sixTail`, `sect_sure`, `clean_sixBody`, `clean_consBody`, `clean_n10Body`, `clean_fromSure`).  `runX_ok`: the run relations on `FragX`; `finishLF_bellContractX`, `crash_iffX`: the top theorems on `FragX` with at most 255 `%6`; `fragX_of_fragM`.  For example `[[6 [0 2] [0 3] [0 1]] [0 1]]`, `[5 [6 [0 2] [1 0] [1 1]] [0 3]]`, `[10 [2 [6 [0 6] [0 7] [1 0]]] [0 1]]`, `[7 [0 6] [6 [0 2] [0 3] [0 1]]]` and `[6 [[0 2] [6 [0 2] [1 0] [1 1]]] [0 2] [0 3]]` are in `FragX`; `[7 [6 [0 2] [1 1] [1 2]] [6 [0 2] [0 3] [0 1]]]` (a `%6` run for a need with forks) and `[7 [4 [0 3]] [6 [5 [0 1] [1 6]] [0 1] [1 5]]]` (a `%4` run for a need with forks, which reads its register in the lazy blocks through a proxy) are not. |
| `Ska/CompN5N.lean` | `[5 p q]` over operands with `%6`: `n5Pre`, the block that compares the two registers (the same computation in `run` and in `runL` on a fork-free goal, `runL_n5_toL`); `n5Pre_ok`, the block part of `run_n5_ok` with `Grow` and its label in `[1, bo)`; `kc_n5Pre`; `runOKG_n5`: `RunOKG` for every fork-free goal from `NextOK` and `CleanN` of the operands, through `binaryN_ok`. |
| `Ska/CompN10N.lean` | `[10 [a v] q]` over operands with `%6`: `n10Body` (the case after `+simple-next`, `runL_n10_body`), `n10N_body`, and `runOKN_n10`, `runOKP_n10D`, `runOKP_n10P` for `%next`, `%done` (a `%don` block) and `%pick` (a `%brn` block), through `binaryN_ok` with `into_ok`: the donor `v` is the operand compiled first, the recipient `q` the second. |
| `Ska/CompFromRun.lean` | `[7 [0 axe] q]` with `q` in `FragX`: the `%0` runs for the need of `q`, which has entries, and is `+from` alone (`runL_n0_next`, `runL_seven_from`).  It puts no op anywhere, and the need it returns has the goal's entries at the same labels, each moved under `axe` (`fromL_ent`, with `FromEnt`), so the relation of `q` carries over (`runOKG_from`): the fill ops of an entry fill its moved need for the subject, which is its own need for the noun at `axe` (`fillSem_from`, `fromSure_fits`, `fromSure_dist`).  `RunOKP.toG`, `RunOKN.toG`. |
| `Ska/CompEntryGoal.lean` | `FrontSemR K` and `FrontK K`: `FrontSem` and the front clause of `RunOKE` with any hypothesis `K` on the input registers (`FrontSemR.strict`: the strict `FrontSem` is the case `lo ≤ r`).  (a) `RunOKE`, the relation for a run for a goal `%next laz th` whose need has entries.  The arms that meet such a goal keep the goal's labels (`+from` moves each entry's need, `+split` and `+into` split it and put `%con` ops in front, `+mede` and the collapsing arms consume it), so `RunOKE` pairs each entry `(o, L)` of the goal with the ops `F` the run put in front of block `o` and with the result's entry at `o`, if any: that entry's need, filled and followed by `F`, fills `L` for the product (`FrontSem`); with none, `F` alone fills `L`.  Other entries of the result are new, as in `RunOKG`; blocks below the start other than the goal's entries are kept.  It replaces `EntryFill`/`GoalSemE`, which put every entry at a new label.  (b) `ProxyHeld`: the register half of the threading invariant of `+sect`; the guard half is `Guarded` of `ExecPath.lean`. |
| `Ska/CompEntryRun.lean` | `runOKE_n0`: `[0 axe]` (`2 ≤ axe`) meets `RunOKE` (`+from`, `F = []`).  `runOKG_sevenE`: `[7 p q]` meets `RunOKG` from `RunOKG` of `q` and `RunOKE` of `p` for the goal `q`'s run returns, when `p` puts no op at the goal entries that stay in its result (`nf`) and the goal's registers precede the run.  `runOKG_from'`: `runOKG_from` again through the two. |
| `Ska/CompWalk.lean` | `walkLazy_ok`: `+walk-lazy` with a gate that puts ops at the one block it is given (`GateOK`) puts ops at the block of the need and of every entry, each meeting the gate's property, and changes nothing else (`WalkOK`), given distinct labels. |
| `Ska/CompMedeE.lean` | `runOKE_n1`: `[1 k]` for a goal with entries meets `RunOKE` (the first part of `runOKE_n1K`, which also gives the front `FrontK K` for every `K` that keeps the input registers out of the registers the walk allocates, and the `sem` for every keep set outside the goal's sure need).  `+mede` writes `k` into the sure need's registers at its block and into every entry's at the entry's block (`medeGate_ok`, from `medeLoop_spec` and `+sure-require-look`); the result has no entry, and the ops at each goal entry fill it alone. |
| `Ska/CompSevenOne.lean` | `runOKP_seven1`: `[7 [1 k] q]` for `%done` from the relation of `q`; `finishLF_bellContract71`, `crash_iff71`: the top theorems for `[7 [1 k] q]` with `q` in `FragX` and at most 255 `%6`, for example `[7 [1 [0 7]] [6 [0 2] [0 3] [0 1]]]`.  Not a constructor of `FragX` yet, so not nested. |
| `Ska/ExecPath.lean` | Executions of `execBlob` as paths: configurations `Cfg` (label, what is left of the block, arguments not yet passed, registers, trace, state), `Step` (pass the arguments, one op that returns `%0`, or the jump of the terminator, with the edge `(from, to)` of the labels), `Steps` with the edges in order, and `endOf`, what a configuration returns when its next action ends the function.  `run_iff_path`, `execEntry_iff_path`: a run returns `r` exactly when a path reaches an end that is `r`; `step_run`, `steps_run`, `step_det`, `steps_det` (of two paths from one configuration one extends the other), `step_hop`, `step_brn0`, `step_brn1`, `step_brz`.  Real reads (`poleReads`, `finReads`, `ReadsAt`; a `%mov` and a hop argument are none) crash on poison (`run_poison`), so every real read on a path of a successful run reads a value (`steps_reads`).  The guard half of the threading invariant, `Guarded` (every path from the entry to a real read of `k` passes the edge `e`), with `HeldAfter` (past `e` the read finds a value) gives `guarded_reads`.  The header reads the raw blocks of `[7 [6 [0 2] [1 0] [6 [0 6] [0 7] [0 1]]] [6 [0 2] [0 3] [0 1]]]` (`tools/hoon/proxy-dump.hoon`): the guard of the poisoned join parameter is a `%brz` on the outer test register in the lazy blocks, so it holds of executions and not of the graph. |
| `Ska/CompEntryRel.lean` | The relaxed `sem` for goals with entries (README Gaps 12.2).  `FrontER`: the front clause of `RunOKE` with `InR` of the run as the hypothesis on the input registers (`FrontK` and `FrontSemR` of `CompEntryGoal.lean`).  `frontER_n0` and `runOKE_n1R`: `[0 axe]` and `[1 k]` meet it, `[1 k]` also `SemNR`.  `semGR_sevenE`: `[7 p q]` with `p` for the goal that `q`'s run returns, from `SemGR` of `q` and the relaxed front and `sem` of `p`.  `semPR_seven1`: `[7 [1 k] q]` for `%done` with `q` in `FragX`.  `SemPR.toG`: the relaxed `sem` of `%done` and `%pick` as `SemGR`. |
| `Ska/CompWalkU.lean` | `+walk-lazy` with a gate that may make proxies.  `WalkOKC C`: `WalkOK` with the relation `C` between the states in place of an unchanged `cond`, and `walkLazy_okC`, the walk for any reflexive and transitive `C`.  `ProxyFor r`: `cond` grew only by fresh proxies for `r` (`proxy_proxyFor`), and `walkLazy_okP`, the walk at it, for the gate of `+collapse-lazy-atom`. |
| `Ska/CompCollapseGate.lean` | The gate of `+collapse-lazy-atom` (`collapseGate r o`).
