| `Ska/CompUnproxy.lean` | The proxies.  `unproxyBlob cond`: a block with each proxy register read as its definition (`renBlob` of `unproxy`, labels kept, no chase); with no proxy it is the identity (`unproxyBlob_empty`).  `CondFresh g0 g1`: `cond` changed only at registers allocated in between, which `+proxy` meets (`proxy_condFresh`: the proxy is `g.re`, unproxied it reads as its definition) and which composes (`CondFresh.trans`); below `g0.re` unproxying does not change (`unproxy_condFresh`); `CondBelow`: the keys of `cond` are allocated registers.  `AgreeU cond`: `AgreeP` with the blocks outside the lazy blocks compared after `unproxyBlob cond`, the hop of a lazy block renamed too; with an empty `cond` it is `AgreeP` (`agreeU_empty`).  For the top: `+chase` on the renamed blocks is `+chase` followed by the renaming (`chase_ren`, under `RankDown`, the rank condition of `DownRk` without the bound on labels, which holds of the blocks of `+to-straight` with the start at `0`, `rankDown_start`), so `chaseFin_ren`, `keepBlock_ren`, and `renameBlocks_ren`: the renaming path by `ren ∘ unproxy cond`, which is what `toStraightF_run` gives with proxies, holds at each label the block that the renaming path by `ren` makes of the unproxied blocks, which is `Sim.kept` for the program of the unproxied blocks. |
| `Ska/CompUnproxyEntry.lean` | The entry of `+to-straight` with proxies.  `toStraightF_entryU`: on a run of inputs, with or without proxy registers, if `toStraightF cond blocks ned o` returns `c'` and the blocks go down in a rank, entering `c'` on the arguments `split` takes out of `s` does what the unproxied start block `unproxyBlob cond start` does in a program `c` that holds the Gen blocks unproxied outside `o`, from registers that hold `s` as `ned` asks.  The simulation `Sim` is taken with the map of the unproxied blocks (`HoonMap.put (HoonMap.del c.blocks o) 0 (unproxyBlob cond start)`) and the injective `renReg`, `Sim.kept` from `renameBlocks_ren` with the rank of `rankDown_start`.  `proxRen_eq`: the renaming of the map path is `ren ∘ unproxy cond` in both cases (`hasProxies_none`: no proxy means an empty `cond`).  `FillSem.pair`: a fill from `ned` is a fill from `pair ned sur` holding `[s0 v]` when the registers of `sur` lie below the keep bound `lo`, so the input need of a run can be widened by registers of the run itself. |
| `Ska/CompUnproxyRegs.lean` | The registers that ops mention (`poleRegs`, `RegsIn`).  Unproxying leaves ops alone whose registers are no keys of `cond` (`unproxy_ops`, from `renPole_fix`).  The ops of `+kern-need` mention only the source register, the registers of the target need and registers allocated in the loop (`kernLoop_regs`, `kernBoth_regs`).  The header records a check on the real compiler (`tools/hoon/selfkeep.py`): over 2930 runs of `run` (352 of which make a proxy), no register of a need of a run's result is a key of `cond`, the ops a run emits write no register of its result's sure need nor of its lazy entries' needs, and the sure need and the entries' needs share no register. |
| `Ska/CompUnproxyFill.lean` | The registers the fill ops of `+coerce-lazy` mention.  `sureLoop_regs`: the ops of `sure-loop` from `ned` into `tgt` mention only registers of `ned`, of `tgt` and registers at or above the start's `re`.  `Front.uniq`: the ops in front of a block that exists are unique.  `coerceLazy_regs`: the ops of one coercion mention only registers of the input need, of the lazy need taken there and fresh registers (the target of `+sure-require-look` is made of the lazy need's registers and fresh ones).  `coerceList_regs`, `coerceLazyL_regs`: the same at the block of the `next` and at every entry of a lazy tree with distinct labels (`FillRegs`). |
| `Ska/CompUnproxyTop.lean` | The final pass for runs that make proxies.  `RunOKU`: `RunOKP` with `CondFresh g0 g1` in place of `cnd`, `AgreeU` by the run's final `cond` in `sem`, and no register of the sure need or of an entry's need a key of that `cond` (`ncond`, `econd`).  `RunOKP.toU`: a run without proxies from an empty `cond` meets it.  `mapVals`, `get_mapVals`: a map with every value mapped, over the keys.  `finishLF_compDoneU`, `finishLF_bellContractU`: the final pass with the whole of `+to-straight` (`toStraightF`) meets the relation at the entry and the contract, for any nomm whose run for `%done` from `gen0` meets `RunOKU`; the program it is compared with is the Gen blocks unproxied, and the fill ops are unchanged by the unproxying (`CondBelow`, `ncond`, `econd`, `coerceLazyL_regs`, `unproxy_ops`).  `finishLF_bellContractR_U`, `finishLF_bellContractXU`, `finishLF_bellContract71U`: the earlier top theorems through `RunOKU`. |
| `Ska/CompUnproxyBelow.lean` | Fact (i5) and its use.  `JmpIn`, `FinIn`, `BlobIn`: every register of a jump, terminator or block satisfies `P`, so a renaming that agrees there renames the block alike (`renBlob_congr`).  `BelowIn lo g`: every block of `g` at a label in `[lo, g.bo)` mentions only registers below `g.re`; fact (i5) of a run from `g0` to `g1` is `BelowIn g0.bo g1`.  `agreeU_transfer`: on such blocks `AgreeU` by one `cond` is `AgreeU` by any `cond` that agrees with it below `g1.re`.  `RunOKU.semW`, `RunOKU.semF`: the `sem` of `RunOKU` at a later `cond` (one that agrees below `g1.re`, or the `cond` of a later state reached by `CondFresh`), given (i5).  The steps for the induction that is to prove (i5): `belowIn_start`, `BelowIn.mono`, `belowIn_re`, `belowIn_emitted`, `belowIn_emit` (when the run does not drop), `belowIn_addOps`, `belowIn_proxy`, `BelowIn.ext` (a sub-run that keeps the old blocks), `BelowIn.front` (ops put in front of one old block). |
| `Ska/CompBelowRun.lean` | Fact (i5) for every run of `run`, by induction on the nomm as `run_clean`.  The invariant is stronger than (i5): `Keeps g g'` says that every `BelowIn lo` of `g` holds of `g'` (for every `lo`), with `re` and `bo` grown and `drop` kept, so a sub-run composes with what comes before it whatever old blocks it changes.  `NB n ned`, `GoalIn n goal`, `NextIn n nex`: the registers of a need, of a goal (its need and the arguments of its jumps), of a `next` are below `n`.  The steps `keeps_re`, `keeps_emit`, `keeps_addOps`, and the arms `keeps_must`, `keeps_splitNeed`, `copyNeed_regs`, `keeps_copyNeed`, `keeps_sureRequireLook`, `keeps_crashNeed`, `keeps_medeLoop`, `keeps_mede`, `keeps_forl`, `keeps_doneBlocks`, `keeps_simpleNext`, `keeps_bomb`, `keeps_intoLoop`, `keeps_split`, `keeps_copy`, `keeps_into`, `keeps_collapseLazyAtom`.  `run_keeps`: every run of `run` from a state that does not drop, for a goal whose registers are below `re`, keeps every `BelowIn lo` and returns a `next` whose registers are below its end (every case: `%0`, `%1`, cons, `%3`, `%4`, `%5`, `%7`, `%10`); `run_below`: fact (i5) of the run. |
| `Ska/CompBelowX.lean` | Fact (i5) on `FragX`.  `KeepX nm`: every run of `runL nm` for a fork-free goal whose registers are below `re`, from a state that does not drop, when `nm` has at most 255 `%6`, meets `KeepR`: `Keeps`, the registers of the result (sure need, the needs of its lazy entries, `LazyIn`, and the jump's arguments, `NextInL`) below its end, `cond` kept, at most `2 * sixCount nm` entries, at labels allocated in the run.  The cases `keepX_frag` (through `run_keeps`), `keepX_sixP` (`%done`, `%pick`, through `sixBody_apart`), `keepX_sixN` (a fork-free `%next`, through `sixNextSimple_apart`, `forkSure_spec` and `sect_spec`: the branches keep `cond`, so `+sect` changes no block), `keepX_four`, `keepX_three`, `keepX_seven`, `keepX_cons`, `keepX_five`, `keepX_ten` (the copy through `copyL_apart_some`, `keep_copyL`), `keepX_sevenFrom`; `runX_keeps`, the induction; `runX_below`: (i5) on `FragX`; `runX_okU`: the top run for `%done` from `gen0` meets `RunOKU` and (i5), so `RunOKU.semW` and `RunOKU.semF` apply to it.  The 255 bound keeps every fork small, so `+lazy-shut` makes no proxy. |
| `Ska/CompFootRun.lean` | Fact (i) for `run`, the write footprint.  `poleWr` (the destination of an op), `OpsWr Q A`, `BlobWr Q b` (the parameters and destinations of a block satisfy `Q`), `WroteIn Q g g'` (every block of `g'` is the block of `g` with ops in front that write only `Q`, or a block that writes only `Q`), with `WroteIn.refl`, `.trans`, `.mono`, `wroteIn_re`, `wroteIn_emit`, `wroteIn_addOps`.  The footprints of the arms: `wr_splitNeed` (the need split), `wr_crashNeed`, `wr_medeLoop`, `wr_mede`, `wr_forl`, `wr_doneBlocks` (the goal's need and fresh registers, `NewOr`), `wr_simpleNext`, `wr_bomb` (nothing), `wr_copyNeed` (registers of the two needs and none of the copy, for distinct needs apart and below `re`, through `wr_two`), `wr_collapseLazyAtom` (the goal's need and fresh registers, and the register it returns).  `Foot g g' goal nex`: the run writes only registers below its end that it allocated or its goal needs and none of its result's sure need (`wr`), the sure need lies in those registers (`sure`) and is distinct (`dist`).  `FootIH`: every run from a state that does not drop, for a goal whose registers are below `re` and whose need is distinct (`GDist`), meets `Foot`.  Done: `foot_n0`, `foot_n1`, `foot_n7`, `foot_n4`, `foot_n3` (through `foot_tail`); `run_foot_of`: the induction, with cons, `%5` and `%10` as hypotheses (cons and `%5` are now `foot_cons` and `foot_n5` of `CompFootCons.lean`). |
| `Ska/CompFrame.lean` | The semantic frame lemma on the paths of `ExecPath.lean`.  `stepWr x`: the registers the next step writes (the block's parameters when it passes arguments, the destination `poleWr` of the op it runs, nothing for a jump).  `evalPass_frame`, `execPole_frame` (with `localPole_frame`): `+eval-pass` changes only the parameters, an op only its destination.  `step_frame`: a step leaves `HoonMap.get` unchanged outside `stepWr x`, so a value stays that value and poison stays poison.  `steps_frame`: when every block at a label of `L` writes only registers of `Q` (`BlobWr Q`) and takes only jumps into `L` (`ProgIn`, `TakenIn`), and so does what is left of the block of `x` (`CfgIn`), every path from `x` keeps the registers outside `Q` and enters only labels of `L`; `steps_frame_all` with every label.  `run_frame`, `execEntry_frame`: the same for the path of a run that returns (`run_iff_path`, `execEntry_iff_path`); `steps_reads_frame`: on such a path a real read of a register outside `Q` reads the value it had at the start. |
| `Ska/CompFootCons.lean` | Fact (i) for cons and `%5`.  `copyNeed_dist`: the copy of two distinct needs apart and below `re` is distinct, with no `Clean`.  `wr_splitNeedS`, `split_foot`: `+split` writes only registers of the goal's need that are in neither half, and the halves are distinct, apart, and made of the goal's registers and fresh ones.  `foot_copyTail`: the run of `q`, the run of `p` and the `+copy` of the two results, from the footprints of the two runs (the code of `p` writes no register of `q`'s result).  `foot_cons`, `foot_n5`: the cases of `run_foot_of`, for every goal.  `%10` is in `CompFootTen.lean`. |
| `Ska/CompFootTen.lean` | Fact (i) for `[10 [a v] q]`, so for every run of `run`.  `intoRec_wr`: the `%con` ops of the walk of `+into-need` write registers of the need being taken apart or registers allocated in the walk, and none of the donor's or the recipient's need (the op of a step writes the top register of a `%this` or `%both`, which is in neither half, and the deeper ops write the fresh registers the step made for the half on the path); `intoNeed_wr`, `into_wr`: the same through the loop's one `+add-ops` and the block `+into` emits.  `foot_n10`: `+simple-next`, `+into`, then `foot_copyTail` with the donor `v` run first.  `run_foot`: `FootIH` of every nomm (`run_foot_of` with `foot_cons`, `foot_n5`, `foot_n10`). |
| `Ska/CompFootX.lean` | Fact (i) over `FragX`, with the lazy entries.  `FootR g0 g1 goal nex`: the code writes only registers below the end that the run allocated or the goal needs, none of the result's sure need and none of its entries' needs (`wr`, facts (i) and (iii)); the sure need and every entry's need lie in those registers (`sure`, `esure`) and are distinct (`dist`, `edist`); the sure need shares no register with an entry's need (`apart`, fact (iv)).  `FootX nm`: every run of `runL nm` for a fork-free goal whose registers are below `re` and whose need is distinct, from a state that does not drop, with at most 255 `%6`, meets it.  `FootR.pre`: code in front of a run that writes no register of the run's goal or allocations, with a wider goal.  The cases, mirroring `CompBelowX.lean`: `footX_frag` (from `run_foot`), `footX_four`, `footX_three` (through `footX_tail`), `footX_seven`, `footX_sevenFrom`, `footX_cons` (through `footX_consBody`), `footX_five`, `footX_ten`, the last three through `footX_binary` (the two runs and `+copy-lazy` of their results, whose entries lie apart, stated for the goal whose need is the pair of the operands' needs) and `foot_copy` (the `+copy` of two distinct sure needs).  `footX_sixP`: `[6 c a b]` under `%done` and `%pick` (the branches write registers they allocate, the test registers above both, `sixGenL` nothing, the `+copy` of the test's need with the two lazy blocks the registers of the test's need; the entries are the test's, the two branches' lazy needs and their entries).  `footX_sixN`: `[6 c a b]` under a fork-free `%next` (`+fork-sure` writes only the join's parameters, the goal's need, through `wroteIn_forkSure`; the branches write registers they allocate or the copies `s1` and `s0`, which are fresh and apart; `+sect` changes no block, `wroteIn_sameBlocks`).  `runX_foot_of`: the induction over `FragX` with that case as its hypothesis, and `runX_foot`: fact (i) over `FragX` with no hypothesis. |
| `Ska/CompLeafKeep.lean` | The leaf conversion with the relaxed input hypothesis (README Gaps 12.2).  `Clear g`: no block at or above `g.bo`; `clear_gen0`, and `Clear.grow`, `Clear.growU` keep it; `clear_new`, `clear_newU`: from a clear state every block of the end state at a label from the start's `bo` on was made by the run, so it writes only the footprint and jumps below its label (or as `JumpsU` says).  `steps_transfer`: a path of one program is a path of every program with the same blocks at the labels it enters.  `steps_exit`: the frame lemma up to the first exit, for blocks at labels of `L` that write only `Q` and may jump out of `L` (`WrIn`); its exit case returns the configuration before the exit jump, the jump and the block it enters.  `regionProg`: the program of the proof, with the blocks of a state at the labels of a range and one block at the goal's label, and no other block (`regionProg_in`, `regionProg_z`, `regionProg_out`, through `putFrom`).  `run_jump`: a configuration at its terminator runs as `goJ` of the jump it takes.  `leaf_exit`: if entering `je` does what entering a jump `j` without arguments below `g0.bo` does from some register file in every `regionProg` at `j.there`, then one register file `R'`, equal to the start outside `Q`, does it in every program that agrees with the run (the programs with `bomB` and `zeroB` at `j.there` exclude an end inside the run and fix the state and the trace).  `holdsN_pointwise`: a need held register by register by several files is held by one.  `leaf_sem` and `leaf_keep`: from `RunOK`, `Grow`, `Foot` and `Clear g0`, for a goal whose jumps pass no arguments and go below `g0.bo` (`LeafJ`), the code keeps the registers below `g0.re`, from `g1.re` on and of the result's sure need, outside the goal's need; for `%next` the need is held by `R'` through one program `⟨[], [], don r⟩` per register `r`. |
| `Ska/CompLeafRel.lean` | The relaxed `sem` (README Gaps 12.2).  `InR goal g0 g1 sur r`: the input registers a run may be given, from its end on, below its start and not named by its goal, or in its result's sure need `sur`; `KeepI goal g0 ned r`: what the code keeps, the registers below the start not named by the goal and every input register.  `SemPR` (`%done`, `%pick`), `SemGR` (any goal) and `SemNR` (`SemGR` at `%next`): the `sem` fields with `InR` as the hypothesis on `ned` and, for `SemGR`, `KeepI` as the keep set; `SemPR.strict` and `SemGR.strict` give the strict forms back.  The form planned first, `g1.re ≤ r ∨ NMem nex.laz.sure.ned r` with the old keep set, does not compose through two operands: in `[5 [0 2] [0 3]]` the whole's sure need is `pair rp rq`, and the `sem` of `[0 2]` would be asked for `rq`, below its start and outside its sure need `pair rp none`; in `[5 [0 1] [1 6]]` the operand `[0 1]` runs for `%next rp` with `rp` in `ned`, which the old keep set drops.  `leaf_keepK`: the leaf conversion `leaf_sem` with the footprint `run_foot` for any keep set outside the footprint.  `runOKP_frag_semR`, `runOKN_frag_semR`: the relaxed `sem` at the leaves, from `Clear g0` and `LeafJ`. |
| `Ska/CompRelSix.lean` | The relaxed `sem` over `FragP` and `FragN`, proved beside the strict relations (README Gaps 12.2).  The relations keep their strict `sem`, because the leaf conversion and the footprints come after the files that build them in the import order (`CompLeafKeep.lean` imports `CompNestPick.lean`, `CompFootX.lean` imports `CompBinaryN.lean` and `CompFragM.lean`); the relaxed `sem` is proved by the same induction from the relations of the parts and the relaxed `sem` of the parts.  `OKPR`, `OKNR`: what the induction proves, `OKP` and the hypotheses of `runN_ok` with `LeafJ`, `Clear g0` and `SemPR`, `SemNR` as the conclusion.  `sixP_semR`: `[6 c a b]` for `%done` and `%pick`, the `sem` part of `sixP_ok` with the relaxed hypothesis passed on (the test gets it as it is, the lazy blocks of the branches because the test's sure need lies from `g2.re` on, `Rc.regs`).  `lazyBlkNR`, `sixNext_semR`: the same under a fork-free `%next`, where the input registers also avoid the goal's need (`dsn`).  `runP_semR`, `runN_semR`: every run on `FragP` and `FragN`. |
| `Ska/CompRelCopy.lean` | The copy block keeps the merged need (README Gaps 12.2, a step toward `binaryN_ok` with the relaxed `sem`).  `holdsN_some`: a register of a held need holds a value.  `execOps_don_zero`, `runs_read`: a run of ops (`Runs`) that does not write a register keeps it, when it holds a value after the ops.  `copy_okR`: `copy_ok` with the clause that the copy block changes no register of the merged need (from `wr_copyNeed`: the ops write only registers of the two needs outside the merged one), which `binaryN_ok` needs when the input registers lie in the merged need, as `rq` of `[5 [0 2] [0 3]]` may. |
| `Ska/CompRelBinary.lean` | The relaxed `sem` through two operands, a unary operand and `%7` (README Gaps 12.2).  `KeepB`: `Keep` and the registers of the two operands' needs, the keep set the block lemmas must give.  `binaryN_semR`: `SemGR` of the shape of `binaryN_ok` from `RunOKN` and `SemNR` of the two operands and a block lemma with `KeepB`; the input registers go through the copy block by `copy_okR`, to `p`'s run as its sure need, as below its start outside `lp` (when in `q`'s sure need) or as above its end, and to `q`'s run a register of `lp` as below its start outside `lq`.  `n5Pre_okK`: the block of `%5` with `KeepB` (under `%next` it stores only into the collapse register and a fresh one).  `SemGR.toP`.  `runOKG_n5_semR`: `[5 p q]` for any fork-free goal, given `OKNR` of the operands and `Clear`.  `split_okK`: `split_ok` whose block keeps the head and tail registers (`wr_splitNeedS`, `runs_read`).  `consN_semR`: cons under a fork-free `%next`.  `unaryN_semR`: the shape of `unaryN_ok` with a block that keeps the operand register.  `runOKN_n7_semR`, `runOKP_n7g_semR`: `[7 p q]` with `q` a leaf, from `OKNR` of `p` and `leaf_keepK` of `q`. |
| `Ska/CompRelUnary.lean` | The relaxed `sem` of `[4 p]` and `[3 p]` for every goal (README Gaps 12.2).  `unaryG_semR`: the shape of `unaryG_ok` for `%done` and `%pick`, from `SemNR` of `p` and a block that keeps the operand register `a` besides `Keep`, the counterpart of `unaryN_semR`.  `runOKN_n4_semR`, `runOKN_n3_semR` (with `semN_n3_of_none` for the branch where the goal asks nothing of the product), `runOKP_n4_semR`, `runOKP_n3_semR` (`%done`), `runOKP_n4p_semR`, `runOKP_n3p_semR` (`%pick`): the blocks of the strict proofs with the keep set extended by `a`, since in `[4 [0 1]]` the input need may hold `a`, which the increment block does not write (it stores only into the product register). |
| `Ska/CompRelCons.lean` | Cons under `%done` and `%pick` and `[7 [0 axe] q]` with the relaxed `sem` (README Gaps 12.2).  `consN_semRK`: `consN_semR` as `OKNR`.  `runOKP_consD_semR`: under `%done`, the goal `%next (lazyReg r)` of the `%don` block, through `unaryG_semR` with the identity.  `runOKP_consP_semR`: under `%pick`, through `binaryN_semR` with the goal `%pick` and the `%bom` block.  `semGR_from`, `semPR_from`: `[7 [0 axe] q]` from the relaxed `sem` of `q`, whose sure-need registers are those of the whole (`fromSure_spec`), so `InR` carries over. |
| `Ska/CompRelTen.lean` | `[10 [a v] q]` with the relaxed `sem` for every goal (README Gaps 12.2).  `n10N_semR`: the proof of `n10N_body` with `binaryN_semR` and the block of `into_okK`, whose keep clause gives `KeepB` (the recipient's need as `lp`, the donor's as `lq`).  `runOKN_n10_semR` (`OKNR`), `runOKP_n10D_semR`, `runOKP_n10P_semR` (through `unaryG_semR`). |
| `Ska/CompRelX.lean` | The relaxed `sem` over `FragX` (README Gaps 12.2).  `OKXR`: `OKPR` for `%done`, `OKNR`, and `OKPR` for every `%pick` whose jumps pass no arguments.  `runX_semR`: every nomm of `FragX` meets `OKXR`, by the induction of `runX_ok`, every case, with no hypothesis (the strict relations of the parts from `runX_ok`).  `runX_semR_gen0`: the run for `%done` from `gen0` meets `SemPR`.  The top theorems are unchanged: they still use the strict `sem`, which `SemPR.strict` recovers. |
| `Ska/KnowSound.lean` | `knowGo_sound`: the nomm `+know` folds under a bake stands for the same formula on the subjects that fit both the bake and the code requirement, and its product sock fits every product. Every fold drops only a safe computation. `know_annot_of_implies`: on every subject of the bake, if the bake implies the code requirement. |
| `Ska/BakeGraph.lean` | A model of `+bake-graph`, arm by arm. `bakeLoop_sound`: at the end of the worklist, every site that `+pick` sends to no existing variant fits its callee's bake. `variant_annot`: a variant's nomm stands for its code bell's formula on the subjects of its bake and code requirement. `bakeOf` has the guard of commit 8f2162e (the bake is `less.c` when `+huge:so less.c both` says no), so every bake implies its code requirement (`bakes_ok`, `BakeImp.lean`), and the composition across pokes is `skaPokeB_sound` (`PokeBake.lean`). |
| `Ska/CompNestCoerce.lean`, `Ska/CompNest.lean` | Every lazy block of a lazy need, at any depth, in the order `+coerce-lazy` visits them (`entries`); `coerceLazyL_spec`: each gets ops in front that fill its need from the input registers, and no other block changes. Below `+lazy-max`, `+lazy-size` is one more than the number of entries. |
| `Ska/CompNestPick.lean`, `Ska/CompNestFinalP.lean` | `FragP`: `%6` nested in branches and in tests to any depth, under `%done` and `%pick`. `finishLF_bellContractP` and `crash_iffP`: for such a nomm with at most 255 `%6` (so `+lazy-shut` never fires and no proxy is made), the final pass meets the contract and the code crashes exactly when the nomm does. The final pass goes through `toStraight_entryR` with the rank `upRk`, and `finishLF_bellContractR`, `crash_iffR` state the same for any nomm whose run for `%done` meets `RunOKP`. (`CompNestRun.lean` and `CompNestFinal.lean` hold an earlier, narrower version and helpers.) |
| `Ska/BakeImp.lean` | When a bake implies its code requirement: `uniSock_left`, `purr_sub`, `normSockH_rev`, and an invariant of the worklist. `bakeOf_ok`: with the guard of commit 8f2162e every new bake is `BakeOK` (under `less.c`, and with a noun when it is not `less.c`: `uniSock_void`, `normCD_inhab`), and `bakes_ok`: every bake at the end of the worklist is `BakeOK`, with no hypothesis.  `bakes_ok_of_meet` (from `SitesMeet`, false on the real compiler, `SitesMeetFalse.lean`) is now a corollary that does not use its hypothesis; before the guard its conclusion was false too and the compiler miscompiled (`VariantCollision.lean`). The header explains, from the Hoon, why two identities of one code bell are rare within a poke (only through the `.indi` test, a `%he` generalization, or an incomplete `+huge:so`), and records a check of `SitesMeet` on the real compiler: 11,370 sites under 3,033 bakes after fifteen pokes up to `(ream '(add 1 2)')`, with no failure. |
| `Ska/PokeBake.lean` | `+ska-poke` with `+bake-graph`, `dat`, `new-code` and the code table. `skaPokeB_sound`: the invariant `InvB` is kept across pokes, so every bell of the real code table agrees with `+mink` on every subject that fits it.  `BakesImply` holds of every `+bake-graph` by the guard of commit 8f2162e (`bakesImply`), so the hypothesis `himp` is gone (before the guard it was false, `SitesMeetFalse.lean`, and its failure miscompiled, `VariantCollision.lean`).  `InvB.varsSub`: every variant `vars.lon` lists under a code bell has a bake under that bell's code requirement.  `InvB.variant_agree`: a variant listed under a code bell `c` has `c`'s formula, and on every subject of its bake, which fits `less.c`, its entry agrees with `+mink` on `c`'s formula; `InvB.collide`: two code bells that list one variant share that one entry and it is right for both (the variant key `[bake fol]` needs no code bell, since `CodeOK` states the contract of an entry on its own bake). |
| `Ska/PokeAna.lean` | `+ska-poke` as the analysis arm of `+poke` (`anaArm`, `skaPokeB` on `[&+sub fol]`). `AnaHyp` from `SitesMeet` of every poke (`anaHyp_of_meet`). The root's code requirement holds of the subject (`skaPoke_root_fits`). `pokeS_sound_of_meet`, `pokeS_invKept_of_meet` and `gap_of_meet` need only `CompHyp` and `PokeSites`, and `anaHyp_of_bakes` needs `PokeBakes`; both were false of the Hoon before commit 8f2162e (`SitesMeetFalse.lean`, `VariantCollision.lean`).  With the guard `PokeBakes` always holds (`pokeBakes`), so `anaHyp_of_arm`, `pokeS_sound_of_comp`, `pokeS_invKept_of_comp` and `gap_of_comp` need only `CompHyp`.  The root's code requirement holds of every subject of `root.1` by `app_fits`, whatever cape is united with `want` (1813f09). |
| `Ska/PokeHalt.lean` | `FullAnswers` from `CompHyp` and one hypothesis per loop (`AnaEnds`, `BakeEnds`, `RootAnswers`; `fullAnswers_of_loops`; `PokeSites` is no longer needed since the guard of commit 8f2162e). The final pass never crashes (`finalEnds_always`). The worklist does not depend on extra fuel (`bakeLoop_mono`), and bakes only lose knowledge (`bakeLoop_grows`). Not proved: that the worklist and the analysis end. |
| `Ska/BakeMeasure.lean` | The normal form of `+norm-sock` (`CanonCD`, `normCD_canon`, `normCD_of_canon`) and a measure `kap` on it: on normal socks, a strict loss of nouns lowers it (`kap_sub`, `kapS_lt`), and the same nouns give the same sock (`canon_ext`).  Every bake at the end of the worklist is `less.c` or normal (`bakes_canon`), and a change of a bake that keeps its nouns goes to or from `less.c` (`noLoss_lessc`).  `less.c` is often not normal on the real compiler (2,045 of 3,033 pairs checked), so `BakeEnds` still needs a bound on the changes back to `less.c`. |
| `Ska/SitesMeet.lean` | Obsolete for good: since the guard of commit 8f2162e `bakes_ok` needs no `SitesMeet`.  A dead route (true theorems, kept as a record; `SitesMeet` is false, see `SitesMeetFalse.lean`).  `SitesMeet` from three smaller invariants (`sitesMeet_of_parts`): `LessProvides` (under the code requirement itself, every site provides its callee's code requirement), `KnowMono` (`+know` is monotone in the subject sock: under a bake below `less.cur` each site has a site of the same callee under `less.cur` that knows no more; false for the model in general, see `KnowMonoFalse.lean`) and `SitesInhab` (no site's sock is `%void`).  `pokeSites_of_parts`: `PokeSites` from the three for every poke.  None of the three is proved; all three held on the real compiler on the 11,370 sites of `sitesmeet2.hoon` (3,033 pairs of a code bell and a bake, 1,555 of them strict) with no failure (`tools/hoon/sitesmeet3.hoon`).  `LessProvides` reduces (an argument in the header) to the coherence of the products of the identities of one code bell. |
| `Ska/KnowMonoFalse.lean` | Obsolete for good since the guard of commit 8f2162e (a part of the `SitesMeet` route).  `knowMono_false`: `KnowMono` fails for every folding environment whose `dat` has two given datums, so it cannot come from the sock operations of `+know` alone.  Under the bake a read folds and loses its provenance, a call on the other branch of a `%6` keeps provenance `2`, and `+double-int` forgets the part (`|`), while under `less.cur` neither folds and the join keeps `[& |]` `[7 0]`.  `SitesMeet` is not refuted by it (the site knows `|`).  Not checked on the real compiler. |
| `Ska/SitesMeetNF.lean` | Obsolete for good: since the guard of commit 8f2162e `bakes_ok` needs no `SitesMeet`.  A dead route (true theorems, kept as a record; `SitesMeet` is false, see `SitesMeetFalse.lean`).  `sitesMeet_of_nf`: `SitesMeet` from `LessProvidesNF` (under `less.cur`, `+know` without folding lists sites that provide their callees' code requirements) and `KnowMeetsNF` (each site under a bake shares a noun with a site of the same callee of that run without folding), with no `SitesInhab`.  `+knit:so`, `+double-int` and `+pull:so` keep `Meets` (`meets_knit`, `meets_doubleInt`, `meets_doubleInt_left`, `meets_pull`).  Both invariants held on the real compiler (`tools/hoon/sitesmeet4.hoon`: 11,370 sites under 3,033 bakes, 11,661 sites without folding, no failure). |
| `Ska/KnowMeets.lean` | Obsolete for good: since the guard of commit 8f2162e `bakes_ok` needs no `SitesMeet`.  A dead route (true theorems, kept as a record; `SitesMeet` is false, see `SitesMeetFalse.lean`).  `knowMeetsNF_of`: `KnowMeetsNF` from `DatInhab` (datum products have a noun), `LessInhab` (code requirements of datums have a noun) and `NoAx0Dat` (no `%10` at axis `0`), all three checked on the real compiler (`tools/hoon/sitesmeet5.hoon`).  By induction on the nomm over the run under the bake and the run without folding under `less.cur` (`knowGo_meets`, every case), with `+patch-prod` and `+darn:so` keeping `Meets` (`meets_patchProd`, `meets_darn` through `forceEdit`), literals knowing exactly their noun (`knowGo_lit`), and the run without folding never crashing (`knowGo_nf_some`).  `sitesMeet_of_lessNF`: `SitesMeet` from `LessProvidesNF` and the three facts, and `pokeSites_of_nf`: `PokeSites` from them for every poke (`PokeNF`).  The three facts hold of every poke from `InvB` (`poke_datFacts`, from `skaPoke_dat` and the memo invariant `PokeInv.dat`), so `PokeSites` needs only `LessProvidesNF` of every poke (`PokeLessNF`, `pokeSites_of_lessNF`), which is false on the real compiler for some pokes (`tools/hoon/voidscc.hoon`, see `SiteCover.lean`), so this route to `PokeSites` is closed.  The residue of `LessProvidesNF` as `ProdSame` (two identities of one code bell have the same patched products; `ProdMeet` is the weaker form), not proved to imply it; no two such identities occurred in the fifteen pokes checked, and on seven pokes built to make them by an `.indi` miss (`tools/hoon/prodsame.hoon`, 8 groups) `ProdSame` (under `less.c`), `ProdMeet`, `LessProvidesNF` and `SitesMeet` held with no failure. |
| `Ska/LessProvides.lean` | Obsolete for good: since the guard of commit 8f2162e `bakes_ok` needs no `SitesMeet`.  A dead route (true theorems, kept as a record; `SitesMeet` is false, see `SitesMeetFalse.lean`).  `lessProvidesNF_of_cover`: `LessProvidesNF` from `SiteCover` (every site of `+know` without folding under `less.cur` has a sock `ka` with a noun that provides the callee's code requirement and knows, on that requirement's cape, no more than the site), through `sub_app_cape` (a sock with a noun under `[cs ds]`, masked by `cs`, is still under `[cs ds]`; for example `&+[5 7]` under `[[& |] [5 0]]` masks to `[[& |] [5 7]]`) and `sub_of_cover`; `pokeSites_of_cover`: `PokeSites` from `SiteCover` of every poke (`PokeSiteCover`).  `SiteCover` is not proved.  The header says why the split into a replay of the analysis' walk as `+know` with a table `datA` and a comparison of two walks was dropped: one nomm can call two identities of one code bell with different products (the `.indi` miss of `prodsame.hoon`), so `datA` must be indexed by the site, and the comparison needs `ProdSame` at the site socks that occur and the provenance fact that `want` holds what each callee reads.  Inlining needs no case (an inlined call is a `%7` of the nomm).  `ProdSame` at the site socks that occur (the sites of `+know` without folding under `less.cur` and the callees' subject socks) held on the pokes of `prodsame.hoon` and `he2.hoon` (`tools/hoon/prodsites.hoon`: 490 site socks, 6,848 comparisons, no failure); the header ends with a plan for `SiteCover` (`knowT`, `Replay`, `Compare`). |
| `Ska/SiteCover.lean` | Obsolete for good: since the guard of commit 8f2162e `bakes_ok` needs no `SitesMeet`.  A dead route (true theorems, kept as a record; `SitesMeet` is false, see `SitesMeetFalse.lean`).  Refuted on the real compiler (`tools/hoon/voidscc.hoon`, `voidSccWitness`): `SiteCover`, so `Compare` and `LessProvidesNF`, are false for a poke whose root's SCC has a member that never returns.  The root's last run reads that member's product `%void` and keeps the other branch of a `%6`, while `dat` has the `|+~` that `+pop` makes of it, so `+know` without folding forgets the branch: one site, whose callee requires `&+[1 7]`, has `|+~`.  `KnowMeetsNF` and `SitesMeet` hold there.  The header says why the five formulas of `voidcall.hoon` make no `%void` and what a comparison that can hold would walk (the analysis' table with `%void` kept).  `knowT`: `+know` without folding and with `idc`, its call products read from a table `PTab` indexed by the sites so far, the callee bell and the site sock.  `+know false` with `dat` is the instance `datTab` (`knowT_datTab`, every case; `know_sites_knowT`).  `siteCover_of`: `SiteCover` from `Replay` (every datum has a `knowT` walk of its nomm under a subject sock `more` with `less.cur = app want more`, whose non-root sites have socks with a noun under their callees' code requirements) and `Compare` (each site of the walk under `less.cur` covers a site of the same callee in that walk on the callee's code cape); `lessProvidesNF_of_replay`.  Neither is proved, and no case of `Compare` is.  `ProdSameM`: `ProdSame` with equal maps (`prodSame_of_M`), which `tools/hoon/prodmaps.hoon` supports: 490 site socks, 6,848 comparisons, 0 maps differ; at 230 callee identities the unpatched product used by the analysis has the nouns and map of itself and of `dat`'s patched for its subject sock. |
| `Ska/SitesMeetFalse.lean` | Refuted on the real compiler before commit 8f2162e (`tools/hoon/bakedrop.hoon`, `bakeDropWitness`): `SitesMeet`, so `PokeSites`, and `BakesImply`, so `PokeBakes`.  On the changed compiler `SitesMeet` still fails at the one site, but the guard gives `H`'s variant the code requirement itself (`bakedrop.hoon`: `bad=0` of 7 variants, 2026-11-24), `BakesImply` is proved (`bakesImply`) and nothing needs `SitesMeet` any more.  The root's SCC has a member `F` that never returns.  The analysis reads `F`'s product `%void`, so a branch `[[[3 0] 5] F]` of a `%6` is `%void` and `+double-int` keeps the other branch `[[[1 7] 5] 9]`, and the callee `H = [2 [0 1] [0 4]]` requires axis `4` to be `[1 7]`.  `+know` under the bake folds the literal test to the first branch, where `F`'s product is the `|+~` of `+pop`, so `H`'s site knows axis `4` is `[3 0]`: no common noun (`witness_no_meet`, proved).  `+uni-sock` keeps the fully known side, so `H`'s variant gets a bake that is not under its code requirement (1 bad pair).  `KnowMeetsNF` holds there.  Within this poke the code is right (the site follows a call that never returns), but every route to `SitesMeet` is closed, including a reference walk that keeps the analysis' `%void` products.  A later poke makes the compiled code wrong (`VariantCollision.lean`). |
| `Ska/VariantCollision.lean` | Fixed by the guard of commit 8f2162e: on the changed compiler `collide.hoon` agrees (`[%after-witness %same]`, 2026-11-24), and `InvB.collide` (`PokeBake.lean`) proves a shared variant right for both code bells.  The record of the bug: a miscompile on the real compiler before the fix (`tools/hoon/collide.hoon`): after the poke of `bakeDropWitness`, the formula `[7 [[1 [[3 0] 5]] (dec 40)] [2 [0 1] [1 H]]]` on the library gives `[%0 7]` under `+eval-top` and `[%2 ~]` under `+mock` (alone the two agree, `collide0.hoon`).  `H`'s new code bell (axis `4` is `[3 0]`) bakes to the same sock as the dead variant of the first poke (`collide_same_bake`), variants are keyed by `[bake fol]`, and `new-code` skips a key the table has, so the live call runs the dead variant's nomm `[7 [0 1] [1 7]]` (`collide-vars.hoon` prints both code bells listing the one variant).  No invariant with a domain per bell repairs this.  The fix in the Hoon: use `less.c` as the bake when `(huge:so less.c bake)` fails; then `BakesImply` holds by construction and `GoodSite` survives the fallback (`bakeGuard_fits`).  Or key variants by their code bell too. |

| `Ska/AnaEnds.lean` | Termination of the analysis. Track F (Gaps 16, `PassMeasure.lean`) measured the two open points of the pass loop over 2,269 calls: the map changes alone (151 runs), no culled member is found again on that corpus (but `tools/hoon/repush.hoon` builds one, Gaps 16), and the mask of `less-code` rises in `CapeLe`, not in size. |

The invariant over the walked prefix (`WInv`) says that each reachable walked block has counts in `out` that cover the stack at its end. The relation on traces is `TrR D g t t'` for the bracketed stack `g`: read from its top, a frame of a key that is not dropped, or a frame without key, is on top of both traces, a frame of a dropped key (`TDrop`: not unsafe and opened) is on top of the trace before alone, and below `g` the traces are equal. A kept hint op pushes the same frame on both traces or pops the top of both (`kept_pole`, through `TrR.inv_keep`), and a dropped one pushes on the trace before alone or pops the frame of its key, which is on top (`drop_pole`), without crashing, since its reads hold values by `ReadsOK` (`drop_isSome`). With `DInvT` (`DInv` and some `TrimBr`) as the block invariant, `DriverRestT` is `DriverRest` with the field `trim` replaced by `P.trim = trimTraceHints` (discharged by `trim_fieldT`) and the field `alias` asked to give `DInvT`, and **`optimize_keeps_restT`** says that `optimizeF` keeps the code on blocks with `DInvT`, given `alias` and `switch`. |

| `Ska/OptAliasAll.lean` | The walk invariant of `+alias` over executions (README Gaps 12.8).  `epath es`: the path of blocks an execution with edges `es` took (`awa_entry_at`: `ACfgAt` along it).  `awa_steps_last`: an execution from the entry stays in block `0` or ends with a jump into its block, after which `awa_inblock_passed` gives the pass and the run of a prefix of the ops (`OpsRun`, through `as_sem`).  **`EndOK`**: at the end of a walked jumping block, the facts, immediates and block after are stored, the registers the end after reads are in `DefW`, `AOK` and `ABInv` hold with the global `old` and `DoneE` along the path taken, and the jump the end before takes is listed in `rev` and taken by the end after to the same block with its arguments renamed by the global `old` (so of the same values, `argV`).  `awa_start_entry`, `awa_start_jump`: `AOK` and `ABInv` at a block start from `entryInfo` or from `EndOK` at the predecessor taken (`aedge_aok`, `aedge_abinv`, `awi_edge_side`, `aedge_pass`); `awa_end`: `EndOK` at the end of the block just walked (`awi_bodyOK`, `as_body`, `afn_jump`, `awa_fin_reads`); `awa_end_step`, `awa_rebase`: one more block of the walk for an end walked before (`awi_end_later` for one block of a walk over the whole `topo`); `awa_rev_keep`: a block of the walk deletes from `rev` only itself.  **`AWInv`** (`ARev`, `OldW`, the edges of blocks not walked still listed, `EndOK` for every execution), `awa_start` (the start of the block just walked, for an execution standing in it), `awa_step`, `awa_walk`, `awa_walk_part`, **`alias_walk_endOK`** and **`alias_walk_inv`**.  **`alias_walk_at`**: at every configuration of an execution that passed the parameters of its block and runs its body from place `k`, the block after is the parameters, the ops kept from `aliasBody` split at `k` (state `sta` there) and the end of `aliasFin`, and when no op before `k` is a dead fill the facts and immediates of `sta` hold and every register the next op (or the end) reads reads through the `old` of `sta` the value it holds (`awa_read_done`, from `DefFirst`).  **`alias_walk_end`**: at the end of a block that leaves the function, the end after returns what the end before returns on the values of the code before (`afn_end`; a `%bom` end is kept as it is).  A first piece of the agreement relation: **`AKept`** (the values of the code after equal those of the code before at every register of an immediate, every register a fact names, and the `old` of every done register) and **`aedge_kept`**, its transfer along the edge taken, the jump after passing the arguments before renamed by `old`. |
| `Ska/OptAliasSim.lean` | Toward the simulation of `+alias`: the agreement relation `AKept` through the body.  `ACov` (the registers `AKept` covers), **`acov_op`** (after one step of `aliasOp` a covered register was covered before or is the destination of a kept op), **`akept_op`** (`AKept` after one step, the code after writing a kept destination with the same value and leaving a dropped one alone), **`akx_exec`** (a kept op after, with its reads renamed by `old`, runs as the op before when its reads read through `old` the values before: `XRel`, both crash or the same tone, trace, state and write), **`akd_exec`** (a dropped op before goes on, from `ReadsOK`, `LocalOK` and the facts), **`akb_op`** (one op forward on the values, with `AKept` after). |
| `Ska/OptAliasKeeps.lean` | Pieces of `KeepsB` for the model of `+alias` (README Gaps 12.8).  `aStart`: the state at the start of a body in the walk (`awa_start` now returns it).  **`alias_at_of`**: given the walk up to block `o` (`pre`, `g1`, `aStart`) and the state `sta` after the first `k` ops, at every configuration of an execution from the entry that passed the parameters of `o` and runs its body from `k`, every register read at `k` is done, and before the first dead fill `AOK` and `ABInv` hold at `sta` with `Done` the `bodyDone` of the first `k` ops from `DoneS`, and `BodyOK` holds for the next ops up to the first dead fill.  **`akept_jump`**: `AKept` at the end of the body of a walked jumping block, with the walk-time state there, rebased to the `old` of the walk at a later point and `DoneE` there (`awa_frame_seg`, `awa_done_seg`: the frame of the walk over a part of `topo`).  **`APres`** (presence: a done register present in the file before has its `old` present in the file after), **`apres_op`** (kept through one op, kept or dropped; a dropped `%mov` from a poisoned register copies the poison, `dropped_dst_val`), **`apass_loop`** (the pass after of the renamed arguments goes on when presence holds and neither an argument nor its renaming is a parameter), **`akp_edge`** (along the edge taken, the pass after goes on and `AKept` and presence hold at the start of the block jumped to), **`akb_live`** (one op of a live body forward with `AOK`, `ABInv`, `AKept` and presence after) and **`akj_jump`** (the jump at the end of a walked block with its pass: the end after on the file after takes the jump renamed, the pass after goes on, and `AKept` and presence hold at the start state of the block jumped to). |
| `Ska/OptAliasPick.lean` | The choice of `+alias` instantiated: `pickR`, the first key of `rap` of the `+silt` of a list of registers (on the treaps of the Hoon the root `n`, since `+rep` visits `n` first), and **`pickR_mem`**: it picks a member of a non-empty list (the hypothesis `hpick` of the model).  The pill link owes that the Hoon's `n` is this key. |
| `Ska/OptAliasRel.lean` | The relation of the simulation of `+alias` (README Gaps 12.8).  `ASplit`, `ASplitOK`: the walk split at place `k` of the body of a block (`pre`, `l`, the states `g1`, `g2`, the start state `st0`, the ops kept `outa` up to `k` reaching `sta` and `outb` after it reaching `st'`, the end `rf`), `ASplit.done` its done registers.  **`AliasI`**: the configuration before is reached from the entry, both have the same label, trace and state, and one of three modes holds: **`ALive`** (both passed, the body before is the body from `k`, the body after is `outb` with the end `rf.1`, no dead fill below `k`, `AKept` and presence at `sta`), **`ADead`** (`%imm` ops only up to a `%bom` on both sides), **`APend`** (right after a jump: the arguments after renamed, present when present before, no argument a parameter, and whenever the pass before goes on the pass after goes on into the live mode at place `0`).  `asplit_g2` (the walk state after the block), `alive_at` (`AOK`, `ABInv`, `BodyOK` and the reads done, from `alias_at_of`), `alive_drop_exec` (a dropped op goes on, trace and state unchanged), **`alive_op`** (one op forward into the next place), `alive_dead` (the live mode at a dead fill is the dead mode), `alive_next` (a kept op runs as the op before, `XRel`), `alive_end`, **`alive_jump`** (the jump at the end into the pending or the live mode, from `akj_jump`). |
| `Ska/OptAliasFwd.lean` | The simulation of `+alias` forward.  `endPole`, `endOf_op`, `endPole_xrel`, `fe_step`; `apend_pass` (the pass from the pending mode), `adead_step`, `adead_flush` (the code after runs its `%imm` ops of the dead mode).  **`alias_fwd_step`**: a step of the code before is matched by at most one step of the code after with `AliasI` after.  **`alias_fwd_end`**: an end of the code before is an end of the code after (a kept op ends as the op before, a dropped op does not end, an end of a block returns the same by `afn_end` and `endR_congr`).  **`alias_fwd`** on paths. |
| `Ska/OptAliasBack.lean` | The simulation of `+alias` backward.  `renameP`, **`aop_rename`** (a kept op is the op before with its reads renamed by `old`), `poleReads_rename`, `safeAssign_rename`, `poleReads_alias`; **`afn_jump_back`** (the end before takes a jump when the end after does, on a file where the facts hold and the reads hold values); `passLoop_len`, `apass_some` (the pass before goes on from `ArgsOK` and the check).  The normal form `ANorm`, `AliasN` (pending, live at the end or at a kept op that is no dead fill, dead at the end) and **`alias_norm`** (the code before runs the dropped ops and the `%imm` ops of the dead mode); **`alias_bwd_step`**, **`alias_bwd`**; at the normal form **`alias_bwd_end`**, **`alias_reads`**, **`alias_local`**, **`alias_args`** (`EntryOK` of the code after from that of the code before). |
| `Ska/OptAliasKeepsB.lean` | **`KeepsB` for the model of `+alias`.**  `alias_zero` (block `0` walked from the initial state, `old` the identity), `alias_entry` (the two entries related: live at place `0`, or pending when block `0` has parameters), **`alias_keeps_of`** (`Keeps c (withBlocks c g.new)` for the walk `g` under `DInvS`, `TopoInvR` and `hpick`), **`alias_keepsB`** (`KeepsB bl bl'` for `aliasM pick n bl rev topo = some (f, bl')`, the first conjunct of `AliasFieldSSAB`, with no hypothesis beyond the driver's invariant) and `alias_keepsB_pickR` (with the choice `pickR`). |
| `Ska/OptAliasResult.lean` | The blocks `+alias` returns.  **`aw_new`** (a block of `g.new` is the block the walk made of a walked block that was not dropped), `afin_nolti`, **`afin_jmps`** (every jump of the end after goes to a target of the end before, the edge kept in `rev`), `alias_nolti`, `alias_parNodup`, `alias_targetsSub`, `alias_acyclic`, `rev_keep_walk`, **`alias_closed`** (every jump of a block after goes to a block after), `idxOf_filter_lt`, **`topoOK_filter`**, **`alias_topoInvR`** (`TopoInvR` of the result on the filtered `topo` and `revCfg`).  **`AliasRestSSAB`** (what is left of the field: `PassOK`, `DownPar`, `TrimBr`, `AliasSSAB` of the result), **`aliasFieldSSAB_of`** and **`optimize_keeps_alias`** (`+optimize` with the model of `+alias` keeps the code on `DInvS`, given `hpick` and `AliasRestSSAB`). |
| `Ska/OptAliasStatic.lean` | The walk invariant of `+alias` free of executions.  **`DoneW`** (no definition, or a definition that counts in a walked block, a parameter or an op of a block that jumps; in `ClR`; no dead fill walked) and **`SInv`**: `ARev`, `OldW`, the edges not walked yet listed, the blocks of `new` walked, every listed edge of a walked block a jump of its end after (`live`), the global `old` mapping `DoneW` into `DefW` (`glob`), and at the end of every walked jumping block facts, immediates and the registers of the end after within `DefW` (`ends`).  `sst_body` (`ab_op` without values), **`sst_start`** (`ABInv` at a block start from `ends` of one listed predecessor through `aedge_abinv`, or from `entryInfo`), **`sst_step`**, `sst_walk_part`, **`sst_at`** (`SInv` right after each walked block); **`afin_regs`** (the registers the end after reads, hop arguments included, are `old` of those before), **`afin_rev_tgt`** (an edge `aliasFin` leaves listed is a jump of the end after). |
| `Ska/OptAliasPass.lean` | **`alias_downPar`**, **`alias_passOK`**: `DownPar` and `PassOK` of the blocks `+alias` returns, under `DInvS`, `TopoInvR` and `hpick`.  The end after of a walked jumping block reads only `DefW` registers right after it (**`alias_fin_defW`**, from `SInv.ends`), and a parameter of a block below a target, later in `topo`, is outside `DefW` (`awi_par_notDefW`); `rfrom_idx`, `rfrom_reach`, `rfrom_sub`. |
| `Ska/OptAliasTrim.lean` | **`alias_trimBr`**: `TrimBr` of the result with every key renamed by the final `old` (`renKey`).  **`brOp_rename`**, **`abr_body`**: a renamed body acts on the renamed stack as the body before when no register of a key is written later in the body.  **`trb_defs`**: every definition of a register read at a place is an earlier op of the block, a definition in a block before it in `topo`, or a dead fill of the block after the read.  **`trb_origin`**: a key at a block start was pushed in a block before it in `topo`.  A block with a dead fill ends in `%bom` and is taken up to its first dead fill (`trb_min`), after which only `%imm` ops follow. |
| `Ska/OptAliasAvail.lean` | The targets of renaming are defined in the code after.  **`TAv st A Done`** (immediates, registers facts name and `old` of done registers in `A`), **`tav_op`**, **`tav_body`** (`A` grows by the destinations of the ops kept), **`areads_rename`** (the reads of a renamed op are renamings of its reads), **`abody_at`** (the `i`-th op kept and the walk up to it), `AvP` (the registers defined on a path of the result), **`TInvA`** (edges of walked blocks listed, `TAv` at the end of every walked jumping block along every path of the result, the `old` of its definitions available), `tav_start`, **`tav_pre`** (`TAv` at every place before the first dead fill along every path of the result), **`tav_step`**. |
| `Ska/OptAliasFirst.lean` | **`alias_defFirst`**: `DefFirst` of the result (a read after is `old` at its op of a read before, which `tav_pre` makes available: no definition, a definition after on the path, a parameter or an op kept before it), with `tav_walk`, `tav_at`, `sst_pre`, `bpathG_some`, **`alias_allDefs`**. |
| `Ska/OptAliasOnce.lean` | **`AliasRestSSAB` is false**: `DefOnceB` of the result fails (header: a dead fill of the register of an `%inc` whose duplicate `+alias` renames to it, read in another block; checked in `OptAliasWitness.lean`).  What holds: **`alias_restB`** (`PassOK`, `DownPar`, `TrimBr`, `DefFirst` of the result under `DInvS`, `TopoInvR`, `hpick`), **`alias_onceB`** and **`alias_restSSAB_fresh`** (`DefOnceB` too under **`FillFresh`**: no register with a dead fill in a reachable block has a definition that counts; `alias_fill`: a dead fill before stays one after; `alias_live_op`, `abody_split`, `abody_take_mono`), **`alias_fillFresh`** (`+alias` keeps `FillFresh`).  **`AliasOnceB`** (the conjunct left), `aliasRestSSAB_of_once`, **`optimize_keeps_aliasO`** and `optimize_keeps_aliasO_pickR` (the driver theorem with `hpick` and `AliasOnceB`, whose hypothesis is false). |
| `Ska/OptAliasWitness.lean` | `LM` (a map as an association list, so the models evaluate by `decide`).  **`not_aliasOnceB`**: blocks with `DInvS` and `TopoInvR` (`v_dinvS`, `v_topo`) on which the result of the model of `+alias` (`v_alias`) has two definitions that count of one register.  **A miscompile of the model of `+optimize`** on blocks with `DInvS` (`w_dinvS`, `w_opt`): round 1 of `+alias` makes a dead fill live (it renames a duplicate `%inc` to the filled register, read in a block not after the fill), `+remove-hops` then moves a `~` argument into a parameter as `[%imm 0 p]`, and round 2 of `+alias` drops the fill and renames the later read to `p`: the code returns `0` where it returned `s + 1`.  `+comp` does not emit such blocks since commit `af5cc39` (the fill of the register a `%cel` tests is gone). |
| `Ska/OptAliasPlain.lean` | **Plain single assignment**, `DefOnceR` (every register has at most one definition, a parameter or an op, dead fills included, in the blocks reachable from `0`), and the block invariant of the driver **`DInvR`** (`DInvT`, `DefFirst`, `NoLti`, `DefOnceR`), which implies `DInvS` and `FillFresh` (`dInvR_dInvS`, `defOnceB_of_R`, `fillFresh_of_R`) and is implied by single assignment over all blocks (`defOnceR_of_defOnce`: `DefOnce`, the definitions of the whole map distinct).  Why it rather than `DInvS` with `FillFresh` (header): a pass keeps it through the map of the places of definitions after to places before that the proofs of `DefOnceB` already build (`onceR_of_map`, `onceR_of_sub`), with no clause on dead fills, and raw `+comp` output then owes no statement about dead fills.  Every pass of the loop keeps it: **`rub_onceR`**, **`mid_onceR`**, **`trim_onceR`** (`filt_onceR`), **`dead_onceR`** (on `TopoOK`), **`hops_onceR`** (`hk_onceR`, `hk_site`: the chains of `HkSites`, disjoint by `hk_disj`), **`alias_onceR`** (`alias_site_op`).  The header gives the two ways a pass makes a dead fill of an ordinary `%imm` (a merge with a `[%bom ~]` block, a `%brn` on a known immediate that is no loobean turned into `%bom`), under which the invariant survives. |
| `Ska/OptAliasPlainDriver.lean` | **`optimize_keeps_plain`**: `optimizeF` with the model of `+alias`, the real `+trim-trace-hints` and the model of `+switch-chains` keeps the code (`Keeps`) on blocks with `DInvR`, given `hpick` alone (`driverHypR_of`: every field of `DriverHyp` at `DInvR` and `TopoInvR`; `alias_fieldR`: `alias_onceB` with `FillFresh` from `DefOnceR`); **`optimize_keeps_plain_pickR`**: the passes of the Hoon (`pickR`), with no hypothesis but `DInvR` of the input blocks.  **`OptIn`**: the invariant raw `+comp` output owes, field by field with a one-line meaning each (`optIn_iff`: it is `DInvR`; `optimize_keeps_optIn`).  The witnesses of `OptAliasWitness.lean` violate it (`not_defOnceR_v`, `not_defOnceR_w`, `not_fillFresh_v`, `not_fillFresh_w`, `not_dInvR_v`, `not_dInvR_w`: register `2` is written by the `%inc` of block `0` and by the fill).  `ex_hops`, `ex_alias`: the two examples of the header of `OptAliasPlain.lean`, evaluated on `LM`. |
| `Ska/OptAliasFresh.lean` | The driver under the weaker invariant **`DInvF`** = `DInvS` and `FillFresh` (`dInvF_of_R`: `DInvR` implies it; it allows several dead fills of one register).  **`fillFresh_of_map`**: a map of the places of definitions after to places before, reachable, injective for a fixed register, and taking a definition that counts to one that counts, carries `FillFresh` over under `DefOnceB`.  Every pass keeps it: **`rub_fresh`**, **`mid_fresh`** (the identity map), **`trim_fresh`**, **`dead_fresh`** (`SubPass`: labels, parameters and ends kept, a monotone part of each body, `filt_sub`, `dead_sub`; `sub_live`: a dead fill before stays one after), **`hops_fresh`** (`hk_fresh`, through `hk_site`, `hk_live`, `hk_disj`), and `alias_fillFresh`.  **`optimize_keeps_fresh`** (given `hpick`) and **`optimize_keeps_fresh_pickR`** (no hypothesis but `DInvF`), through `driverHypF_of` and `alias_fieldF`; `not_dInvF_v`, `not_dInvF_w`. |
| `Ska/OptAliasPerm.lean` | The order of `pre` in `+alias` (track E, Gaps 15).  **`InfoEq`** (the units and `isCell` equal, `hedOf` and `telOf` with the same members, `isLoob ∨ loobImm` equal: what `InfoRel` fixes) and, up to it, **`loobC_joinReg`** (the class of `isLoob` of a join is the meet of the classes), `joinReg_congr`, `joinReg_comm`, `joinReg_assoc`, `joinReg_idem`, `joinRegE_eq`; the same for `joinInfoA` (`AInfoEq`) and `joinImmsA` (`ImmsEq`); `fold1_perm` (a fold over two permutations); **`aliasStart_perm`**, **`aliasStartImms_perm`**, **`aliasEdges_perm`**, **`paramInfo_perm`**, **`aliasPars_perm`**, `aliasPars_edges_perm` (related by `OptR`: both `none`, or both `some` and related); `perm_of_nodup`; `aliasStart_order` (a witness that equality fails). |
| `Ska/OptAliasBrz.lean` | Invariants of the walk of `+alias` on any input: **`BrzInv`** (a `%brz` end of `new` has a test register whose facts in `info` exist and have no `hasImm`: `afin_brz`, `brzInv_block`, `brzInv_walk`, **`brzInv_loob`**), **`RevNodup`** of `rev` (`revNodup_walk`), both at every state (`aliasM_inv`); **`aliasM_noLti`** (no `%lti` in the result when the input has none, with no other hypothesis); `aliasBlock_walked`, `aliasBlock_dropped`. |
| `Ska/OptAliasPickRoot.lean` | **`sawRel_root`**: a set noun related to a non-empty list by `SawRel` is `[pickR FinMap l L R]`, so the Hoon's `n.res` and `n.intersect` are `pickR FinMap` of the model's list; **`pickR_ext`** (the choice depends only on the members); **`infoRel_of_infoEq`** (`InfoRel N x` and `InfoEq x y` give `InfoRel N y`); `tapList_set_nodup` (the list of `~(tap in ...)` of a set has no duplicates). |
| `Ska/AuditAliasE.lean` | `#print axioms` of the main theorems of track E. |
| `Ska/OptBranch.lean` | `+remove-useless-branching` (`rubFin`, `removeUselessBranching`) and `rub_keepsR`: it keeps the code (`KeepsR`; `rub_keeps` of `OptArgs.lean` gives `Keeps`), with no hypothesis on the CFG.  A simulation on paths (`RubRel`, `rub_fwd`, `rub_bwd`, `rub_bwd_end`, `rub_reads`); `ReadsOK` is used where a `%clq` or `%brz` became a hop and so no longer reads its register. |
| `Ska/OptHops.lean`, `Ska/OptMiddle.lean`, `Ska/OptDead.lean` | Models of `+remove-hops` (`hopSeg`, `hopChain`, `hopsStep`, `removeHops`), `+remove-empty-middle` (`rewriteJump`, `rewriteHop`, `mkSub`, `passArg`, `readersOf`, `removeEmptyMiddle`) and `+remove-dead-code` (`safeAssign`, `deadBody`, `removeDeadCode`), with their preservation statements as definitions (`HopsKeeps`, with `ParNodup` and `PassOK`, proved in `OptArgs.lean` from `OptHopsSim.lean`; `MiddleKeeps`, with `PassOK` of the input and of the result and `ArgsOK` on the entry, proved in `OptMiddleSim.lean`; `DeadKeeps`, `Keeps` on a `topo` with `TopoOK`, proved in `OptDeadSim.lean`). |
| `Ska/OptRegLe.lean` | `RegLe R R'`: at every register the same unit, or poison in `R` and a value in `R'`.  An op, a pass, a branch and an end whose real reads read values in `R` do the same on `R'` (`execPole_le`, `evalPass_le`, `taken_le`, `endOf_le`), and a step of the code before that does not crash read values (`execPole_reads`, `taken_reads`, `endOf_reads`). |
| `Ska/OptHopsPass.lean` | The moves of a merged hop (`hopSeg_eq`: `[%mov a p]` or `[%imm 0 p]` per argument, in the reverse order) against the pass of the code before: both are runs of pairs (`runPairs`, `evalPassLoop_pairs`, `runMoves_pairs`), whose order does not matter when the parameters are distinct and no argument is one (`runPairs_spec`); `passRel_init`: the registers after the moves are above those after the pass, and the two succeed together. |
| `Ska/OptHopsBlocks.lean` | The blocks after `+remove-hops`: `MTail` (the tail and end a chain of merges appends to a block), `hopChain_spec`, `hopsFold_spec`, `hops_blocks` (every block after is the merge of the block at its label, every label of `topo` holds one or was merged into its only predecessor in `rev`, block `0` holds the merge of block `0`), `revCfg_mem` (`+rev-cfg` lists every member of `topo` that jumps to a block). |
| `Ska/OptHopsSim.lean` | **`hops_keepsR_of`**: `+remove-hops` keeps the code (`KeepsR`) given `HopsHyp` (every reachable block in `topo`, `rev` listing the predecessors in `topo`, `ParNodup`, `PassOK`), `topo` without duplicates and `0` first (`hops_keeps_of`, `hops_keeps`, `hopsKeeps` of `OptArgs.lean` give `Keeps`); `hops_parNodup`, `hops_passOK`: the pass keeps both hypotheses.  A simulation on paths (`HopI`: the same position with registers in `RegLe`; `HopII`: a pass pending before, its moves running after), forward (`hop_fwd_step`, `hop_fwd_end`) without hypothesis on the reads, backward through a normal form (`hop_norm`, `hop_bwd_step`, `hop_bwd_end`, `hop_bwd_reads`) with `ReadsOK`. |
| `Ska/OptDriver.lean`, `Ska/AuditOpt.lean` | `+optimize-once` (`structLoop`, `optimizeOnce`) and `+optimize` (`optLoop`, `optimizeF`) over the pass models, with `+alias`, `+trim-trace-hints`, `+switch-chains` as parameters (`Passes`); `optimize_keeps`: `optimizeF` keeps the code on blocks with `BInv` given `DriverHyp` (each pass keeps the code at the arguments the driver gives, over two invariants: `BInv` of the blocks alone, which every pass keeps whatever its change flag, and `TInv` of the blocks, `topo` and `rev`, which `+bb-topo` with `+rev-cfg` makes and a structural pass keeps when it reports no change); the fields are discharged in `OptDriverFields.lean` and `OptDriverTopo.lean`.  Termination is `OptHalts`.  The axiom audit of these theorems. |
| `Ska/OptMiddleBlocks.lean` | The blocks after `+remove-empty-middle` (`midStep`, `mid_blocks`, `MidBlocks.back`: same labels, parameters and bodies, the end from `middleFin`); the pass keeps `ParNodup` (`mid_parNodup`), and keeps `PassOK` exactly under its hypothesis, which is `PassOK` of its result (`mid_passOK`); `+remove-useless-branching` keeps both with no hypothesis (`rub_parNodup`, `rub_passOK`). |
| `Ska/OptDriverFields.lean` | The invariants meant for `DriverHyp`: `BlkInv` (`ParNodup` and `PassOK`) and `TopoInv` (`TopoOK`, and `rev` lists every member of `topo` that jumps to a block; `topoInv_revCfg` from `TopoOK`).  Discharged: the semantic half and `BlkInv` of the fields `hops` (`hops_field`) and `rub` (`rub_field`, `rub_keepsB`), and of `middle` under its hypothesis `PassOK` of the result (`middle_blkInv`, `middle_field`, from `middle_keeps`); the `TopoInv` halves and `Acyclic` are in `OptDriverTopo.lean`. |
| `Ska/OptMiddleAgr.lean` | Register files that agree on a set (`Agr`): an op whose registers (`poleRegs`) lie in the set does the same on both and keeps the agreement (`execPole_agr`); two passes whose arguments pass the same units (`argU`) and pass the check of `+eval-pass` succeed together and agree on the parameters (`passLoop_agr`); `pass_sub`, the substitution of `rewrite-hop` (after a pass a parameter holds what the argument `mkSub` maps it to passes). |

| `Ska/RawInvATX.lean` | `RunAT` on `FragX` (README Gaps 14): `ATR` (a `GA` and a `Fresh` result in `[1, g1.bo)`), `ATX`, the cases in the shape of `runX_eap` (`atx_frag` by `run_at` and `run_down`, `atx_seven`, `atx_sevenFrom`, `atx_four`, `atx_three`, `atx_binary` with `copyL_apart_some`, `atx_cons`, `ga_n5Pre`, `atx_five`, `atx_ten`, `atx_sixP`, `atx_sixN`), `ga_sixGenL`, `ga_same` (`+sect` by `SameBlocks`), `forkSure_out`, **`ga_forkSure`** (the fork blocks pass as many registers as the join has parameters, `NCopy.length`; the join hops to the goal's `Fresh` block), **`runX_at`**, **`runAT_fragX`** (`RunAT` on every nomm of `FragX` with at most 255 `%6`), and the theorems of `RawInvStX.lean` without `RunAT`: **`stIn_finishLFX_at`**, **`stIn_finishLUX_at`**, **`optInput_finishLFX_at`**, **`optInput_finishLUX_at`**, **`opt_total_finishLFX_at`**, **`opt_total_finishLUX_at`** (given only `GateTotalN` and `AliasRetN`, track H's). |
| `Ska/RawInvCheckC.lean` | Executable checks with indirect calls (README Gaps 14 (b)): on the 120 nomms of `enumC` run through `runLC envR` (`runC`), the raw blocks meet `Closed`, `ArityOK`, `TwoNA` (`stB`, the facts of `RunAT`), and both final passes (`finishLUC`, `finishLFC` as `naryC`) meet `okSt` (`Closed`, `ArityOK`, `TwoNA`, the block `0`) and `okReads` (`ReadsIn nargs` over the reachable blocks); 60 raw runs and 60 n-ary outputs have a jump with arguments (`argsB`). |
| `Ska/BugWitness.lean` | The three places where the Hoon broke the contract when this was written (an inlined call dropping its formula's formula, malformed operands, `%bare`). Fixed in the Hoon since; the models still describe the old code. |
| `NockSmallStep.lean`, `Nomm.lean`, `DecExample.lean`, `NOMM_PROOF.md` | The earlier work: small-step semantics, a model of `nomm-1`, a toy decrement. |

Every main theorem depends on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`
only.  There is no `sorry` and no `native_decide`.

## How a proof about an arm goes

A proof never unfolds Nock by hand.  It opens the arm's formula once
(`nock_open dec.loop`), and `simp only [nock_exec, ...]` runs it on a subject
that has variables in it, up to the first branch that the hypotheses do not
decide.  Calls into other arms are rewritten by those arms' theorems, and
recursion by the induction hypothesis.  For example the whole proof that the
Nock of `+put:by` computes `putF` is nine lines for ten cases.

Formulas that are constants stay closed until a proof opens them, so a
recursive call cannot unfold forever.

A theorem about a gate quantifies over the gate's context `c` and assumes
only what the gate's Nock reads from it: a predicate such as `LthIn c` says
that `c` holds the arm `+lth` at `lth.axis` and what `+lth` reads in turn.
The predicates are `abbrev`s of conjunctions of `slot?` equations, so a
hypothesis `hc : LthIn c` given to `simp` rewrites the `slot?` that the
executor cannot compute on the variable `c`.  Each family file proves that
the pill's layer satisfies its predicates (`arith_layer1`,
`mugNeeds_layer2`), so a change elsewhere in the pill leaves the theorems
standing.

Arithmetic in models stays out of reach of reduction.  The models of `+muk`
and `+mug` recurse through generic combinators (`iterDown`, `retry`,
`foldNoun`), and they are tied to the expressions that the execution
produces by small equations over variables (`mukBlock_eq`, `fmix32_eq`),
proved by `delta` and `Eq.refl`.  An equation lemma of a recursive
definition whose body calls the murmur3 arithmetic, or an `rfl` between two
such expressions that are not syntactically equal, took the elaborator past
3 GB.

A function that `simp` runs on the pill's formulas must not be structurally
recursive.  `nockW` of `Iter.lean` was first defined by structural recursion
on the formula, and `simp only` with its rules on the loop of `+mink` took
more than 2 GB after a few hundred steps, where the same rules for `nock` ran
in half a second.  Defined with `partial_fixpoint`, like `nock`, it runs as
fast as `nock`.

## Regenerating from a new pill

```sh
python3 tools/pill2core.py pill/steel.pill core/Pill.lean
python3 tools/pill2lean.py pill/steel.pill lemmas/Pill
python3 tools/pill2cue.py pill/steel.pill lemmas/Pill/Cue   # after pill2lean
```

`pill2cue.py` writes only the files whose text changes.  Its files hold no
terms: the windows and the nouns are defined by the commands of
`CueTac.lean` from the bits of `pill`, and the theorems are one line each.

`pill/steel.pill` is the steel pill of this branch (sha256 `b041a7ca...`,
793,205 bytes, built from the Hoon at af5cc39): its
`desk/sys/hoon.hoon` built from nothing and `desk/lib/nock-compilation.hoon`
on it, by the vere test harness without a ship (`U3NC_STEEL_GEN=out.pill`,
`U3NC_STEEL_SYS`, `U3NC_STEEL_LIB`).  `pill2lean.py` finds the arms by their
`%fast` labels, and the few arms without one through the arms that call them,
so a new pill needs no edits to the proofs unless the Nock of an arm changed.
The axes in `core/Statement.lean` are not regenerated: check them after a
change to the arms of the last three cores of the library.

## Caching

The generated files and the theorem files are Lean modules.  An arm file
exports its nouns without their bodies, except the exposed axis constants.
The arm's own theorem file reads the bodies with `import all`, and its callers
import only its theorems.  So:

1. A change to the Nock of one arm, after regeneration, changes its arm file
   only: an arm file is byte-identical when the arm's Nock and axis did not
   change, and the chain refers to arms by name.  It rechecks the arm file,
   the arm's theorem file, `Facts.lean` and `Audit.lean`, and nothing else,
   as long as the arm's theorems still hold.  (Measured by changing a
   character of the tape in `dec.battery`.)
2. A change to a declaration of the chain rebuilds the rest of the chain,
   `Facts.lean` and the layer files, and no gate theorem file.  A comment
   rebuilds the edited file only.
3. A clean build takes about 100 seconds (6 min 50 s of CPU) and peaks at
   2.7 GB for the whole build (measured 2026-11-23 under a 3 GB scope; before
   `lemmas/Pill/Cue/` it was 100 s, 5 min 35 s and 2.6 GB).  The files of
   `Pill/Cue/` form chains (`Win`, `J000`, ..., `KV`, then the `D` files in
   order), so that at most one of them is in memory at a time: with the `D`
   files built in parallel the build went past 3 GB.  The `D` files set
   `Elab.async false`, which keeps a theorem's elaboration from living until
   the end of the file (1 GB instead of 0.3 GB for 24,000 tokens).

## Gaps

1. **The statements are proved from a list of hypotheses.**
   `Assembly.lean` proves `Full` (`Loads`, `NockEquivalence`,
   `MockEquivalence`, `Halts`) from `Gap`; what is left of the project is
   that list.  The direction of `NockEquivalence` from the interpreter to
   Nock needs no hypothesis beyond those (`meek_conv`: `interp_bwd`, then
   `eval_of_minkF_nil`).
   One of them, `Halting`, was false of the Hoon up to 1813f09: the pass
   loop of `+analyze` did not end on the poke of `tools/hoon/swaploop.hoon`
   (`Ska/PassLoop.lean`).  The pill of this branch is built from the Hoon at
   1813f09, where `+run` unites `less-code` with the old entry's cape, and
   that poke returns.  `Halting` is not proved: `CgHalts` would need the
   measure of the header of `Ska/PassLoop.lean` (per entry, the cape size of
   `less-code`, which now only grows inside the cape of `bus`, then the
   knowledge of the product, which only shrinks, then `indi` and `seek`),
   with the provenance `map` and a culled member found again left open.
   Four changes of the Hoon made this morning are on the pill and in the
   models: the guard of `+bake-graph` (8f2162e, `Bake.bakeOf`,
   `Ska/VariantCollision.lean`), the open trace hints counted per key in
   `+trim-trace-hints` (753abe9, `Ska/OptTrim.lean`), the memo lookup of the
   `resolve` loop at the current identity's sock (1df76d0,
   `Ana.resolveStep`, linked by `resolveStepH_eq` and `resolve_stepW`), and
   the union of `less-code` with the old cape (1813f09, `runDatum`, linked
   by `run_datum_new` and `run_stepW`).  The links of the analysis
   (`resolve_iff`, `fol_loop_iff`, `analyze_iff`, `ska_callgraph_iff`,
   `ska_poke_ana_iff_pill`, `ska_poke_iff`) and its model theorems
   (`skaCallgraph_frozen`, `skaPoke_sound`, `skaPokeB_sound`,
   `skaCallgraph_closed`, the fuel monotonicity of `AnaFuel.lean`,
   `resolve_he_bound`) hold of the fixed compiler with their statements.
2. **The pill is tied to `root`** (`cueRoot` of `Assembly.lean`, from
   `cue_pill` of `lemmas/Pill/Cue/Root.lean`).  The kernel cannot evaluate
   `cue` on 793 KB in one piece, so `CueRel.lean` proves once that `cue`
   returns what the relation `Dec` accepts, and 381 generated theorems check
   the 546,237 tokens of the jam in pieces of at most 2000, reading the bits
   through windows of two chunks.  The table of back-references is a fixed
   list of 23,361 entries, numbered in the order in which their decoding ends;
   a derivation counts the entries decoded so far, so nothing is checked about
   the bits that no reference points to.  The noun of the derivation is
   compared with `root` by the kernel (`s0_eq`), in under a second.
3. **`IsMap` and `+apt:by` differ on one shape.**  `apt_by` proves that the
   pill's `+apt:by` accepts exactly the `IsMap` nouns and the lone nodes
   `[x ~ ~]` with an atom `x`: on a node without bounds and without children
   `+apt:by` never reads its head.
4. **Failure is proved for few arms.**  `(dec 0)`, `+sub` below zero,
   `+dvr`, `+div`, `+mod` by zero and `+snag` past the end crash; nothing is
   proved about gates slammed on nouns of the wrong shape, nor about nouns on
   which a model is `none`.
5. **Not done among the arms of the library:** `+int` of `+by`, and
   `+dif` of `+by` (`+int:in` is done, `Hoon/IntIn.lean`, `+dif:in`,
   `Hoon/DifIn.lean`, `+uni:in`, `Hoon/UniIn.lean`, and `+uni:by`,
   `Hoon/UniBy.lean`).
6. **The arms of `+ca` and `+so` are all done.**  No arm of `+ca` or `+so`
   disagrees with B's model.
7. **The virtualization arms are done** up to formatting: `+mink`, `+monk`,
   `+mack`, `+mook` and `+mock`.  `+scow`, `+smyt`, `+crip` and `+soft` are
   not proved; `mock_pill_iff` assumes what they compute, field by field of
   `Lib`.
8. **The small arms of the analysis are done:** `+inlineable`, `+safe-nomm`,
   `+safe-fol-fol`, `+knit-anno`, `+patch-prod`, `+msg-sock`, `+msg-anno`
   agree with their models on every input.
9. **The interpreter is done in part.**  `+read`, `+store`, `+move`,
   `+eval-pass` and the ops of `+eval-pole` that stay in the function agree
   with `Ska/Interp.lean` on maps of `TreapMap mugF`, and so do the ops that
   leave it (`Compiler/EvalCall.lean`), given the poke and what `+eval-top`
   and `+eval-blob` compute, and what `EvalTop` says, the pill's `+eval-top`
   returns (`eval_top_of_model`) and conversely (`eval_top_iff`), for a poke
   that a model `pk` computes and that keeps layers 2 and 4 (`PokeIs`,
   `CorIn`); `+eval-rend` and `+eval-mook` likewise (`Compiler/EvalRend.lean`).
   Not done: that `+poke` computes `pk` (`PokeModel` of `Assembly.lean`); that
   it keeps the layers is proved (`pokeFrame_corIn`).  The arms that thread the compiler's core read their context
   through its tail `cor`, so after a poke they read the new `cor`: a theorem

     `+callee-compiled` (c), `+laze-big` (f), `+laze-soft-fork` (f),

     `+soft-less`, `+soft-done` (s), and the rest listed below as without
     a model.  The models of `+run`, `+kern`, `+crash-need`, `+soft-fork`
     take `soft` to be empty, which is wrong once a direct call has a
     `soft-call`: a report for track C.
     The model's `need-lazy` is its `sure` alone (`lazyNoun s` is
     `[s ~ ~]`): the forks and bonds of the Hoon's `need-lazy` are left
     out, so `+from` and `+split` are modelled only on `fork = ~` and
     `bond = ~` (the Hoon then runs `+turn`, `spin-split` and
     `+lazy-prune`).  `Need.toNoun`, `Sure.toNoun`, `CGoal.toNoun` agree
     with the Hoon types `need`, `sure`, `goal` (checked against the
     Nock: `%both` `0x68746f62`, `%this` `0x73696874`, `%none`
     `0x656e6f6e`, `%next` `0x7478656e`).  The Hoon's
     state is the 10-tuple `[re-gen bo-gen id-gen mode blocks tags cond
     cache soft sofs]`; the model's `Gen` keeps `re`, `bo`, `id`, `drop`
     (for `mode`, `%drop` or `%run`), `blocks`, `tags`, `cond`, and lacks

     the `soft-*` arms) and `sofs` (a set of `@uxid`): each is a place where
     the model departs from the Hoon, and a link needs the noun of `Gen`
     extended with them as parameters.  Arms with a model: `+re`, `+oo`,
     `+id` (`freshId`), `+dropping` (`drop`), `+set-tag`, `+copy-tag`,
     `+emir`, `+emit`, `+add-ops`, `+bomb`, `+from-sure`, `+from`, `+must`,

10. **`Treap` of `Ska/Analysis.lean` has an instance** (`Compiler/TreapKey.lean`):
   finite maps, with the treap noun made when the key encoding is given.  Its

   shape of the treap does not depend on its values (`toNoun_relabel`).
11. **`fol-loop` against `Ana.folStep` is done one level deep**
   (`Compiler/FolLoopLink.lean`, `Compiler/FolLoopCall.lean`):
   `fol_loop_step_all` gives one level of the trap on every formula, from
   `GoIs` (the calls of the trap to itself compute `go`), `ResIs` (the loop
   `resolve` computes `res` on the identity the trap gives it), `hana` (`ana`
   at axis `511` of the rest of `+run`'s subject) and `RegisterFastIs`;
   `SoftSpotIs` is discharged by `softSpotIs_of` and `CalleePutIs` by
   `calleePutIs` (`fol_loop_step_all'`).  Left:

   (`resolve_step`) and in the direction "the model returns, the pill
   returns" (`resolve_le`), against `resolveStepH` of
   `Compiler/ResolveLoop.lean`, which is `Ana.resolveStep` (`resolveStepH_eq`,
