The plan before this, which changed the structures themselves (kept for the record): they cannot change one at a time, because `RunOKG.toP` turns `RunOKG` into `RunOKP` and `RunOKG.toN` into `RunOKN` by passing the strict hypothesis on, and the `%4`, `%3`, `%7`, cons and `%5` cases of `CompFragM.lean`, `CompBinaryN.lean`, `CompSevenN.lean`, `CompN10N.lean`, `CompConsN.lean` reach `RunOKP` through `RunOKG`; so `RunOKP`, `RunOKG`, `RunOKN` (and then `RunOKE`) change together, each proof of a `sem` that passes the input registers across code it emits needing `FootR.wr` with `steps_frame` in place of the bound `g1.re ≤ r`, and `Clear` and `LeafJ` threaded like `UpBelow` (`OKP` and `NextOK` take them; `runX_ok` already restricts `%pick` and `%next` to jumps without arguments).  The relations, as planned before: the relaxed hypothesis `g1.re ≤ r ∨ NMem nex.laz.sure.ned r` in `RunOKP.sem`, `RunOKN.sem` and `RunOKE.sem` needs `runX_foot` and, in each proof of a `sem` that passes the input registers across emitted code, `FootR.wr` with `steps_frame`.  The semantic frame lemma is done (`CompFrame.lean`, 2026-11-24): `step_frame`, `steps_frame`, `run_frame`, `execEntry_frame`, `steps_reads_frame` (a path through blocks that write only `Q` keeps every register outside `Q`, poison included).  The relaxed `sem` cannot be derived from the present `sem` with it, beside the structure.  The reason is the clause `FillSem` of `AgreeP`: it quantifies over every register file `R` that holds `ned` for `s0`.  The present `sem` accepts only an `AgreeP` whose `ned` lies at or above `g1.re`, and when `ned` overlaps the sure need at a register `d` below `g1.re`, the fill ops `A` read `d` (in the collapse case they are the `%mov d r'`), so no `ned` above `g1.re` makes `FillSem` of those `A` hold: `R` says nothing about `d`.  Changing the program instead (copying `d` to a fresh register and renaming the fill ops) needs a simulation of whole runs between two programs, and it fails because `GoalSem` includes everything after the goal's jump, where an arbitrary program may write `d` and jump back into the lazy blocks.  So the relaxed hypothesis must go into the structures (`RunOKP.sem`, `RunOKN.sem`, `RunOKE.sem`) and each proof of a `sem` must be changed where it shows that the input registers are kept across code it emits: the old argument (the written register is below `g1.re`, the input registers at or above it) becomes the written register is below `g1.re` and not in the result's sure need, which is `Foot.wr` (and, for the lazy blocks reached, `steps_frame` with `Q` the footprint).  That needs `Foot` for every case the relations cover, so `%10` and `FragX` come first.  Not yet: (i5) for runs that make proxies (`+kern`, `+collapse-lazy-atom` under a goal with entries), where `cond` changes and `+sect` threads proxies, so `KeepR.cnd` must give way to `CondFresh` and the definitions in `cond` must be shown below `re`.  Tested on the real compiler (`tools/hoon/selfkeep.py`, check i5, 2026-11-24): every register a new or changed block of a run mentions (parameters, ops, the terminator with its jump arguments) is below `re` at the end of the run, on the same formulas (5860 lines, each case's sequence twice, 704 with a proxy), no failure.  With `CondFresh` from the run's end to the final state this says that unproxying by the final `cond` is unproxying by the run's own `cond` on those blocks (`unproxy_condFresh`); the ops a later run puts in front of the run's lazy blocks are what `AgreeU`'s lazy-block clause takes unproxied.  Left for the runs: `AgreeU` in the `sem` of a relation that makes proxies and `CondFresh` in place of `cnd`; the present relations keep `AgreeP`, since `AgreeU` with a non-empty `cond` would need every block of a run without proxies to mention no proxy register, an invariant (the registers of the blocks a run emits are below its end, except the fill ops of the goal's entries) that nothing proves yet.  The `+collapse-lazy-atom` case needs a second change, and the earlier statement of it here was wrong about where `d` lies.  `FillSem` and `FrontSem` fill a lazy block from the input registers `ned` only, while the unproxied `%mov d r'` reads `d`.  In `[7 [4 [0 3]] [6 [5 [0 1] [1 6]] [0 1] [1 5]]]` (raw blocks of `tools/hoon/proxy-dump.hoon`, case `%h`, rerun 2026-11-24), `d` is raw `v3`, Gen register 2, the `rp` of the `%5` inside `q`: `q` allocates Gen 0 to 3 (`imm 5`, `don`, `rp`, `rq`), `p` then the proxy (Gen 4, gone after unproxying) and `arg` (Gen 5).  So `d` is a register of `q`'s own sure need, allocated in `q`'s run and below its end, and `collapseLazyAtomL` takes it as `r` because `q`'s sure need is `this d`.  A write bound of the form "a run writes only registers allocated in it and those of its goal's need" therefore does not keep `d`.  What keeps it is that a run's code does not write the registers of its own result's sure need (here the code of `q` is `imm 6 v4` and `eqq v3 v4`).  The route that fits: widen the input need of `q`'s `sem` to `pair ned sur` holding `[s0 s]`, with `sur` the sure need of `q`'s result.  The fill ops of the final pass at the other lazy blocks meet the widened `FillSem` (`FillSem.pair`, since `sur` lies below `q`'s end); the earlier objection to this route was wrong.  What blocks it is the hypothesis `∀ r, NMem ned r → g1.re ≤ r` of `RunOKP.sem`, `RunOKN.sem` and `RunOKE.sem`.  So the change is to relax it to `g1.re ≤ r ∨ NMem nex.laz.sure.ned r` and to prove, case by case, that the ops a run emits do not write its own sure-need registers.  Tested on the real compiler (`tools/hoon/selfkeep.py`, 2026-11-24): over the 74 formulas of `thread-a.hoon` and the two of `proxy-dump.hoon`, 2930 runs of `run` in `%run` mode, 352 of which make a proxy, the ops a run emits (new blocks, ops put in front of old blocks, parameters written by new jumps) write no register of its result's sure need (974 runs have both a non-empty sure need and writes) nor of its lazy entries' needs, no register of a need of the result is a key of `cond` (834 runs have both a non-empty sure need and a non-empty `cond`), and the sure need and the entries' needs share no register; no failure.  Done for the fill ops (`CompUnproxyRegs.lean`, `CompUnproxyFill.lean`): `unproxy_ops`, `kernLoop_regs`, `sureLoop_regs`, `coerceLazy_regs`, `coerceLazyL_regs`.  Left for the collapse case, in this order: relax `∀ r, NMem ned r → g1.re ≤ r` in `RunOKP.sem`, `RunOKN.sem` and `RunOKE.sem` to `g1.re ≤ r ∨ NMem nex.laz.sure.ned r` and prove fact (i) case by case (fact (iv) gives the disjointness the widened need asks); then `RunOKE` (or a `U` variant of it) for `[4 p]` and `[3 p]` under a goal with entries through `+collapse-lazy-atom` (`walkLazy_ok` with a property that allows a proxy, `proxy_condFresh`); then `runOKG_sevenE` with `q`'s `sem` taken at `pair ned sur` holding `[s0 s]` and `FillSem.pair` for the other lazy blocks, ending in `RunOKU` of `[7 [4 [0 3]] [6 [5 [0 1] [1 6]] [0 1] [1 5]]]`.  Done since (`CompCollapsePre.lean`, `CompCollapseRun.lean`, 2026-11-24, second session): `hsq` is removed.  `collapsePre_spec` describes the first half of the `%4` case for every shape that `+sure-require-look` gives the sure need (`this d`, `none`, `pair`, `both`, and a need equivalent to `none`, where `+collapse-lazy-atom` returns `~`), checked against raw blocks (`[7 [4 [0 3]] [0 2]]`: the increment hops to a block `[%bom ~]`; `[7 [4 [0 3]] [1 5]]`: it writes a fresh register; `[7 [4 [0 3]] [6 [5 [0 1] [1 6]] [0 1] [0 2]]]`: the lazy block of the branch `[0 2]` gets `[%cel v3] [%imm 0 v1]`).  `CollRunU` changed: it gained `dreg` (the register `d` the increment writes is in the goal's sure need or allocated in the run), `dkey` (`d` is no key of the final `cond`), `dich` (every atom fits the goal's sure need, or none does), `dhold` (in the first case `d` holding an atom holds the sure need) and `semT` (the relaxed `sem` for the goal `this d`, which says that the product is left in `d`).  `collRun_n4` changed: it no longer takes `hs`, `hl`, `hdre`, `hd0` (the sure need `this d` with looks at axis `1`), but `hnr`, `hnc`, `hen` (the registers of the sure need and of the entries' needs are below the start, the first no keys of `cond`), and it returns `∃ d, CollRunU …`.  `runOKU_seven4` and `finishLF_bellContract74U` no longer take `hsq`: when no atom fits `q`'s sure need the code crashes at the `[%bom ~]` block and `q` crashes on the atom (`nec`), otherwise `q`'s `sem` is taken at `pair ned (this d)` as before, `d` being admitted by `InR` as a register of `q`'s sure need or as one allocated after `q`'s run.  `AboveX` changed: it assumes what `collRun_n4` has at the start of `p`'s run (a jump without arguments below the start, no drop, `UpBelow`, `CondBelow`, `a` no key of `cond`, at most 255 `%6`), `aboveX_n1` gives it for every `[1 k]` and `aboveX_n4` for `[4 p]` from `p`.  So `finishLF_bellContract74U` covers `[7 [4 p] q]` with `q` in `FragX`, `p` a chain `[4 [4 ... x]]` of zero or more `%4` over `x = [0 axe]` with `2 ≤ axe` or `x = [1 k]`, at most 255 `%6`, nothing baked and a cape with no `[| |]`, with no other hypothesis; for example the target (`finishLF_bellContract_target`) and `[7 [4 [4 [0 6]]] [6 [0 1] [0 1] [1 0]]]`.  Not covered: `[7 [4 [5 [0 2] [0 3]]] [0 1]]` (`AboveX` of `%5` is not proved), `[7 [3 [0 3]] q]` (`%3`), and any formula with `[7 [4 p] q]` under another parent, such as `[6 [0 1] [7 [4 [0 3]] [0 1]] [1 0]]`. So `finishLF_bellContract74U` covers `[7 [4 p] q]` and `finishLF_bellContract73U` covers `[7 [3 p] q]` with `q` in `FragX`, `p` a chain of zero or more `%3` and `%4` over `[0 axe]` with `1 ≤ axe` or over `[1 k]`, at most 255 `%6`, nothing baked and a cape with no `[| |]`; `finishLF_bellContract73U` also takes `hq3`, that the run of `q` for `%done` returns a lazy need not equivalent to `none` (when it is, `[3 p]` compiles as `p` for the goal with entries, which needs `p` under such a goal: only `%0`, `[1 k]`, `%4` and `%3` are proved there).  Covered: `[7 [3 [0 3]] [0 1]]` (`finishLF_bellContract_three`, `hq3` by `runL_n01_done_ne`) and `[7 [4 [3 [4 [0 6]]]] q]` for every `q` of `FragX` (an `example` for `[7 [4 [3 [4 [0 6]]]] [6 [0 1] [0 1] [1 0]]]`); not covered: `[7 [3 [0 3]] [6 [5 [0 1] [1 6]] [0 1] [1 5]]]` (on the real compiler its `q` returns the sure need `this v3`, so it meets `hq3`, but the proof needs the run of the `%6` computed), `[7 [4 [5 [0 2] [0 3]]] [0 1]]` (`AboveX` of `%5`).  Done since (`CompAboveRun.lean`, `CompAboveX.lean`, 2026-11-24, fourth session): `AboveX` over all of `FragX`.  The lower bound is `AboveIn m lo g` (every block of `g` at a label in `[lo, g.bo)` mentions only registers from `m` on) with the invariant `KeepsA m` (every `AboveIn m lo` kept, `re` and `bo` grown, `drop` kept), under the hypotheses that the registers of the goal (its need and the arguments of its jumps, `GoalA m`) are at least `m` and that `m ≤ re`; the registers of the result are then at least `m` too (`NextA`, `NextAL`).  It holds of every case of `run`, because every register that an emitted block or a put op mentions is a register of the goal's need (`+mede`, `+split`, `+into`, `+copy`, the `%mov` and `crashNeed` of `+collapse-lazy-atom`), an argument of the goal's jumps (the `%brn`, `%eqq`, `%clq` under `%pick`) or a fresh register, which is at least `re` and so at least `m`, while the `%bom` blocks (of `[0 0]`, of cons under `%pick`, of `+collapse-lazy-atom`) mention none; and since the run does not drop, every block counted in `[lo, bo)` is one that `emit` put there, so `Clear` is not needed.  `run_keepsA` is `run_keeps` helper by helper with the bound turned around, and `runX_keepsA` is `runX_keeps` case by case, the `%6` cases of `runL` included (`keepXA_sixP`, and `keepXA_sixN`, where the three blocks of `+fork-sure` mention the fresh copies `s0`, `s1` and the goal's need), taking `KeepX` of the parts for the labels of the entries, their number and `cond`.  `aboveX_of_fragX` gives `AboveX p` for every `p` of `FragX`, so `finishLF_bellContract74X` covers `[7 [4 p] q]` and `finishLF_bellContract73X` covers `[7 [3 p] q]` for every `p` and `q` of `FragX`, at most 255 `%6`, nothing baked and a cape with no `[| |]`, the second still given `hq3`. Not covered: `[7 [3 [0 3]] [6 [1 0] [1 5] [1 6]]]`, whose `q` returns, in the model, a lazy need equivalent to `none` (the test `[1 0]` for `%pick` needs nothing and so do the branches), so `hq3` fails and `[3 [0 3]]` compiles as `[0 3]` for the goal with entries; and any `[7 [4 p] q]` under another parent, such as `[6 [0 1] [7 [4 [0 3]] [0 1]] [1 0]]`.  Left for the collapse case, in this order: the none-equivalent branch of `[3 p]`, which needs the run of `p` under a goal with entries whose lazy need is equivalent to `none` (`runOKE_n0`, `runOKE_n1R`, `collRun_n4`, and `[3 p']` again by the same branch) composed with a lemma that the code of `q` then computes the same for every subject (its `sem` holds for any value, the fill ops of its entries being empty by `collP_noneEq`), so that `pureEval q (pureEval p s)` and `pureEval q (pureEval [3 p] s)` agree when `p` returns; `[7 [5 p1 p2] q]` (the first half as for `%3`, then `binaryN_semR` under `this d`); then the composable form, `[7 [4 p] q]` and `[7 [3 p] q]` under `%6`, cons and the other parents, whose induction needs for a run with entries a relation shaped like `RunOKE` (each entry of the result paired with the ops put in front of it, `FrontER` under `InR`) with `ProxyFor` or `CondFresh` in place of an unchanged `cond`, its `sem` against `AgreeU` by any later `cond` that agrees with the run's below its end (as `CollRunU.sem` takes `cf`), and (i5) and `AboveIn` carried beside it; the strict `KeepR.cnd` and `FootR`, and `sect_spec` (`+sect` changes no block when the branches keep `cond`), must then allow `cond` to grow by proxies.  For the linking track: no look set the model makes holds the axis `0`.  `+from-sure` makes looks `axe` (never `0`: `fromSure 0` is `none`) and `pegF axe x` of old looks (`fromSure_lok_ne0`), `+split-sure` crashes on a look at `0` and makes `mas` of looks from `4` on (`splitLok_ne0`), `+into-sure` crashes on one (`gep` of `0` is `none`) and makes old looks, `gep` results (`lowB ||| 2^k`, not `0`) and `axe ≥ 2`, and `+copy` and `+fork-sure` take unions and copies.  So the model's `pegF axe 0 = 0` is never reached, but the invariant over every run is not proved (an induction over `runL` with the look sets of goals and results), and the model's `fromSure` would follow the Hoon exactly with `none` when `0` is in `sur.lok`.  Second, for `+collapse-lazy-atom` under a `%done` parent (`[7 [4 [0 3]] [6 [5 [0 1] [1 6]] [0 1] [1 5]]]`) the proxy is read only by the `%mov` that `collapseGate` puts in the lazy block, and its definition is the `%4`'s register, which every path to that block defines, so no guard is needed there; the guard is needed only for the join parameters that `+sect` threads, where it is a `%brz` on the outer test register that `+fork` puts in front of each lazy block (`Guarded` with the edge from the `%brn` of the `%6` to the side that made the proxy). A proxy needs a `%4` or `%3` (or `%5`) run for a goal whose lazy need has entries, which is the run of `p` in `[7 p q]` with a `%6` in `q`.  `runL` of `[7 p q]` runs `q` and then `p` for the goal `q` returns, and `runL` of `[4 p]` and `[3 p]` ends with the run of `p` for every goal, so `[7 a [7 b c]]` runs and computes as `[7 [7 a b] c]` and `[7 a [4 p]]` as `[4 [7 a p]]` (`SameL`, a congruence for `%7`, `%4`, `%3`), and the final pass reads the nomm only through its run and its product (`RunOKU.congr`). For that relation: the ops the collapse puts at the goal's entries read the register `d` the `%4` writes (`CollP d`), not only the input registers `ned`, so the front clause of a run with entries that makes proxies cannot be `FrontSemR` from `ned` alone; `runOKU_seven4` meets it by taking `q`'s `sem` at `pair ned (this d)` holding `[s0 v]` with `semT` (the code leaves the product in `d`). For a tail that makes proxies, or a `%next` goal with registers below keys of `cond` (a cons inside a cons, where the inner goal's registers come from the outer `+split` before the outer tail's proxies), `hkm` fails, and the conversion of a run of `FragX` under `%next` to `RunOKNV` needs the invariant that its blocks mention only the goal's registers and registers allocated in the run: `AboveIn` with a predicate that holds of the goal's registers and from the start's `re` on in place of `m ≤ ·`, by the induction of `CompAboveRun.lean` and `CompAboveX.lean`, every case of which already argues this way (the goal's need, its jumps' arguments, or fresh). Baking with `hbake`: `+bake-need` not the identity, so the entry need is the baked one and the constants it fixes must be the subject's.  `SitesMeet` of `BakeImp.lean` was to give that and is false; `+bake-graph` before commit 8f2162e miscompiled (`VariantCollision.lean`). Done since (2026-11-24, `OptAliasPlain.lean`, `OptAliasPlainDriver.lean`, `OptAliasFresh.lean`): the witness was checked on the real `+optimize` (with the arguments `41` and `0` the code before returns `42`, the code after `0`), but `+comp` was not seen to reach it (9,600 targeted formulas agree with `+mock`, and over the 553 compiles of `ssacheck.hoon`, at every pass of every round, a register with a fill and another definition is always the one `+crash-need` tests and is never read outside the fill's block); the driver now carries plain single assignment on the reachable blocks, **`DInvR`** = `DInvT`, `DefFirst`, `NoLti` and `DefOnceR` (every register has at most one definition, dead fills included, in the blocks reachable from `0`), which implies `DInvS` and `FillFresh`, every pass of the loop keeps (`hops_onceR`, `rub_onceR`, `mid_onceR`, `alias_onceR`, `trim_onceR`, `dead_onceR`), and which holds at every pass of every round on the corpus of `ssacheck.hoon` once `+crash-need` stops filling the register it tests (the change of the Hoon, applied in commit `af5cc39`; the fill sat after a `%cel` of a known atom and never ran).  So **`optimize_keeps_plain_pickR`**: `+optimize` with the passes of the Hoon (the model of `+alias` with `pickR`, the real `+trim-trace-hints`, the model of `+switch-chains`) keeps the code on blocks with `DInvR`, with no other hypothesis (`optimize_keeps_plain` for any `pick` that picks a member of its list).  The same holds under the weaker `DInvF` = `DInvS` and `FillFresh` (`OptAliasFresh.lean`, **`optimize_keeps_fresh_pickR`**: every pass keeps `FillFresh` too), which `DInvR` implies; `DInvR` is the one to prove of `+comp`, since it says nothing about dead fills and the corpus has it with the change.  `optimize_keeps_plain_pickR` replaces `optimize_keeps_aliasO`, which is kept with its false hypothesis; the witness blocks violate `DInvR` and `FillFresh` (`not_dInvR_v`, `not_dInvR_w`, `not_fillFresh_v`, `not_fillFresh_w`, `not_dInvF_v`, `not_dInvF_w`).  What raw `+comp` output owes the optimizer is **`OptIn`** (`optIn_iff`): `ParNodup` (distinct parameters per block), `PassOK` (no argument register of a jump is a parameter of its target, the check of `+eval-pass`), `Acyclic` (a rank falls along every jump), `DownPar` (no argument register of a jump is a parameter of a block reachable from its target), `TrimBr` (a stack of open trace frames at each reachable block, empty at `0`, that the body takes to the stack of each target), `DefFirst` (on every path of blocks from `0` a register with a definition is read only after one: a parameter, an earlier op, an earlier block of the path), `NoLti` (no `%lti` end) and `DefOnceR` (at most one definition per register in the reachable blocks), and `EntryOK` on each entry; before the change of the Hoon (commit `af5cc39`), `DefOnceR` and `FillFresh` both failed on raw output (the fill of `+crash-need` wrote the register it tests).
   9. **Calls: the design** (2026-11-24, eleventh session, `CompCall.lean`, `tools/hoon/calldump.hoon`).  The case `%2` of `+run` (lines 3900 to 4051 of `nock-compilation.hoon`): an indirect call is `+simple-next`, `+kerf` (a hop block and the product `pro` from `+kern`), a block `[%nok r-sub r-fol pro]` hopping to the `+kerf` block, then `q` for `this r-fol`, `+past`, `p` for `this r-sub`, `+copy`, never a tail call.  A direct call reads the callee's need (`+callee-need`: a hot jet's, `map-local` or `top` in the SCC, else the n-ary cache or a compile of the callee's SCC, which extends `cache`), allocates its registers (`+need-ord-alloc-regs`), and emits under `%done` a tail call `[%jmp b sub-v]`, otherwise `[%cal b sub-v pro]` into the block of `+kerf`; then `q` (skipped when `+safe-fol-fol`, as when the analysis made it `[1 battery]`), `+past`, `p` for the callee's need, `+copy`.  A pure callee whose product nothing needs is not called (a callee with a `%6`, or in a recursive SCC, is never pure).  Memo keys give `%cam`, `%csm`; a ring gives `%caf`, `%jmf`; `mono` (only the whole-subject version, `+compile-unary`) gives an optimistic call and a pessimistic one on the whole subject joined by `+mono-try-call`; `sof` (the need is not sound for `made p sup` and soft or hinted) records a soft call (`+soft-new`), whose need is bound at three blocks before the call (`+soft-bond`) and taken apart there after `+coerce-lazy` (`+soft-done`, `+soft-emit`).  The n-ary versions compile the nomm of `+inlined`, which inlines a callee alone in its SCC, not recursive, not hot, with no memo key, of at most 48 nodes: the raw blocks need a recursive callee to show a call.  Raw blocks (subject unknown, the recursive `R = [6 [3 [0 3]] [9 2 [10 [3 [0 7]] [0 1]]] [0 3]]`, need `[~ this]`): `[8 [1 R] [9 2 [0 1]]]` is `mov v0 v1` then `jmp R [v1]`; `[4 [8 [1 R] [9 2 [0 1]]]]` is `cal R [v3] v4`, `mov v4 v2`, `inc v2 v1, don v1`; for `%pick` `cal R [v3] v5`, `mov v5 v4`, `brn v4`; the indirect `[2 [0 3] [0 2]]` is `nok v4 v5 v3`, `mov v3 v2`, `don v2`.  The model: the state needs `soft`, `sofs` (`GenC`) only for soft calls; what the case reads of `cache` and the code table is `CallEnv`, functions of the callee (a compiled callee's need does not change within a poke).  `callL` models the indirect call, the dropped call and the direct call for `mono` `|`, no memo key, no ring, `sof` false, with the runs of `p` and `q` as parameters; it is exact there (the call block is fresh, so no `+soft-bond`, and `+kern` meets no soft block, as `kern` with `noSoft` assumes), and four `#guard` lines check it against the raw blocks (indirect for `%done`, tail call, `%next`, `%pick`; the registers are the raw ones less the number of arguments).  Done (twelfth session): `runLC` (`CompRunLC.lean`, generated by `tools/gen/runlc.py`), with `env.cod` for `+past` (a call of an impure callee is hinted: in `[[call] [0 1]]` the tail's need is bound behind the call, `0w3 mov v0 v3` after `0w5 cal`) and `sup` threaded as the Hoon does; `runLC_eq` (without `%2` it is `runL`, so the links and theorems about `runL` stay valid); nine `#guard` checks of `finishLFC` against whole functions (`CompRunLCEq.lean`), all equal to the raw blocks.  `runLC` is over `Gen`, not `GenC`: `callL` is (sof false never touches `soft`), so `GenC` waits for the soft calls.  The call blocks: `goJ_cal`, `callBlockSem`, `goJ_jmp`, `goJ_nok`, `allocSplit` (`CompCallSem.lean`); one call against the oracle: `callStep_fwd`, `tailStep_fwd` (`CompCallStep.lean`).  Two corrections to `SemC` (twelfth session, `CompCallStep.lean`).  First, the fuel: `SemC` takes `nommF` with fuel `n` and `OracleFwd` with the same `n`, but a callee's nomm may take fewer steps than `+mink` on its formula (`Annot.fold` makes a computation `[1 v]`), so the callee's `+mink` fuel is not below `n`; `+mink` of `[2 b c]` with fuel `j + 1` runs the callee with fuel `j`, so the relation must be indexed by `+mink`'s fuel on the caller's formula with `Annot` (`SemCM`).  Second, `GoalSemC` compares a crash with `Tone.two t` of the interpreter's trace, which `ToneRel` does not relate to itself when it holds a `%meaf` frame: `GoalSemCM` takes `+mink`'s trace `tm`.  The joint induction over a fragment with calls (the analogue of `UOK`, not written yet) needs per nomm and goal: the structural clauses of `UOK` as they are (for a part without `%2` they are `runL`'s through `runLC_eq`; for a call they come from `callL`'s blocks, which are fresh, and the runs of `p` and `q`), and the clause `SemCM` instead of the `sem` of `RunOKV`.  A pure part gets `SemCM` from its existing relation through `goalSemC_of_goalSem` (the state stays, `toneOf tm v`) and `nomm_bwd` (its `pureEval` is `+mink`'s product); a parent (cons, `%6`, `%7`, `%10`) composes `SemCM` of its parts with the state moving between them, which needs `AgreeU` at every state and `frames_mono` for the trace handed on; a direct call composes `SemCM` of `p` (the subject held as the callee's need) with `callStep_fwd` or `tailStep_fwd`, `Annot.direct` giving `Fits b.less` and `b.fol` from `q`.  Left for the first call theorem: the run of `p` must put the callee's subject in the registers of `+need-ord-alloc-regs` (the `%next` goal `lazy-from-need sub-ned`, a `RunOKV` of `p` for that goal), the product register `pro` and the `+kerf` block at `out` must meet the caller's goal (as `kerf` does for `%4`), and the `q` of `+safe-fol-fol` must be shown not to run.  Features of calls not modelled, with what a first end-to-end theorem about a program with one call needs: (a) `+inlined` (line 3298): a pass before `+comp`, whose output carries `Annot.inline`; not needed in the model of `+comp`, which takes the nomm it receives. (b) Memo calls (`%cam`, `%csm`, lines 3973 to 3980; `b-ned` is `this` for a persistent key, line 3948): only under a `%memo` hint; not needed.    10. **The whole-subject version** (2026-11-24, thirteenth session, `CompWhole.lean`, `CompWholeKern.lean`, `CompWholeTop.lean`, `tools/hoon/wholedump.hoon`).  `+poke` answers `%full` with `+compiled-mono` (line 9683, after `+optimize`), `%dire b &` with `+compiled-mono` and `%dire b |` with `+compiled-nary` (lines 9694, 9695).  `+compiled-mono` is `+compile-unary` (line 3390): `run` with `mono` `&` for `[%done ~]`, then `+kerf` (a new hop block to the run's entry, and `+kern` of the run's lazy need from one fresh register `sub`, through proxies at the lazy blocks other than the new one), then `+to-straight` of `[%next [[this+sub ~] ~ ~] ~ o]` at the new block.    11. **The skeleton from `CompHyp` to this track** (2026-11-24, fourteenth session, `CompHypOf.lean`).  `gap_of_comp` needs `CompHyp`, and `compHyp_of_pieces` derives it from `CompPieces`, so every theorem of this track should discharge or refine a field of `CompPieces`.  The reference compiler `ref long jets hot b m` is the straight `%dire b m` returns before `+optimize`: for `m` true the code of `+compile-unary` on the code table's nomm (`finishLUC`), for `m` false the final pass of `member` on the nomm of `+inlined` (`finishLFC` with the shape and the bake as parameters). The need and the argument count of the whole-subject version are proved for every nomm (`finishLUC_this`).  So `compHyp_of_frag` derives `CompHyp` from `CompRest` on the fragment.    12. **The first call theorem** (2026-11-24, fifteenth session, `CompIndirectRun.lean`, `CompIndirectTop.lean`, `CompIndirectFwd.lean`, `CompIndirectBwd.lean`).  Proved: `indirectTop_contract`, the whole-subject code (`finishLUC env`) of the nomm `[2 p q]` (no callee) for the formula `[2 b c]` (`Annot.indirect`, given as `bl.fol = [2 b c]` with `Annot` of `b` by `p` and of `c` by `q` on the subjects that fit the bell), with `p`, `q` in `FragU` and `sixCount p + sixCount q ≤ 255`, has the need `this` and meets `BellContract lib N bl true` against any needs `N` (`indirectTop_fwd`, `indirectTop_bwd`). The part after the `%nok` block is the tail of a cons without the block of `+split`, so `binaryNV_ok` composes the two runs (`indirect_mid`: `RunOKNV` of `[p q]` for `%next [[rSub rFol] ~]` into the `%nok` block), which answers the question of 12.11 on the register of `p`'s product: the cons's relation keeps it across `q`'s code (`K` holds the registers below the state). The entry is `finishLU_compDone` with the run's `sem` abstracted to a property `Φ` of the code from the run's jump (`finishTop_gen`, `indirectTop_gen`), and `Φ` is the forward (`fwdPhi`) or backward (`bwdPhi`) contract: `goJ_nok`, `OracleFwd.nok` at fuel `n - 1` (`minkF_n2_pure`), `minkF_trace` for the caller's trace under a crash's, `Forall2.append` with `frames_mono`.  No `SemCM` is needed for this shape.  `IndirectTopFwd` of `CompIndirect.lean` (every `Annot` of the nomm) is false as stated: `Annot.bare` (and `Annot.bareLit`) give the nomm `[2 [0 1] [1 f]]` for `f = [11 %bare g]`, whose code makes one `nok` of `f` itself, which `+mink` runs with all `n` of its fuel, outside `OracleFwd`'s bound `j < n` (an oracle that answers wrongly exactly there meets `OracleFwd n`).  So `RawMono` holds on `FragUM` still, and a class with indirect calls needs, besides excluding `q = [1 f]` with `f` bare, the inversion of `Annot` on `[2 p q]` through the wrappers `Annot.memo`, `Annot.six0`, `Annot.six1` (the formula is `[2 b c]` under hints and constant `%6`, where `+mink` spends fuel before the call, so `OracleFwd` still covers it) with `minkF_n2_pure` and `mink_n2_of` lifted through them.  Left after that: the indirect call under the parents of `FragU` (a constructor of a joint induction whose `sem` is a `Φ` like `fwdPhi`, `bwdPhi` at a `%next` goal).  Done in the sixteenth session (12.13): the inversion through `Annot.memo`, `Annot.six0`, `Annot.six1` (`minkN2_of_annot`), the first call theorem on every such `Annot` (`indirectTop_contractA`), and `RawMono` on `FragUCM`.
   13. **`RawMono` with indirect calls** (2026-11-24, sixteenth session, `CompIndirectWrap.lean`, `CompIndirectGen.lean`, `CompIndirectWrapTop.lean`, `CompHypOfCall.lean`).  Proved: `rawMono_fragUC`, the raw whole-subject contract (field (b) of `CompPieces`) on `FragUCM`, the union of `FragUM` and `IndUM` (the nomm `[2 p q]` with no callee, `p` and `q` in `FragU`, at most 255 `%6`, and `q` not the literal `[1 f]` of a `%bare` formula, `bareLitQ q = false`), and `compHyp_of_fragC` (`CompHyp` from `CompRest` with that class).  The constructors of `Annot` that give a nomm `[2 p q]` with no callee are `indirect`, `bare`, `bareLit` and the wrappers `memo`, `six0`, `six1` (whose nomm is their premise's); every other one gives another head or a callee, which `minkN2_of_annot` checks case by case.  `bareLitQ q = false` removes `bare` and `bareLit` (and, more than needed, the formula `[2 b [1 f]]` with `f` bare).  What the proofs use of the formula is `MinkN2` (`+mink` on it runs the product formula with fuel below `n`, its runs are `+mink`'s, its crash is the parts'), which holds of `[2 b c]` (`minkN2_indirect`) and goes through `%memo` (`[11 [%memo h] f]` with the nomm of `[2 [0 1] [1 f]]`: the clue returns, then `+mink` runs `f` with one less fuel, `mink_call1`) and constant `%6` (the test returns `0` or `1`, one less fuel), so `OracleFwd n` covers the call.  For example `[6 [1 0] [2 [0 3] [0 2]] [0 0]]` has the nomm `[2 [0 3] [0 2]]` and is covered.  The `%bare` case: `Annot.bare` with `f` the whole bell formula gives the code one `nok` of `f` itself, and `+mink` on `f = [11 %bare g]` with fuel `n` runs `g` with `n - 1`, so the oracle would have to answer `+mink` on `f` with fuel `n`, outside `j < n`; under a parent (a cons, `%memo`, `%6`, any formula of which `f` is a strict part) `+mink` spends one step first and `j = n - 1` is enough.  So covering `%bare` needs `RawMono` to see the formula (the class is a predicate on the nomm, and the nomm `[2 [0 1] [1 f]]` is also the `Annot.indirect` of `[2 [0 1] [1 f]]`), with either a proof that no bell whose formula is itself bare is compiled for the whole-subject version (`topBody` runs a bare formula with `+mink` and never pokes `%full` with it) or an `OracleFwd` whose `nok` covers `j ≤ n` on a bare formula.  The indirect call under a parent (`CompIndirectNext.lean`): at a goal `%next laz th` the run is the top's with `+simple-next` the identity and the `+kerf` block `out` hopping to `th` after `+kern`'s ops on `pro`; at `%pick` `+simple-next` adds a `brn` block.  The relation for code that calls is `GoalSemCM`, not a `Φ` as at the top, because under a parent the code goes on at the goal's jump, which the parent's later parts own, and the parent needs the state and the registers there (`GoesC`), which `GoalSemCM` gives, with the pure parts lifted at the same state by `goalSemC_of_goalSem`.  Proved: `indirect_semG` (and `indirect_semN` for `%next`): from the `GoalSem` of the run of `[p q]` into the `%nok` block (what `indirect_mid`'s `RunOKNV` gives at any state), the block itself, and `OutGoal` of the block `out` (it meets `GoalSemCM` at `Tone.zero p` for a product in `pro`, at every state), the code from the run's jump meets `GoalSemCM` for `+mink`'s tone on any formula with `MinkN2`; `goalSemCM_step` moves `GoalSemCM` back over a step that grows the needs and keeps `K` (the shape of every composition of a call with what follows it).  Left: `OutGoal` of the `+kerf` block from `kern_spec` (for the block `o` itself `+kern` uses `pro` with no proxy, so `KernP pro` at `out` is the taking apart of `pro` into `laz`'s registers), the prefix lemma for a `%next` and a `%pick` goal (the analogue of `indirect_prefix` from any state with `CondBelow` rather than `gen0`), the parents (a cons with the call in its head runs the tail's code after the call, so the tail's `SemNV`, which holds at every state, is used at the call's new state, and `AgreeU` must hold at every state as `SemCM` asks), the joint induction (the analogue of `UOK` with `SemCM` as its `sem`), and an end-to-end example such as `[[2 [0 3] [0 2]] [0 1]]`.  Seventeenth session (12.14): `OutGoal` of the `+kerf` block under a fork-free `%next` (`outGoal_kerf`), for `%done` and `%pick` (`outGoal_done`, `outGoal_pick`) and the prefix lemma from any state (`indirect_prefixN`) are done, and with them the run relation of the call under each goal (`indirect_runN`, `indirect_runD`, `indirect_runP`).  The parents, the joint induction and the end-to-end example are left.  Eighteenth session (12.15): the cons with the call in its head is composed and `[[2 [0 3] [0 2]] [0 1]]` is proved end to end, forward half only.
   14. **The indirect call under the goals of a parent** (2026-11-24, seventeenth session, `CompIndirectOut.lean`, `CompCallParent.lean`).  Proved: `indirect_runS` (and its corollary `indirect_runN`): the run of `[2 p q]` with `p`, `q` meeting `UOK`, at most 255 `%6` and a formula with `MinkN2`, under a fork-free `%next sur th` with `th` argless, from any state with the hypotheses of `OKNV` (`GJ`, `GoalOK`, `1 ≤ bo`, no drop, `UpBelow`, `Clear`, `CondBelow`, no register of `sur` a key of `cond`), meets `RunOKNC`: every structural field of `RunOKNV`, `nec` on the pure pair `[p q]` (a subject that does not fit the need makes `[p q]` crash, so `+mink` crashes by `MinkN2`), and `SemNC`, which is `SemNV` with `GoalSemCM` against an oracle with `OracleSteps` and `OracleFwd n` for `+mink`'s tone with fuel `n` on the formula, keeping `(x < g0.re ∨ NMem ned x) ∧ ¬ NMem sur.ned x` as `SemNV` does.  `indirect_runD` and `indirect_runP` do the same under `%done` and `%pick z o`: the block of `+simple-next` (`[don re]` or `[brn re z o]` at `bo`) is kept to the end, and the rest of the run meets `RunOKNC` under `%next (lazy-from-reg re)` into it from the state after that block.  `outGoal_done` and `outGoal_pick` turn `OutGoal` for that `%next` into `OutGoal` for `%done` and `%pick`.  The pieces: `kerf_ofSure` (under a fork-free `%next` the lazy need has no entries, so `+kern` walks the block `out` only, with `pro` itself as its source and no proxy, and the block is the ops of `+kern-need` then `hop th`), `outGoal_kerf` (the block meets `OutGoal` in every program whose block at `out` is it unproxied by a `cond` without its registers as keys), `indirect_prefixN` (from any state the blocks before the runs grow the state with `GrowU` and keep `cond`).  `KernSemS` keeps only the registers below the ops' fresh ones, while a parent needs the registers of `ned` kept too, and those lie above every register of the run: in the raw blocks of `[[2 [0 3] [0 2]] [0 1]]` the tail's block `0w3 mov v0 v3` reads the subject register `v0` after the call's blocks `0w5 nok v5 v6 v4` and `0w4 mov v4 v2`.  `runs_keep` gives that: ops of `+kern-need` (`KOp`: moves, `cel`, `hed`, `tal`, `kernLoop_kop`) keep every register they do not write, read through a `%don` after the ops because `Runs` is an equation of the interpreter.  Raw blocks: `CompCallParent.lean` checks `finishLUC` by `#guard` against `tools/hoon/wholedump.hoon` with the cases `[[2 [0 3] [0 2]] [0 1]]`, `[[0 1] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]` and `[7 [2 [0 3] [0 2]] [0 1]]` on the subject `[[4 0 1] 5]` (the compiler's products `[6 [4 0 1] 5]`, `[[[4 0 1] 5] 6]` and `6`), all equal.  `[7 [0 1] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]` is the call alone after the analysis.  Left, in order.  First, the cons with a call in its head under `%done` (`binaryGV_ok` with the head's `RunOKNV` replaced by `RunOKNC`): the copy block as there, then the head's `GoalSemCM` gives a state `st'` and registers keeping the cons's `K`, then the tail's `SemNV` at `st'` (so the `AgreeU` the cons takes must hold at every state, which `finishTop_gen` gives since it proves `AgreeU` for each state), then the `con` block, with `goalSemCM_step` and `goalSemC_of_goalSem` for the pure steps, and `+mink` on a cell formula runs the head first, so its crash is the cons's.  The top then follows from `finishTop_gen` with `Φ` the `ReturnsC` of the `%done` goal turned into `fwdPhi` and `bwdPhi` as `indirectTop_fwd` and `indirectTop_bwd` do.  Second, the mirror case (the call in the tail runs first at run time, so the head's pure `SemNV` is used at the call's new state) and `[7 p q]` (a call in `p` runs before `q`'s code, a call in `q` after `p`'s).  Third, `UOKC`, the joint induction with `GoalSemCM` clauses, whose leaves are `UOK` lifted by `goalSemC_of_goalSem` and whose call case is `indirect_runN`, `indirect_runD`, `indirect_runP`.  Eighteenth session (12.15): the first step is done for the forward half (`consCHTop_fwd`); `RunOKNC` changed (`SemNC` takes `AgreeU` at every state, no `size`, entries' registers may be the goal's), and the second step's description above of the mirror case is corrected in 12.15.
   15. **The cons with the call in its head** (2026-11-24, eighteenth session, `CompConsCall.lean`, `CompConsCallTop.lean`, `tools/gen/conscall.py`, `tools/gen/topall.py`).  Proved: `consCHTop_fwd` (and `consCHTop_fwdA` on `Annot`, `consCH_example`): the whole-subject code (`finishLUC env`) of the nomm `[[2 b c] d]` for a formula `[fh ft]`, with `b`, `c`, `d` meeting `UOK` (for example in `FragU`), at most 255 `%6`, `fh` a cell with `MinkN2` (any `Annot` of `[2 b c]` but the bare ones) and `ft` whose `+mink` is `d`'s value, has the need `this` and meets `ContractFwd` on every subject that fits the bell.  The backward half is not proved: no backward relation exists for a call under a parent (`indirect_semB` is a `Φ` at the top only), and `BellContract` needs both halves, so `RawMono` is not extended.  The run (`consCH_runN`): `+split`, the tail `d`, then `+past` behind the call (`pastL_hinted`: the call is hinted, so a tail need that asks something is parked in a bond behind a new `[hop]` block, which `+kern` later fills from the subject, `0w3 mov v0 v3` in the raw blocks; a need that asks nothing is left), the call (`indirect_runN`), `+copy`.  At run time the call runs first, then the block of `+past` fills the tail's need at the oracle's new state, so the subject is checked against the tail's need only after the call, as `+mink` (head first) asks.  `consNC_ok` (generated from `binaryGV_ok`) composes: the copy block, the head's `GoalSemCM`, the tail's `SemNV` at the new state (`TailNV`: `RunOKNV` with the block of `+past` among the entries, `tailNV_past`), the `con` block (`goalSemC_of_goalSem`, `goalSemCM_step`); `minkF_consC` is `+mink` on the cell formula.  The top: `finishLUC_fwdC` (any nomm whose run for `%done` is `[don 0]` and `RunOKNC` into it), through `finishTop_genA` (`finishTop_gen` with `AgreeU` at every state, which its proof gives since the ops of `+kern` do not touch the oracle).  Changes to `RunOKNC`: `SemNC` takes `AgreeU` at every state (the lazy blocks of a parent run after the call, at a new state), the field `size` is gone (the block of `+past` is one more entry than the `%6` count allows, and no theorem reads the bound), the entries' registers may be the goal's (`+split` of a pair goal gives the tail the goal's own registers, which the block of `+past` then fills), and `nec` speaks of the head's pure part (`[b c]` for the cons).  A correction to 12.14 on the mirror case `[[p] [2 ..]]`: in the raw blocks of `[[0 1] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]` the block `0w3 mov v0 v2, hop 2` is the `+past` of the head's need behind the call (the call in the tail is hinted), so at run time the head's code runs first, then the call, then the head's need is filled.  Left: the backward relation of the call under a parent (a `GoalSemCM` for `OracleBwd`: every result of the code from the jump is related to a `+mink` result), then `BellContract` of the cons; the mirror case (the `+past` of the head behind the call); `[7 p q]`; `UOKC`.  Nineteenth session (12.16): the backward half is proved (`consCHTop_bwd`, `consCHTop_contract`), on every `Annot` of the nomm (`consCH_contract_of_annot`), and `RawMono` holds on `FragUCCM`.
   16. **The backward relation of code that calls** (2026-11-24, nineteenth session, `CompIndirectOutB.lean`, `CompConsCallB.lean`, `CompConsCallTopB.lean`, `CompConsCallWrap.lean`, `CompHypOfCons.lean`, `tools/gen/conscallb.py`).  The design: two relations with one core, not one "iff" relation.  The forward relation plus determinism does not give the backward one: an oracle with `OracleBwd` need not answer where `+mink` returns (the oracle that never answers has `OracleBwd` and no `OracleFwd n`), so `SemNC` cannot be applied to it, and extending it with `+mink`'s answers needs an interpreter tone related to every `+mink` tone, which `ToneRel` does not give (a `%mean` frame whose trap is neither a bell's call nor `+mack`s has no related frame), plus the monotonicity of the interpreter in the oracle; and the case where `+mink` does not halt (the code must not return) is the whole backward induction anyway.  On `[2 b c]` the backward step is direct: the code returned, so the oracle answered the `%nok` on the products `x`, `y`, `OracleBwd.nok` gives `+mink` on `[x y]`, `MinkN2.bwd` makes it a run on the formula, and the block `out` gives the rest as forward.  So `SemNCB` (the counterpart of `SemNC`, same hypotheses with `OracleBwd` and no fuel) says: if the code from the run's jump returns anything, there is `r` with `Mink lib gul tm s f r` and `GoalSemCM` at `r`, the same predicate as forward, so a parent's step from the head's `GoalSemCM` at `rh` to its own at `r` is the forward text (`consNC_bwd` is `consNC_ok` with only the head's tone and the cons's `+mink` run changed, generated).  Proved: `indirect_semGB`, `indirect_runSB`, `indirect_runNB`, `indirect_runDB`, `indirect_runPB` (the indirect call under a fork-free `%next`, `%done`, `%pick`, the hypotheses of `indirect_runS`), `finishLUC_bwdC` (`ContractBwd` of the whole-subject code from `RunOKNC` and `SemNCB` under `%done`), `indirectTop_bwdC` and `indirectTop_bwd'` (the check: `indirectTop_bwd` re-derived, on any formula with `MinkN2`), `mink_consC_bwd`, `consNC_bwd`, `consCH_runNB`, `consCH_runDB`, `consCHTop_bwd`, `consCHTop_contract` (`BellContract` of `[[2 b c] d]`, the tail's `+mink` given in both directions, `Mq` and `MqB`), `consCHTop_contractA` and `consCH_exampleC` (`[[2 [0 3] [0 2]] [0 1]]`, both halves).  The wrappers: `RawMono` quantifies over every `Annot` of the nomm, and `Annot.memo`, `Annot.six0`, `Annot.six1` wrap any nomm, so `consCH_contract_of_annot` goes through them with `WrapRel` (`+mink` on the wrapper runs the inner formula with no more fuel and returns what it returns, `contract_wrap`).  So `RawMono` holds on `FragUCCM` = `FragUM` or `IndUM` or `ConsUM` (`[[2 b c] d]` with `b`, `c`, `d` in `FragU`, at most 255 `%6`, `bareLitQ c = false`), `rawMono_fragUCC`, and `compHyp_of_fragCC` derives `CompHyp` from it.  `RunOKNC` is unchanged: the backward relation is a separate `SemNCB` beside it (a field would have forced every producer to change at once).  Left: the mirror case (the call in the tail, with the head's need filled behind the call by `+past`) and `[7 p q]` with the call in `p`, both halves; then `UOKC` with the clauses `RunOKNC` and `SemNCB`.  Twentieth session (12.17): `UOKC` carries both clauses in one relation, `RunOKGC`, with `RunOKNC.nec` replaced by the semantic `NecC`.
   17. **The joint induction for code that calls** (2026-11-24, twentieth session, `CompUOKC.lean`, `CompConsGH.lean`, `CompFragUC.lean`, `CompHypOfUOKC.lean`, `tools/gen/consokf.py`, `tools/hoon/cc2dump.hoon`).  The relation of one run, for any goal: `RunOKGC lib P f goal g0 g1 nex`, the structural fields of `RunOKNC` (with `GMem goal` for the goal's registers), `nec`, `sem` (`SemGC`, which under `%next` is `SemNC` itself) and `semB` (`SemGCB`, under `%next` `SemNCB`).  One clause had to change: `RunOKNC.nec` says that a pure part `pq` has no value where the subject does not fit the run's need, which a call-free part does not meet (`[0 1]` under a goal that asks a cell has a value on an atom, a value that does not fit the goal), so `RunOKGC.nec` is semantic: `NecC lib f goal s`, every `+mink` result on `[s f]` is a crash with the caller's trace or a product that does not fit the goal (`BadC`), and there is one.  The clauses of a part, `UOKC lib env P f nm`: `kind` (the part is call-free, with `UOK` and `MinkEq`, `+mink` on `f` its pure value, which a parent uses for the call-free relations; or it is hinted, so `+past` parks a need that asks something behind it, `pastL_hinted`), `clean` (`CleanA` for `runLC`) and `run` (`OKCN`: every run from a state with the hypotheses of `OKNV` meets `RunOKGC`).  Both `clean` and `run` are asked for every goal but `%pick` (`NoPick`): the leaves meet the whole `OKC` (`okc_of_uok`, `okc_indirect`), but the cons whose head calls runs under `%pick` as a `bom` block with both parts under `%next bunt`, which needs `binaryGV_ok`'s pick case with a calling head, not done.  The tag clauses (`TagNV`, `TagV`) are read only by `%6` under `%next` and are not restated.  Proved.  The lift (`uokc_of_uok`): `RunOKNV.toGC`, `RunOKV.toGC` with `goalSemC_of_goalSem` (the state stays), backward with `+mink`'s result the pure value.  The call (`uokc_indirect`): `indirect_runN` and `indirect_runNB` under `%next`; under `%done` and `%pick` the block of `+simple-next` at `bo` then the run under `%next (lazy-from-reg re)` into it, turned into the goal by `runOKGC_of_next` (`goalSemCM_done_of_next`, `goalSemCM_pick_of_next`); `nec` from `MinkN2` (`minkN2_crashes`).  The wrappers (`UOKC.wrap` through `WrapRel`, for `Annot.memo`, `Annot.six0`, `Annot.six1`).  The top (`finishLUC_contractG`): the `%done` run of any nomm with `UOKC` gives `TopS`, and `finishTop_genA` with `fwdPhi`, `bwdPhi` gives both halves, without the `[don 0]` shape that `finishLUC_fwdC` asks.  The first parent (`uokc_consH`, `CompConsGH.lean`): the cons `[p d]` with `p` hinted with `UOKC`, `d` with `UOK` and `MinkEq`, for `[fh ft]` with `fh` a cell, under `%next` (`consGH_runN`: the run of `consCH_runN` with `p`'s `RunOKGC` in place of `indirect_runN`) and `%done` (`consGH_runD`).  `consNC_ok` and `consNC_bwd` are reused unchanged: the head is passed as a `RunOKNC` with the pure part `[0 0]` (`RunOKGC.toNC0`, its `nec` holds of every subject), and the cons's `NecC` is made from the head's (`necC_consH`) through the fit of the head's need, which `consNC_okF` (`consNC_ok` with one more conclusion, generated by `tools/gen/consokf.py`) returns.  The fragment: `FragC` (`ind`: `[2 p q]` with `p`, `q` in `FragU` and `bareLitQ q = false`; `consH`: `[p d]` with `p` in `FragC`, `d` in `FragU`), `FragUC` = `FragU` or `FragC`, `uokc_of_fragUC` (every `Annot` of a nomm of `FragUC` meets `UOKC`), `finishLUC_contractUC` (`BellContract … true` on `FragUC` with at most 255 `%6`), `rawMono_fragUCG` (`RawMono` on `FragUCG`), `fragUCCM_sub`, `compHyp_of_fragUCG`.  Example: `[[[2 [0 3] [0 2]] [0 1]] [0 1]]` (`uokc_example2`), whose `finishLUC` equals the raw blocks by `#guard` (the outer tail's need is parked behind the inner cons, `0w3 mov v0 v3`, and the inner tail's behind the call, `0w5 mov v0 v5`).  Not in the fragment, and why.  The cons with a call in its tail (`[[0 1] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]`) or in both parts (`[[2 [0 3] [0 2]] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]`): the tail is hinted, so `+past` parks the head's need `hed` behind it at the split (`nexh = ⟨mk bunt [] [(o, mk hed [] [])], jmp0 o⟩`), and the head runs for `%next nexh.laz th`, a goal whose lazy need has a bond, while every relation so far (`OKNV`, `UOK`, `UOKC`) is about goals `%next (ofSure sur) th`.  So `UOKC.run` must cover goals `%next L th` with `L` a bunt with one bond entry (the block at `o` fills `hed` from the head's product after the tail's code ran), for call-free heads too; that is the clause to strengthen before the mirror case, and it needs a `GoalSem` for a lazy goal with entries.  `[7 p q]` (`[7 [2 [0 3] [0 2]] [0 1]]`): `p` runs for `q`'s result need, which has entries when `q` has a `%6`, the same issue; for `q` without `%6` (`Frag q`, as `uok_seven` asks) `p` runs for a goal `%next (ofSure ..)` (the shape `runOKNV_n7` hands `OKNV`), so `[7 p q]` with a call in `p` and `q` in `Frag` is the next constructor to try with `UOKC` as it is.  So `RawMono` holds on `FragUCG`.
   18. **Goals with entries: one relation** (2026-11-24, twenty-first session, `CompLazyGoal.lean`, `tools/hoon/lazydump.hoon`).  The raw blocks first.  In `[[4 [0 3]] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]` the bond block of `+past` is `0w3: mov v7 v2, hop 0w2`, and `v7` is written by `0w7: inc v9 v7`, which runs before the call `0w5: nok v5 v6 v4`.  So the ops that a run puts at a lazy block of its goal read registers that the run computed, and every code between the run's end and the lazy block must keep them.  In `[[0 1] ..]` and `[[0 2] ..]` the head keeps the bond entry at its label and the final pass fills it from `v0`, and in `[7 [[0 2] [0 3]] [6 [0 2] [0 3] [1 0]]]` the tail of the cons keeps the fork entry's half `this v2` at the branch block, which the final pass fills.  The relation: `RunOKL lib nm L th D W g0 g1 nex`, the structural fields of `RunOKE` with `CondFresh`, a need `D s` with a noun `W s` for each subject `s` (the registers the run computed for its lazy blocks, allocated in the run), `front` (every goal entry got ops `F` in front of its block, and after any ops `A` that fill the result's entry at that label, `A ++ F` unproxied fills the goal entry for the product from the input need `pair ned (D s)` holding `[s0 (W s)]`, `FrontL`), and `sem` (`SemNV` with `AgreeL`, the agreement at the result entries the run made, and with `D s` holding `W s` at `th`, `GoalSemD`).  The parent keeps `D s` until the lazy block by giving the code that runs in between the input need `pair ned (D s)`, as `runOKNV_seven4` did for one register.  What it keeps of the earlier relations: of `RunOKE` the pairing of goal and result entries at the same label, of `FrontSemR` the shape of the front, and of `CollRunU` the `sem` against every later `cond` and the extra input register.  Proved: `RunOKL.toNV` and `RunOKNV.toL` (for a goal with no entries it is `RunOKNV`, with `D = none`), `[0 axe]` (`runOKL_n0`, and `runOKL_n0_run` from the run), `[4 p]` from `CollRunU` with `D s = this d` (`runOKL_n4`, and `runOKL_n4_none` from the run when the goal's sure part asks nothing, as behind a call), and the composition `[7 p q]` (`runOKL_seven`, `D s = pair (Dp s) (Dq (p s))`), which shows that the relation composes.  Not done: `[1 k]`, `[3 p]`, `%5`, `%10` and the cons, the cons with a call in its tail and `[7 p q]` with a `%6` in `q` in general.  Where it stops, with the raw blocks.  First, the cons at a goal entry whose need is `this r` or `both r ..`: `+split` puts `con h t r` at the entry's block and keeps both halves at that label, so a result entry's block is not empty, which `labs` and `AgreeL` ask.  The weakest change is `AgreeL` with a body (`g1` holds `[[] B [%hop th]]` at the entry, the program `A ++ B` unproxied, `A` filling the entry), with `front` about the prefix that the run put before the parent's body, as now.  Second, the `sem` is strict, so `D s` must be allocated in the run.  In `[7 [4 [0 3]] [6 [5 [0 1] [1 6]] [0 1] [1 5]]]` the goal's sure part is the register `d` of the `%5`, which `+collapse-lazy-atom` reads in the branch blocks, and the `%5`'s code must keep its own input register `d`, which only the relaxed form (`InR`, `KeepI` of `CollRunU`) says, and the relaxed `sem` is proved over `FragX` but not over `FragU`.  Third, under `+fork` the ops of a branch in a kid of a lazy block read that branch's registers only on its path, which is why `D` depends on the subject.  For the cons with a call in its tail the head needs only the strict form: its bond goal's sure part is the bunt (`runOKL_n4_none` gives the head `[4 [0 3]]` of the example), and the call keeps `D s` because `D s` lies above the call's end.  What is left there is the composition itself, with `copyL` and the call's `RunOKGC` in place of `q`'s `RunOKL` in `runOKL_seven`.
   19. **The cons with a call in its tail** (2026-11-24, twenty-second session, `CompConsT.lean`, `CompFragUC.lean`, `CompHypOfUOKC.lean`).  The raw blocks first: `finishLUC` of `[[4 [0 3]] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]` equals the blocks of `tools/hoon/lazydump.hoon` (`#guard` in `CompConsT.lean`), and `[[0 1] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]` already had its `#guard` in `CompCallParent.lean`.  The composition is `consNT_ok`: from `+split`, the gen after `+past` behind the tail, the tail's `RunOKGC` for `%next lq jt`, the head's `RunOKL` under its goal `Lh` into the tail's code, and `+copy`, the cons meets `RunOKGC` under a fork-free `%next`.  At run time the copy block runs, then the head's code (its `GoalSemD` puts `D s` holding `W s`), then the tail's code with the input need `pair ned (D s)` (so the call keeps `D s`, which lies above the tail's end), then the bond block (the head's `front` fills `lp` for the head's product from `pair ned (D s)`, whether the head kept the bond entry, as `[0 1]` does, or consumed it, as `[4 [0 3]]` does), then the `con` block.  The two `sem` clauses share one `core`: the forward clause takes the tail's tone from `minkF_consT` (the head's crash is the cons's, else the tail runs with less fuel), the backward clause takes it from the tail's `semB` and builds the cons's `+mink` run by `mink_consT_bwd`, so no generator was needed.  The case where the head's need asks nothing (`+past` makes no block) is the same theorem with a head goal without entries (`hjt`), the head then given by `RunOKNV.toL`.  Nothing in `RunOKL`, `UOK` or `UOKC` changed.  What the cons asks of its parts are two new predicates.  `BondOK` (head): every run under the bond goal `mk bunt [] [(b, hed)]` returns a clean need (`clean`, without state hypotheses, as `UOKC.clean` asks) and meets `RunOKL` (`run`), proved for `[0 axe]` with `1 ≤ axe` (`bondOK_n0`, with `runOKL_n01` for `[0 1]`, whose `+from` is not `fromL 1`) and `[4 p]` with `p` in `FragX` (`bondOK_n4` from `runOKL_n4_none`).  `EntNew` (tail): the code of every run under a fork-free `%next` begins at a block the run made, which `runOKL_n4_none` needs (`th.there` apart from the bond label), proved for the indirect call (`entNew_indirect`, the block of `+copy`); `RunOKGC` does not record it (its `ent` allows the goal's own jump).  Then `consT_runN` (from the run, mirror of `consGH_runN`), `consT_cleanN`, `consT_runD`, `uokc_consT` (head with `UOK`, `MinkEq`, `BondOK`, hinted tail with `UOKC`, `EntNew`).  The fragment: `FragB` (`[0 axe]`, `1 ≤ axe`, and `[4 p]`, `p` in `FragX`), `FragC.consT` (a head in `FragB`, the tail an indirect call with parts in `FragU`), `uokc_consTA` on every `Annot`, so `finishLUC_contractUC`, `rawMono_fragUCG` and `compHyp_of_fragUCG` cover it.  Examples with both halves: `consT_example1` (`[[0 1] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]`) and `consT_example4` (`[[4 [0 3]] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]`).
   20. **Runs that call under any lazy goal: `RunOKLC`** (2026-11-24, twenty-third session, `CompRunOKLC.lean`, `CompRunOKLCSeven.lean`, `CompTailGC.lean`, `CompPairLC.lean`, `CompCallC.lean`, `CompConsLC.lean`, `CompFragD.lean`, `CompHypOfFragD.lean`, `CompKernW.lean`, `CompKerfLC.lean`, `CompBondCall.lean`, `tools/hoon/calldump2.hoon`).  First the cheap step: `EntNew` holds for every nomm of `FragC` (`copyL_th`, `entNew_consH`, `entNew_consT`, proved beside `UOKC` by `uokc_entNew_of_fragC`), so `FragC.consT` takes any `FragC` tail (`consT_example5`, `[[0 1] [[2 [0 3] [0 2]] [0 1]]]`, both halves).  The raw blocks: `tools/hoon/calldump2.hoon` dumps 16 formulas with calls in several parts; eleven are `#guard`s of `finishLUC` in `CompRunOKLC.lean`, all equal.  In `[[2 [0 3] [0 2]] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]` the head call runs under the bond goal behind the tail call and its `+kern` puts `mov v7 v2` at the bond block, `v7` being the call's product register, which the tail call keeps; so the witness that a run leaves for its goal's lazy blocks is a value of the oracle, not a function of the subject, and the relation takes a witness relation `Q s v d w` in place of `RunOKL`'s `D`, `W`.  The relation `RunOKLC lib P f L th Q g0 g1 nex`: the structural fields of `RunOKL` (without `size`) with the entries' registers allowed to be the goal's, `dreg` and `front` (`FrontLC`) for `Q`, the semantic `NecC`, and `sem`/`semB` with `AgreeL` at every state and `GoalSemCMD` (`GoalSemCM` of `%next L.sure th` whose fitting product also leaves some `d` holding `w` with `Q s v d w`).  Specializations: `RunOKL.toLC` (call-free, `pureWit`), `RunOKGC.toLC` (no entries, `noWit`), `RunOKLC.toGC`.  Constructors proved: `[7 p q]` (`runOKLC_seven`, any lazy goal, either part may call, `sevenWit`); the pair of runs that may both call (`pairLC_ok`, with `TailGC`, the second part behind its `+past`, `tailGC_past`), instantiated for the cons with any calling parts under a fork-free `%next` (`consLC_runN`, `uokc_consLC`, the head under the bond goal through `BondOKC`) and for the indirect call whose parts may call (`indirectC_runS`, `uokc_indirectC`, with `MinkC2`, `nokStep_fwd`, `nokStep_bwd`); the indirect call under any lazy goal with entries (`kerfLC` with `kernWit pro`, from `kern_specW`: `+kern` with the registers its ops write, and `indirectC_inner`), hence `bondOKC_indirectC`.  `[0 axe]`, `[4 p]` and call-free `[7 p q]` come from `RunOKL` by `RunOKL.toLC`; `[1 k]` and `[3 p]` only under goals without entries (`RunOKNV`, then `RunOKGC.toLC`).  The fragment: `FragD` (`FragC` with the indirect call whose subject part, formula part or both call, and the cons with any `FragD` part) and `FragE` (`FragD` with the cons whose head is an indirect call and whose tail calls), `uokc_entNew_of_fragE`, `rawMono_fragUEG` and `compHyp_of_fragUEG` with `RawMono`'s statement unchanged (`fragUDG_subE`, `fragUCG_sub`).  Examples with both halves: `consCC_example` (`[[2 [0 3] [0 2]] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]`), `indC_example1` (`[2 [2 [0 3] [0 2]] [0 2]]`), `indC_example2` (`[2 [0 3] [2 [0 1] [0 2]]]`), `indC_example3`.  Where it stops.  (1) `[7 p q]` is not in the fragment: `runOKLC_seven` composes `RunOKLC` under `%next`, but `UOKC` asks `%done` too (where `q` runs under `%done` and `p` under `q`'s result), which needs the same composition with `GoalSemCM` of `%done` at the end, and `p`'s goal is `q`'s result, which has entries when `q` has a `%6` or a calling head (a bond of `+past`), so `p` needs `RunOKLC` under that goal, which no fragment predicate provides yet; so `[7 [2 [0 3] [0 2]] [4 [0 1]]]`, `[7 [[0 1] [0 1]] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]` and `[[7 [0 3] [4 [0 1]]] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]` are not covered.  (2) Heads under the bond goal (`BondOKC`) are `FragB` and the indirect call with call-free parts; a cons head would put `con` at the bond block (`RunOKL`'s `labs`, 12.18 (1)), and `[4 p]` with a calling `p` needs `+collapse-lazy-atom` with a call below.  (3) The cons is proved under fork-free `%next` and `%done` only, as before (no `%pick`).  (4) `[1 k]`, `[3 p]` under goals with entries need `RunOKL` for them (12.18).
   21. **`[7 p q]` with calls in the fragment** (2026-11-24, twenty-fourth session, `CompSevenG.lean`, `CompSevenMink.lean`, `CompSevenA.lean`, `CompSevenL.lean`, `CompSevenB.lean`, `CompFragF.lean`, `CompHypOfFragF.lean`, `tools/hoon/calldump3.hoon`). The `+mink` side is `MinkC7` (`+mink` on `f` runs `fb`, then `fc` on its product, forward with the fuel of each part, backward), proved for every `Annot` form of the nomm `[7 p q]`: `n7` (`[7 b c]`), `inline` (`[2 b c]` whose `c` returns `g`: `fb = b`, `fc = g`), `n8` and `n8g` (`[8 b c]`: for a cell `b`, `fb = [b [0 1]]`; for an atom `b` both crash at once and `fb = 0`, the `p` part being call-free, `annot_atom`, `annot_slot1_pure`, `uokc_atomPair`), `n8l` (`fb = [1 [y d]]`), `n9` (`fb = c`, `fc = [2 [0 1] [0 a]]`), the wrappers by `UOKC.wrap`; the products of `fb` are among the subjects `q` is annotated on (`img_eight`, `img_atom`, `img_eightL`), and `UOKC.mono` moves `q`'s relation to them (`uokc_sevenA`, `uokc_sevenLA`).  `EntNew` of `[7 p q]`: from `p`'s, or from `q`'s when `p`'s jump is its goal's or new (`JNew`, proved for `[0 axe]`, `[1 k]` and the nomms with `EntNew`).  `BondOKC` of `[7 p q]` (a head under the bond goal): `q` under the bond goal (`BondOKC`), `p` under its result (`LOKC`), `runOKLC_sevenM` (`bondOKC_sevenC`, `bondOKC_sevenA`).    22. **One induction over the nomms that call: `NC`** (2026-11-24, twenty-fifth session, `CompNC.lean`, `CompNCSeven.lean`, `CompNCLeaf.lean`, `CompNCJump.lean`, `CompNCFour.lean`, `CompNCInd.lean`, `CompHypOfNC.lean`, `CompNCExamples.lean`, `tools/hoon/calldump4.hoon`).  The aim: one structural induction, with no shape conditions, in place of the special constructors of `FragI`.  The raw blocks first (`calldump4.hoon`, header of `CompNC.lean`): `RunOKLC` is false in the generality asked for three constructors.  (1) `+split` puts `con` at a goal entry's block and keeps the halves there: `[7 [[0 2] [0 3]] [[2 [0 2] [0 6]] [0 1]]]` (`%bc1`) has `3: mov v0 vg, cel, hed -> v7, tal -> v8, con v7 v8 v3, hop 2`, the bond of `q` kept by the cons `p` with the body `[con v7 v8 v3]`, which `labs` and `AgreeL` (and `RunOKGC.labs` at the top: `UOKC` is false for this nomm) exclude.  (2) `+copy` puts a `mov` there: `[7 [[0 1] [0 1]] [[2 [0 2] [0 6]] [[0 2] [0 3]]]]` (`%bc2`), `5: mov v0 v4, mov v4 v5, hop 3`.  (3) `+collapse-lazy-atom` reads the goal's register under a goal with a bond and a sure part `this d`: `[7 [4 [0 3]] [[2 [0 1] [0 1]] [0 1]]]` (`%s4t`), `8: inc v8 v5` writes `q`'s input `v5` and the bond block `3: mov v5 v3` reads it after the call, so `RunOKLC.dreg` is false and `q` would have to keep its own result register (the strict input need).  `%10` at a bond is (1) again (`%s10`).  `%lim` puts no op at its bond block (`3: mov v0 v3`, the bond need is a `pair`).  So the right hypothesis for all of `NC` is `RunOKLC` with (a) bodies at result entries (`labs` with `⟨[], B, hop th⟩`, `AgreeL` with the program's block `A ++ B` unproxied), for (1), (2), and (b) the relaxed input need of `CollRunU` (`InR`, `KeepI`), for (3).  This session states the hypothesis in the strict relations with three levels of goals: `NCOK lv t` (on every `Annot`: `UOKC`; with `t`, `JNew` and `EntNew` when hinted; from level `1`, `LOKCS`, `LOKC` under goals whose sure part asks nothing and whose jump is at no entry's label, without the jump fact; at level `2`, `LOKC`).  The class `NC lv t`: `[0 axe]` (any level), `[1 k]` (level `0`), `[3 p]` and `[4 p]` (`p` at level `0`, calling or not, level `0`; for `[4 p]` also `p` in `FragX`, level `1`), the cons (level `0`, the head at level `1` when the tail calls), `[7 p q]` (`q` at the level and `p` at level `2`, or at level `0` with `NoEntF q` and `p` at level `0`; a call-free one in `FragU`), the indirect call (parts at level `0`, any level, `bareLitQ q = false`, the one exclusion, the `%bare` literal of `CompIndirectWrap.lean`), `FragU` (level `0`), and the weakenings.    24. **The cons under a goal with entries: fronts at the state level and flat needs** (2026-11-24, twenty-seventh session, `CompFrontS.lean`, `CompFlat.lean`, `CompFlatCopy.lean`, `CompPairLS.lean`, `CompConsLS.lean`, `CompConsLSRun.lean`).  The finding first.  `RunOKLB.front` (`FrontLB`, like `FrontLC` and `FrontL` before it) describes the ops `F` a run put in front of a goal entry's block through every fill `A` of the run's result entry that keeps every register below the run's end.  That form does not compose where `+copy` merges the two parts' entries at one label, which the raw blocks show in `%bc2` (`5: mov v0 v4, mov v4 v5, hop 3`: the halves `v3`, `v4` of the tail's bond merged into `v3` with `mov v3 v4`) and in `%bc1` (the inner cons's halves `[v6 ~]` and `[~ v7]` merged into `[v6 v7]` with no op): at run time the fill of the merged need and the copy's moves write the tail's registers before the head's ops run, those registers lie below the head's end, so no `A` of the head's `FrontLB` describes the state the head's ops start from.  `FrontS` says the same at the level of the state just before `F` (from any registers where the result entry holds the subject, the witness `d` holds `w` and `E` the product, `F` fills the goal entry keeping every register outside the run's range and the entry, or crashes where the product does not fit), with the facts that make it imply `FrontLB` (`FrontS.toLB`): the result entry's registers below the run's start are the goal entry's (`loc`, label-locality), the witness is apart from it (`dis`), and a product that fits the entry comes from a subject that fits the result entry (`fit`).  `frontS_pair` composes the two parts' fronts at one label with the copy's moves in front and the split's ops behind, merged or not (four cases).  `RunOKLS` is `RunOKLB` with every front as `FrontS` (`FrontsS`).  Flat needs: in the plain class a lazy need has no fork and every bond's need is fork- and bond-free (`Flat`); on them `+split`'s loop over the bonds is `+split-sure` of each bond's need at its own block (`SplitBs`, `SplitBs.spec`, with `Pref`: the state grew only by registers and ops in front of blocks) and `+copy`'s merge is `+copy-lazy` of the two bonds' needs at the bond's block (`MergeBs`, `MergeBs.spec`), so `+split` and `+copy` of flat needs are the fork-free arms followed by these loops (`splitL_flat`, `copyL_flat'`), and the old specs of the sure part (`split_ok`, `copy_ok`, `keeps_split`, `keeps_copy`) are reused.  `pairLS_ok`: `pairLC_ok` under a flat goal with entries, into `RunOKLS` with the witness relation `pairWit` (the parts' witnesses side by side), parts that may call (`RunOKLS` of the tail with its `+past`, of the head under `hed` or `hed`'s sure part parked in a bond), the result flat with clean entries.  `LOKS` (`CompConsLS.lean`) is the hypothesis under a flat lazy goal: `LOKB`'s assumptions with the goal flat and clean, and `RunOKLS`, the jump the goal's or a block made and at no entry, the result flat with clean entries.  Proved: `lokS_n0` (`[0 axe]`, `axe ≥ 1`; no op at any entry, the result entry the goal entry seen at `axe`, through the stripped state as `LOKC.toB`), `lokS_consP` (the cons of two call-free parts with `LOKS`: both `+past`s are the identity, `pairLS_ok` composes), so `[[0 2] [0 3]]`, `[[0 1] [0 1]]` and `[[0 3] [0 1]]`, the `p` parts of `%bc1`, `%bc2` and `%lim`, meet `LOKS` under every flat lazy goal with bodies.  Where it stops.  (1) The cons with a calling part: `pairLS_ok` takes it, but the instantiation needs `RunOKLS` of the tail with its `+past` block (`tailGC_past` for `RunOKLS`) and the head's park (the second branch of `consLC_runN`); done in 12.25 (`RunOKLS.past`, `lokS_cons`).  (2) `x`: the parts and the cons have `x = false`; a part that reads its goal's register (`[4 p]` under a goal `this r`, `runOKLB_n4`) needs the halves of `+split` in the cons's witness when `+split` allocates them and `x_p || x_q` when the halves are the goal's own registers.
