   28. **The increment and the cell test under a flat lazy goal and in the class** (2026-11-27, thirty-first session, `CompFourLS.lean`, `CompConsLSF.lean`, `CompThreeLS.lean`, `CompFourTop.lean`).  (1) `[4 p]` meets `LOKS true` for every `p` that meets `LOKS` at goals with no entry (`lokS_four`), calls included.  The run is `+collapse-lazy-atom` (`CollPre`), the block `[inc arg d] hop there` and `p` under `%next (lazy-from-reg arg)`, and the proof follows the indirect call: `incLB` puts the block of the increment behind `p`'s run as `nokLB` puts `%nok`, and `collLS` puts the collapse in front as `kerfLB` puts `+kerf`.  The fronts are the collapse's ops, which read `d` (`CollP`).  When `d` is a register of the goal's sure need, `x = true` gives it as a goal register holding the product (`holdsN_atom_mem`), and when it is fresh the witness holds it (`fourWit`).  When no atom fits the goal, the collapse's block `[%bom ~]` crashes.  The jump of `[4 p]` is always a block it made (`jNewLS_four`).  `[3 p]` is the same (`lokS_three`): `clqLB` puts `+forl` and the block `[clq arg z o']` behind `p`'s run and `collLS` is reused unchanged, and under a goal that asks nothing `p` runs under the goal itself, so `p`'s `RunOKLS` holds for `[3 fb]` with the witness `wutWit` (`RunOKLS.wutNone`: every product fits the goal and its entries).  (2) The flag does not compose yet, so the class keeps it where it does not matter.  At a goal whose sure part asks nothing, or that has no entry, `RunOKLS true` is `RunOKLS false` (`FlagFree`, `RunOKLS.unflag`), and `LOKSF` is `LOKS false` at such goals only.  The operand of `[4 p]` and the parts of an indirect call run at goals with no entry, so they need only `LOKSF`, and the indirect call of such parts meets `LOKS false` at every goal (`lokS_indirectF`).  A cons at a flag-free goal gives its parts flag-free goals, because `+split` of a need that asks nothing gives halves that ask nothing (`split_none`), the halves of a goal with no entry have none, and the head parked behind a hinted tail has the bunt as its sure part (`lokS_consF`).  `[7 p q]` at a flag-free goal runs `q` there (`lokS_sevenF`), while its `p` still needs `LOKS false` at every goal.  (3) The class `CallTreeA` is `CallTree7` with `[4 p]` and `[3 p]`, indexed by whether a nomm meets `LOKS false` at every goal: index `true` for `[0 axe]`, the indirect call, the cons of two such parts and `[7 p q]` with such a `q`, index `false` for `[4 p]`, `[3 p]` and a cons or a `[7 p q]` over a part of index `false`, and the `p` of `[7 p q]` must have index `true`.  Under `%done` every nomm of the class meets `DoneOK` (`callTreeA_done`, with `four_doneB` and `three_doneB` at the top through `unaryGB_ok` and `unaryGTB_ok`), so the whole-subject code meets both halves of the contract with no hypothesis about the run (`finishLUC_contractCallTreeA`, `finishLUC_bellContractCallTreeA`).  For example `[4 [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]` (`fourInd_contract`), `[3 [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]` (`threeInd_contract`) and `[[4 [0 2]] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]` (`fourCons_contract`, the head under the parked goal) are in the class.  Where it stops.  (1) `[4 p]` or `[3 p]` as the `p` of `[7 p q]`, and under a cons whose goal has entries and a sure part that asks something, is not in the class, because a flagged front would need the goal's sure registers at the time it runs.  For a cons that means the halves of `+split` in the cons's witness when `+split` allocates them, and for `[7 p q]` the keep fact of `%s4t`.  (2) `[5 p q]` (no `MinkC5` for calling parts yet; the pair is the indirect call's inner pair with the block `[eqq rp rq z o']` and `+forl` in place of `%nok`, and the goal that asks nothing takes the block `[equ rp rq]`), `%10`, `%1` (a walk spec of `+mede` over the entries), `%6` and the direct call in `RunOKLS` form are not started.
   29. **The compiler's needs in the relations of the class** (2026-11-27, thirty-second session, every file of 12.23 to 12.28).  `SemLB`, `SemLBB`, `SemGB`, `SemGBB` and the structures over them (`RunOKLB`, `RunOKLS`, `RunOKGB`, `LOKB`, `LOKS`, `LOKSF`, `DoneOK`) take the compiler's needs `N : Needs` after `lib`, and `sem`/`semB` assume `∀ st, Inv st → N ≤ ν st` (named `hN`, passed next to `hS hF`/`hS hB`) besides `OracleSteps` and `OracleFwd`/`OracleBwd`, as `ContractFwd`/`ContractBwd`, `fwdPhi`, `bwdPhi` and `callStep_fwd` do.  Every lemma over them takes `{N : Needs}` and is otherwise the same text (a script: the parameter after `lib` at each use, the binder after `lib`'s, `hN` before `hS` at each `sem` intro and call); the lifts from the relations with no `N` hold at every `N` (`RunOKLC.toB`, `RunOKGC.toB`, `LOKC.toB`: their `sem` drops `hN`), and the one way back is at `Needs.none`, the needs that answer nothing (`RunOKLB.toLC`; `Needs.none_le`).  `finishLUC_contractGB` takes `N` before `hR` and asks `RunOKGB` at that `N`, which `finishTop_genB` already supplies (the hypothesis of `fwdPhi`/`bwdPhi` goes to `R.sem`).  The statements of the contracts at the top (`finishLUC_contractCallTree7`, `finishLUC_bellContractCallTree7`, `finishLUC_contractCallTreeA`, `finishLUC_bellContractCallTreeA`, `finishLUC_contractTopTree`, `finishLUC_bellContractTopTree` and the examples `cc2_contract`, `consInd_contract`, `bc1_contract`, `sevenIn_contract`, `fourInd_contract`, `threeInd_contract`, `fourCons_contract`) did not change: their `N` is the one the class is run at.  Why the change is needed (track J's finding F2, Gaps 18.5, checked): without `hN`, `RunOKGB.sem` of a run with a direct call is false.  Take the tail call `[2 [0 1] [1 k]]` under `%done` with `k = [1 1]`, annotated direct to a bell `b` with `b.fol = k` and `N b false = some nd`: its code is the allocation of `sub` for `nd` and the block `[jmp b subV]`, so the result is the oracle's answer `O.call st b false vs`.  Run the relation with `Inv = True`, `ν = Needs.none` (constant) and an oracle whose `call` always answers `(Tone.zero 0, st)` and whose `nok` answers as `+mink` does: `OracleSteps` holds, and `OracleFwd.call` holds vacuously because `argsFor Needs.none b m s = none` for every call; but `+mink` gives `Tone.zero 1` on every subject, and `GoalSemCM` at `%done` asks `ToneRel (Tone.zero 0) (Tone.zero 1)`, which is false.  With `hN` this `ν` is excluded (`N ≤ Needs.none` fails at `b`), and with `N ≤ ν st` the arguments `vs` are `argsFor (ν st) b false s` (`argsFor_of_alloc`), so `OracleFwd.call` constrains the answer (`oracle_call_alloc`).  `[5 p q]` (`CompFiveLS.lean`): `MinkC5` and `minkC5_five` only.  What blocks the rest: the Hoon's `%5` arm (`nock-compilation.hoon`, `[%5 *]` of `+run`, and `runLC`'s case `Nomm.n5`) takes `r-p`, `r-q` with `re` BEFORE `+collapse-lazy-atom` and `+forl`, unlike `[3 p]` and `[4 p]` (collapse first) and unlike the indirect call (`+kerf` first, then `rSub`, `rFol`).  So the `[eqq r-p r-q z o']` block cannot be put behind the pair's run as a `RunOKLB` from the state after the collapse (`clqLB`'s form): that relation's kept registers (`x < g0.re` and not in the goal's need `lazy-from-reg r`) would include `r-p`, `r-q`, which the pair writes.  The lemma must start from the state before the two `re`s and take the collapse inside (`collLS` with registers allocated in front of `CollPre`), the same catch as track J's `callLB` (allocation before `+kerf`).  Next on this track: that lemma (`eqqLB` from the state before `r-p`), the pair part of `lokS_indirect_runF` under `%next (pairReg r-p)` behind it, the goal that asks nothing (`[equ r-p r-q] hop th`, no `noneEquivalentL` shortcut in `%5`), the top through `doneBlocks`, the constructor of `CallTreeA` with index `false`; then a direct call in the class (track J's 18.7: `callLB`, the tail call at the top on the whole-subject code once `mono` `&` is modelled).
   30. **The equality test in the class** (2026-11-27, thirty-third session, `CompFiveLS.lean`, `CompFourTop.lean`).  `[5 p q]` meets `LOKS true` when its parts meet `LOKSF` and `p` is call-free with `MinkEq` or hinted (`lokS_five`), and its jump is always a block it made (`jNewLS_five`).  The catch of 12.29 (the Hoon's `%5` arm takes `r-p = g0.re`, `r-q = g0.re + 1` before `+collapse-lazy-atom`) is met by running the inner relation from a state with fewer registers than the real one, not by a new relation: `eqqLB` concludes `RunOKLB` of `[5 fb fc]` under `%next (lazy-from-reg r) (jmp0 there)` from a state `gL` with the blocks, `bo`, `cond` and `drop` of the state `g2` after the collapse but `gL.re ≤ r-p`, so the kept registers (`x < gL.re`) do not have `r-p`, `r-q`; every other field of `RunOKLB` is weaker from `gL` (`condFresh_low`, `growU_congrL`).  `collLSk` is `collLS` with the rest's run from such a `gL` (`g0.re ≤ gL.re`, with `g2.re ≤ g1.re` and `CondFresh g2 g1` given separately), and the collapse's `CollPre` from the state after the two `re`s holds from the state before them (`CollPre.lower`: `CollP` ignores its lower bound, `ProxyFor`, `Keeps` and `Grow` compose with the register step).  The pair is `pairRun_ok`, the pair part of `lokS_indirect_runF` for any base register and label.  The goal that asks nothing takes `equLS` (the block `[equ r-p r-q] hop th`, the goal's entries get no op, the fronts at the state level with `F = []`; `%5` has no `noneEquivalentL` shortcut, the collapse returns nothing: `collapseL_noneEq`, and else a register: `collapseL_isSome`).  At the top: the two registers, `doneBlocks`, `[eqq r-p r-q o0 o1]` and the pair, through `pairGTB_ok` (`unaryGTB_ok` for a block reading two registers, the cases of `MinkC5` as in `nokLB`) and `five_doneB`.  The class `CallTreeA` has the constructor `five` (`[5 p q]` with both parts in the class, index `false`), so `finishLUC_contractCallTreeA` covers it, for example `[5 [2 [0 3] [0 2]] [0 1]]` (`fiveInd_contract`).  Where it stops: as for `[4 p]` and `[3 p]` (12.28), `[5 p q]` is not in the class as the `p` of `[7 p q]` nor under a cons whose goal has entries and a sure part that asks something.  `%10` is not started: `+into` (`intoL`, `intoLoopL` over the goal's entries) has a spec only for the sure part (`into_ok`, `into_okK`); the cons's `RunOKLS` form rests on the `+split` facts at each bond entry (`SplitAt`, `frontS_pair`, `pairLS_ok`), and `%10` needs the same for `+into` (an `IntoAt` and the edit in place of the cell in `pairLS_ok`'s `Fin`/`FinB`).
   31. **The constant in the class** (2026-11-27, thirty-fourth session, `CompOneLS.lean`, `CompFourTop.lean`).  `[1 k]` meets `LOKS false` at every flat lazy goal (`lokS_n1`), so it has index `true` in `CallTreeA` (constructor `n1`, formula `quote k`) and may sit anywhere in the class, the `p` of `[7 p q]` included.  The run is `+mede` (`medeL`): the block `[hop th]` with the constant's ops in front, and the same walk over the goal's entries, where the ops of `+mede`'s loop write `k` into the entry's need (after `+sure-require-look`) in front of the block's body, or crash where `k` has not the need's shape (`+crash-need` with an immediate atom: `imm`, `cel`, which crash on the atom).  The walk was `walkLazy_ok` with `medeGate_ok` (`CompMedeE.lean`); the one fact added is that the ops mention only the registers of the need and registers allocated in the walk (`medeLoop_specR`, `MedePR`, `medeGateR_ok`), so the unproxying by any `cond` that agrees with the final one, which is the start's (`+mede` makes no proxy) and has no key from the start's `re` on (`CondBelow`), leaves them alone (`unproxy_ops`), and the jump `th` has no argument.  The result asks nothing and has no entry, the jump is the new block, the fronts read no register (`x = false`, witness `pureWit` with no register).  `runOKL_n1` gives the old relation `RunOKL` (no stripping of the entries' bodies is needed: the result has no entry, so `labs` is empty) and `FrontsS`, and `RunOKL.toLC`, `RunOKLC.toB` give `RunOKLB`.  At the top `[1 k]` is the block `[imm k r] don r`, which `UOKC` of `FragX.frag` covers (`n1_doneB`).  Examples, both halves of the contract with no hypothesis about the run: `[7 [[1 5] [0 1]] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]` (`oneSeven_contract`, the form of `%dt`'s outer `%7` of track J, Gaps 19: its `p` `[[1 R] [0 1]]` is in `CallTreeA` with index `true`, so `lokS_callTreeA` gives its `LOKS false`) and `[[1 0] [4 [0 1]]]` (`oneCons_contract`).  Then a first piece of the restriction of 12.28 is lifted: a cons whose tail calls (hinted) and meets `LOKS false` meets `LOKS false` at every goal whatever its head, when the head meets `LOKSF` (`lokS_consH`), because the head then runs under a flag-free goal: the goal parked by `+past` (sure part the bunt) or, where `+past` parks nothing, the head's half of the split goal, which then asks nothing (`pastL_hinted`).  So the index of the constructor `cons` of `CallTreeA` is now `b2 && (b1 || !q.pure)` (was `b1 && b2`): in the class a nomm that is not call-free is hinted (`CallTreeA.hinted_of`).  For example `[[4 [0 2]] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]` has index `true` and is the `p` of `[7 p q]` in `fourConsSeven_contract`.  Where it stops: `[4 p]`, `[3 p]`, `[5 p q]` (and a nomm of index `false` in general) are still not in the class as the `p` of `[7 p q]`, nor as a part of a cons under a goal with entries and a sure part that asks something, except as the head before a hinted tail of index `true`.  The rest needs what 12.28 says: the halves of `+split` in the cons's witness when `+split` allocates them (`this r`: fresh `h`, `t` and `con h t r`; for `pair` and `both` the halves are the goal's own registers, so the cons keeps `x = true`), which is a generalization of `frontS_pair` (parts' `FrontS` with `E` the halves, `pairWit` with the halves holding the parts' products) and of `pairLS_ok`'s `hq`/`hp` to any flag; and for `[7 p q]` the keep fact of `%s4t` (`q`'s code keeps its own sure registers until its result entries' blocks).  `%10` is not started (12.30).
   32. **The cons with flagged parts: the fronts and `pairLS_ok`** (2026-11-27, thirty-fifth session, `CompFrontSX.lean`, `CompPairLSXFronts.lean`, `CompPairLSXCore.lean`, `CompPairLSXSem.lean`, `CompPairLSX.lean`).  The statement was tested first (scratch `DisTest.lean` on the executable model of `CompCall.lean`: 75 nomms over `[0 1]`, `[0 2]`, `[0 3]`, `[4 [0 2]]`, `[3 [0 3]]`, `[4 [0 1]]`, `[1 5]`, `[2 [0 3] [0 2]]` and cons, 30 flat lazy goals, 2 jumps, 2 states: 4,136 runs).  Two facts hold on every run: (G) when a goal's entries are apart from its sure registers, so are the result's entries from the result's sure registers; (P2) under such a goal, a result entry at a GOAL label is apart from the goal's sure registers.  P2 at all labels is false: 108 runs, every one a cons with a hinted tail, whose park entry `(g''.bo, ofSure hed.sure)` holds the goal's own registers (`[[0 1] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]` under the sure need `[this 0 this 1]` has the result entry `(5, this 0)`); so `FrontsS.toFront`'s `hLE` (at every result entry) is not what a flagged cons can give, and `FrontsS.toFrontG` asks it at the goal's labels only, which is all the field `front` reads.  `frontS_pairX`: the parts' state-level fronts compose at one goal label when they read their goals' sure registers `Ep`, `Eq`: these are the parent's own (`+split-need` of `pair a b`, of `both r a b` where `+must` allocates nothing) and then held because the parent's `E` holds the product, or fresh (`this r` split into `h`, `t`; `both r a b` where `+must` allocates `r'`), and then held by the halves `H` in the witness (`pairWitX`: `d = pair H (pair dp dq)`, `w = [[vp vq] [wp wq]]`); the copy's moves and the head's ops keep them (`hEp`, `hEq`), and the halves lie below the tail's start apart from the parts' entries and the goal entry's halves (`hH`).  `pairLSX_ok`: `pairLS_ok` with the parts' `RunOKLS` at any flags `xp`, `xq` and the whole's at any `x`, witness `pairWitX C HW`, copied by script (four files), with `hA`/`hAB` keeping `HW` through the block `o` (`Keep … ∨ NMem HW`: `o` holds `+split-need`'s `con`, which writes only the goal's register) and five new hypotheses: `HW` in the split's range (`hHr`), the halves hold it (`hHh`), the parts' goal registers held from the parent's and `HW` (`hEH`), the goal's entries apart from its sure need (`hG`, fact G), and the parts' result entries at the goal's labels apart from `HW` and the goals' registers (`hPH`, fact P2).  No statement of an existing lemma changed.  Where it stops: the cons lemma in `LOKS` form.  `LOKS.run` neither assumes G of its goal nor concludes G and P2 of its result, so `hG` and `hPH` cannot be discharged from the parts' `LOKS`; the next step is a relation `LOKSX` (`LOKS` with G among the premises and G, P2 at goal labels among the conclusions; for index-`true` nomms P2 is empty, `gsE false = none`), proved for every constructor of `CallTreeA` (for `[4 p]`, `[3 p]`, `[5 p q]` the result has no entry at a goal label: `p` runs under a goal with no entry), then the cons at flag `xp || xq` with `HW` the fresh halves of the flagged parts (`hHr`, `hHh`, `hEH` and the keep of `HW` by the block `o` from `splitNeed`'s cases), then the class.  The index of `CallTreeA.cons` is unchanged (`b2 && (b1 || !q.pure)`).
   33. **`LOKSX`: the relation for the cons with flagged parts, and its leaves** (2026-11-27, thirty-sixth session, `CompPairLSXCheck.lean`, `CompLOKSX.lean`, `CompLOKSXUnary.lean`).  The checker of 12.32 is now a file of the track with `#guard`s, and it runs the exact statement: (G) of the result, (P2) at goal labels and the stronger (P3) at goal labels (the entry reads only the goal's entry at its label and the run's registers, `g0.re ≤ r < g1.re`) hold on all 4,136 runs of the old nomms and on 2,312 runs of 49 shapes of `[5 p q]` and `[7 p q]` alone and under a cons; P3 holds without G.  `LOKSX x` is `LOKS x` with G of the goal among the premises of `run` and P3 among its conclusions (`AtGoalRegs`); P3 with G and the goal's registers below `re` gives P2 (`AtGoalRegs.p2`), and P3 composes where P2 does not (the registers of a part's entry are in a known place, not only away from one need).  G of the result is not a conclusion: a result is a goal again only for the `p` of `[7 p q]`, which meets `LOKS false` (no G premise) while a flagged `p` is out of scope; the checker says it holds, for a later `[7 p q]` with a flagged `p`.  P3 is flag-free and semantics-free, so it is a separate fact `ApartS` and `LOKS.toX` makes `LOKSX` from `LOKS` and `ApartS`.  Proved: `[0 axe]` (`axe ≥ 1`, the result entry is `+from` of the goal entry at its label, `apartS_n0`), `[1 k]` (no entry), `[4 p]`, `[3 p]`, the indirect call, `[5 p q]` (generated from the openings of their `LOKS` runs: the operand or the inner pair runs under a goal with no entry, so its result's labels are above the start, `apartS_four`, `apartS_three`, `apartS_indirect`, `apartS_five`; `[3 p]` at a goal that asks nothing runs `p` under the goal itself and takes `p`'s `ApartS`), `[7 p q]` (`apartS_seven`: `p`'s entry at a goal label is at a label of `q`'s result, P3 of `p` then of `q`; it takes both parts' `LOKS` at any flags for the labels and the premises at `q`'s result), with `lokSX_n0`, `lokSX_n1`, `lokSX_four`, `lokSX_three`, `lokSX_indirectF`, `lokSX_five`, `lokSX_seven`.  Where it stops.  (1) `ApartS` of the cons: the result is `+copy` of the head's and the tail's results, the tail under `+split`'s half of the goal, the head under the other half or the park goal; P3 needs, per goal entry, that `splitL_flat`'s halves read the entry's registers or the split's fresh ones, that `+past`'s park entry is at a new label, and that `copyL_flat'` keeps each label's registers or allocates fresh ones.  (2) Then the cons lemma at flag `xp || xq` (`pairLSX_ok` with `hG` from the premise, `hPH` from the parts' P3 and the ranges: `HW` and the halves of the entries come from `+split` below the tail's start, the parts' fresh registers from their starts on), `HW` from the cases of `splitNeed`.  (3) The class: `ApartS` for every nomm of `CallTreeA` needs (1); for `[7 p q]` with `q` of index `false` it needs `q`'s run facts at any goal, which `LOKSF` does not give, but `LOKSX true` of `q` does at goals with G.  The index of `CallTreeA.cons` stays `b2 && (b1 || !q.pure)` (index `true` means `LOKS false` at every goal, and the cons with a flagged part is flagged), so the flagged cons adds no nomm to the class whose top contract is new: a non-flag-free goal arises only as `q`'s result for the `p` of `[7 p q]`, which needs index `true`.  The instance `[7 [[3 [0 2]] [4 [0 3]]] [2 [0 3] [0 2]]]` does not need the flagged cons: `q`'s result has no entry (`[2 [0 3] [0 2]]` runs its pair of call-free parts under a goal with no entry; 24 runs of the checker's goals, none with an entry), so `p` runs at a flag-free goal where `LOKSF` (`lokS_consF`) is enough; what it needs is a constructor of `[7 p q]` with `q`'s results entry-free and `p` of any index.

   | `+trim-trace-hints` | `optC`, `23` | 1135 | `+get:ja`, `+gut:by`, `+uno:by` (axis `4` of the door of `+by`, `2` of its battery: `uno_by`, `Hoon/UnoBy.lean`), `+max` (`max_gate`), `+got:by`, `+put:by`, `+del:by` (`del_gate`), `+lte`, `+dec`, `+key:by` (`key_loop`), `+uni:in` (`uni_in`), `+has:in`, `+rep:by`, `+roll`, `+put:in`, `+run:by` (`run_by_P`, `Hoon/RunByP.lean`), `+skip` (`skip_gate`), the bunt of `pole` | `trimTraceHints` (`Ska/OptTrim.lean`) | the counts of open hints are a map of key nouns that never holds `0` (`CntRel`, the model a function `Cnt`), the unsafe and opened keys sets of key nouns (`UnsRel`, `OpRel`), `out` a map of maps (`OutRel`) | where the model returns: `tr_gate` (`OptLinkTrimLink.lean`), so `TrimLink` holds of the model (`trimLink`) |
   | `+remove-dead-code` | `optC`, `724` | 295 | `+got:by`, `+flop`, `+gas:in` (`gas_in_loop`), `+has:in`, `+put:by`, `+get-regs`, `+get-reg-safe-assignment` | `removeDeadCode` (`Ska/OptDead.lean`) | `saw` is a list; it runs `+get-regs` on terminators, so a link takes blocks with no `%lti` | `rdc_gate` (`OptLinkDead.lean`) |
   | `+switch-chains` | `optC`, `182` | 2916 | `+bb-topo`, `+rev-cfg`, `+get-regs`, `+rep:by`, `+got:by`, `+gut:by`, `+put:by`, `+del:by`, `+key:by`, `+gas:in`, `+has:in`, `+put:in`, `+tap:in`, `+get:ja`, `+roll`, `+turn`, `+weld`, `+lent`, `+scag`, `+slag` (`scag_gate`, `slag_gate`), `+rear`, `+snip` (`rear_gate`, `snip_gate`), `+sort` (`sort_gate`, against `qsort`; the arms of `+sort`, `+rear`, `+snip` in `Hoon/SwArms.lean`), `+skip`, `+bex` (`bex_gate`), `+lth`, `+gte`, `+div`, `+mul`, `+sub` (`Hoon/Arith.lean`), `+sy` | `switchChains fuel` (`Ska/OptSwitch.lean`) | fuel bounds its loops; `uses` is a map that never holds `0` (`UsesRel`), `done` and `saw` sets (`SawRel`); `nex` folds over the keys of `+tap:in`, the model over those of `rap` | where the model returns: `sw_gate` (`OptLinkSw*.lean`), so `SwitchLink` holds of the model (`switchLink`) |

   **The inventory of `+switch-chains`** (2026-11-24, before its proof; nouns
   by `tools/gen/optlink.py switch`, `Compiler/OptLinkSwNouns.lean`).  The
   gate body (2900 cells) is a chain of `=/` and `=*`; the phases, in the
   order of the Hoon, with their cells and the model each corresponds to
   (`Ska/OptSwitch.lean`):

   | Phase | Noun | Cells | Calls | Model |
   | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
   | `topo` | in `swBodyCut` | 12 | `+bb-topo` (`bb_gate`) | `bbTopo fuel` |
   | `rev` | in `swBodyCut` | 28 | `+rev-cfg` (`rev_gate`), `+sy` (`si_gate`) | `revCfg bl topo` |
   | `uses` | `swUses` (gate `put` `swPutB`, `+rep:by` gate `swRepG`, `+roll` gate `swOpG`) | 229 | `+rep:by`, `+roll`, `+get-regs` (`gr_gate_pole`, `gr_gate_fin`), `+put:by`, `+gut:by`, the bunt of `pole` | `usesOf bl` (a count, so the order of `+rep:by` does not matter) |
   | gate `test-of` | `swTOB` (cut at each test: `swTOClq`, `swTOY`, `swTOYb`, `swTOEqq`, `swTOBod`, `swTOHM`, `swTOH`, `swTOM`, `swTOS`, `swTOC`, `swTOLth`, `swTOEq`, `swTOUses`) | 519 | `+got:by`, `+get:ja`, `+lth`, `+bex`, `+gut:by` | `testOf` |
   | gate `chain-of` | `swCHB` | 153 | `test-of`, `+got:by`, `+get:ja`, itself | `chainOf` (fuel) |
   | gate `plain-of` | `swPOB` (`swTOEqq`, the trap `swPOMT` of `m`, `swPOC`) | 276 | `+got:by`, `+lth`, `+bex`, `+gut:by` | `plainOf` (`immInto` for the trap) |
   | gate `plains-of` | `swPSB` | 148 | `plain-of`, `+got:by`, `+get:ja`, itself | `plainsOf` (fuel) |
   | gate `cases` | `swCSB`, trap `swCST`, sort gate `swCSSG` | 122 | `+has:in`, `+put:in`, `+sort` (`sort_gate`, `qsort`), `+lth` | `casesAcc`, then `hsort` with fuel the length |
   | gate `tree` | `swTRB` (`swTRLeaf`, `swTRSplit`, `swTRRec`) | 245 | `+div`, `+lent`, `+slag`, `+scag`, `+put:by`, itself twice | `stree` (fuel) |
   | `nex` | `swNex`, gate `swNexG` | 71 | `+key:by` (`key_by`), `+tap:in` (`tap_in`), `+roll`, `+gte` | `nexOf` (the fold is `max` of `o + 1`, so the order of `+tap:in` does not matter) |
   | the walk | `swWalk` (`swWHas`, `swWCs`, `swWGte`) | 1016 | `+has:in`, `chain-of`, `+lent`, `+gte` | `switchWalk` |
   | a tagged chain | `swWTag` (`swWTIds`, `swWTR`, `swWTDel`, `swWTCas`, `swWTFin`) | 391 | `+rear`, `+snip`, `+turn`, `+weld`, `+gas:in`, `+roll`, `+del:by`, `cases`, `tree`, `+put:by`, `+got:by` | `switchTag` |
   | a plain chain | `swWPlain` (`swWPB`, `swWPR`, `swWPDel`, `swWPCas`, `swWPNed`, `swWPFin`, the trap `swWPIdT` of `ids`, `swWPNode`, the `+skip` gate `swWPSkG`) | 534 | `plains-of`, `+lth`, `+lent`, `+rear`, `+turn`, `+gas:in`, `+roll`, `+del:by`, `cases`, `+mul`, `+sub`, `+scag`, `+gte`, `tree`, `+got:by`, `+skip`, `+weld`, `+put:by` | `switchPlain` (`freshIds` for the trap) |

   Every library arm it calls has a link (`Hoon/Lists*.lean`, `Hoon/Arith.lean`,
   `Hoon/Bits.lean`, `Hoon/Maps.lean`, `Hoon/Sets.lean`, `Hoon/DelBy.lean`,
   `Hoon/MapFolds.lean`, `Hoon/TapIn.lean`), `+sort` against `qsort` of nouns,
   which `hsort` of the model must be shown to agree with.  The comparison of
   the Hoon and the model, phase by phase, found no difference on blocks
   without `%lti` (the input of the pass): chains are found in the order of
   `topo`, both skipping the labels of `done`; `test-of`, `chain-of`,
   `plain-of`, `plains-of` make the same tests in the same order (a crash is a
   `+got:by` of a missing label, `none` in the model); `uses` counts the
   mentions, so the order of `+rep:by` is immaterial; `cases` keeps the first
   case of each constant (a set `saw`, the model a list) in reverse order and
   sorts it by `lth` on the constants with the quicksort of `+sort`, which
   `hsort` writes out (`partition` is the `+skid`), so ties cannot arise (the
   constants left are distinct) and stability does not matter; `tree` splits at
   `(div (lent all) 2)`, takes the labels of `ids` two by two in pre-order (left
   subtree first), and fails (`?>`) on fewer than two labels, as `stree`; the
   fuel of the model bounds `+bb-topo`, `chain-of`, `plains-of` and `tree`
   (`hsort` takes the length of the list, `freshIds` takes `ned + 1`, both
   enough); `nex` is one more than the largest label of the blocks before the
   pass (`+roll` over `~(tap in ~(key by blocks))` of `?:((gte o m) +(o) m)`,
   the model the same fold over the keys of `rap`, equal since the fold is the
   maximum of `o + 1`), and a plain chain takes its labels from the blocks it
   drops first (`(scag ned old)`) and then from `nex` upward, each new one put
   in front (`freshIds`).  The only difference is the known one of
   `+get-regs` on `%lti`, which the input does not have.  Every phase has
   been linked since (below, and the table rows of `Compiler/OptLinkSw*.lean`):
   the proofs of `test-of` and `plain-of` are equalities with the model, so
   they confirm these comparisons on every input.

   `+get-ipdom` and `+get-idom` (axes `10` and `44` of `optC`) are not run by
   `+optimize`.  The orders: every `+rep:by` of a pass is a fold over
   `MapKeys.keys`, which on the treaps of `TreapMap` must be the keys of
   `rap` (pre-order), so the link needs the instance of `Opt.MapKeys` on
   `TreapMap mugF` with those keys; `+roll` over `topo` and over `+get-jmps`
   is `foldl`/`foldlM` in list order in the models.  The crashes (`got` of a
   missing block, the `?>` and `!!` of the passes) are the models' `none`.

   What is linked.  Exact, crashes included: `+remove-useless-branching` on
   every treap of blocks (`rub_gate`) and `+remove-dead-code` on blocks with
   no `%lti` (`rdc_gate`); `+rev-cfg` with any set of atoms for `topo-set`
   (`rev_gate`, the set related to the list by `SawRel`); the four helpers;
   `+sy` (`si_gate`).  Where the fuelled model returns: `+bb-topo`
   (`bb_gate`), `+remove-hops` (`rh_gate`) and `+remove-empty-middle`
   (`rem_gate`), each by induction on the fuel; the structural loop of
   `+optimize-once` (`oo_loop`); **the whole gate of `+optimize-once`**
   (`oo_gate`, `OptLinkOnceGate.lean`: on a straight function whose blocks
   have no `%lti`, where `optimizeOnce fuel P` returns `(again, s')`, the
   gate returns `[again s']`, given `AliasLink P` and `TrimLink P`; the rest
   of the body after the loop, `ooAfter`, is cut at each `=^` and `=.` in
   `OptLinkOnceAfter.lean`, each step stated as the subject it leaves);
   **`+optimize`** (`opt_gate`, `OptLinkOpt.lean`: where `optimizeF fuel P
   s` returns `s'`, the gate returns the noun of `s'`, given `AliasLink P`,
   `TrimLink P` and `SwitchLink P`, the hypothesis on `+switch-chains`; its
   trap by induction on the fuel against `optLoop`); **`+trim-trace-hints`**
   (`tr_gate`, `OptLinkTrim*.lean`: where `trimTraceHints` returns, on any
   treap of blocks, list `topo` and jar `rev`), so `TrimLink` holds of the
   model (`trimLink`) and `oo_gate_trim`, `opt_gate_trim` leave `+alias`
   (and `+switch-chains`) as the only hypotheses; **`+switch-chains`**
   (`sw_gate`, `OptLinkSw*.lean`: where `switchChains fuel` returns `bs` on a
   treap of blocks with no `%lti`, the gate returns the noun of `bs`), so
   `SwitchLink` holds of the model (`switchLink`) and **`opt_gate_sw` leaves
   `AliasLink` as the only hypothesis of `+optimize`**, which `al_link`
   discharges (`opt_gate_alias`: no hypothesis on the passes).  The phases of
   `+switch-chains`, each its own theorem, in the order of the Hoon: `uses`
   (`sw_uses`), `test-of` and `plain-of` exactly, crashes included
   (`sw_test`, `sw_plain`), `chain-of`, `plains-of`, `cases`, `tree` where
   the fuelled model returns (`sw_chain`, `sw_plains`, `sw_cases` with
   `hsort_qsort`, `sw_tree`), `nex` (`sw_nex`), a tagged chain and a plain
   chain of the walk (`sw_wtag`, `sw_wplain`, each cut into steps stated as
   the subject they leave), the walk (`sw_walk`) and the gate; its gates
   hold the `topo` they were made with while the walk edits its own, so the
   walk's subject is `swWk` over `swSp`.  It took one session (about 2900
   cells, eleven files); no step needed more than a few seconds or the 3 GB
   cap, every `simp` working on one named piece with the subject unfolded
   down to `optC` and `optC` generalized.  On the way: `+uno:by`
   (`Hoon/UnoBy.lean`, `uno_by`: the general union, the relation `UnoByR`
   with the branches of `+uni:by`, on two maps the value at each key the
   merge of the two) and `+run:by` with a gate known only on the values
   (`Hoon/RunByP.lean`, `run_by_P`).  The Hoon and the models agree on every
   arm linked so far; for `+trim-trace-hints` the counts per key (a key at
   count `1` or less is deleted, so the map never holds `0`, which is the
   model's function `Cnt` with `0` for an absent key), the order of the walk
   (`topo` in list order, the predecessors of `rev` in list order, joined by
   `max`, which commutes) and the test `pole-not-crashing` (the same
   fourteen tags) were compared first.  Sets of the Hoon are related to the
   models' lists by `SawRel` (a set of atoms whose members are the list's),
   the jug `readers` of `+remove-empty-middle` by `JugRel`, the maps of
   counts by `CntRel`; the loops are linked by `roll_gate_rel` and
   `rep_gate_rel` when the accumulator holds such a set, by `roll_gate_list`
   and `rep_gate_list` when it is the noun of the model's state, and by
   `rep_gate_fwd` for a fuelled step.  A lesson on memory: one `simp` over
   all of `ooAfter` went past the 3 GB cap, while the same proof cut into
   eight steps (each `unfold`ing one or two pieces at the formula position,
   with `optC` and the block nouns generalized) checks in about a second.

   **The plan of `+alias`** (2026-11-27, before its proof; nouns by
   `tools/gen/optlink.py alias`, `Compiler/OptLinkAlNouns.lean`, every one
   tied to the pill by `al_at_cut`).  The arm (6048 cells) is a gate whose
   body (6029) pushes the bunt of `gen` (`alGenBunt`, 7 cells), sets `rev.gen`
   (axis `382`) to `alRevInit` (90) and runs the arm `$` (axis `95`) of a
   `\|^` core with battery `alBat` (5917).  The subject of the walk is
   `[alBat [GEN [alBodyCut [[nargs blocks rev topo] optC]]]]` (`gen` at `6`,
   `topo` at `247`, `blocks` at `122`); `GEN` is `[new old info imms
   info-local imms-local rev folded]`.  The phases, each its own theorem, in
   the order to link them (the helpers first, since the phases call them):

   | # | Phase | Noun | Cells | Calls | Model |
   | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

   | 2 | `join-reg` | `alJRegB` | 177 | `+int:in` (`int_in`) | `joinReg` |
   | 3 | `param-info` | `alPInfoB` | 134 | `+murn`, `+same`, `+roll`, `+lent`, `join-reg` | `paramInfo`: done, `al_pinfo` (`UInfoRel` on the units) |
   | 4 | `join-info` | `alJInfoB` | 300 | `+mor`, `+gor`, `+uni:by` (`UniByR`), `join-reg` | `joinInfoA`: done, `al_jinfo` (loop relation `JIR`, `ji_loop`, `jiP_of_maps`; maps by `InfoMapRel`) |
   | 5 | `join-imms` | `alJImmsB` | 340 | `+int:in`, `+uni:by` | `joinImmsA`: done, `al_jimms` (loop relation `JMR`, `jm_loop`, `jmP_of_maps`; jugs by `ImmsRel`, an empty set dropped) |
   | 6 | prelude | `alGenBunt`, `alRevInit` | 97 | `+run:by` (`run_by_P`), `+bake`, `+list`, `+silt` (`silt_gate`) | the start state of `aliasM` (`rev` by `JugRel` to `dedupN` of the jar): done, `al_revinit`, `al_prelude` (the body is `alWalk` on `alGen0 R0`) |
   | 7 | end of the walk, `pre` | in `alWalk`, `alPre` | 7 + 35 | `+get:ju`, `+tap:in` (`tap_in`) | `aliasWalk []`; `g.rev o` up to order: done, `al_end`, `al_pre`, `al_pre_perm` (`OptLinkAlStart.lean`) |
   | 8 | unreachable block | `alUnT`, `alUnB` (`alUnRoll`) | 136 | `+got:by`, `+get-jmps`, `+roll`, `+del:ju` | the first branch of `aliasBlock`: done, `al_unt`, `al_unreach` (`OptLinkAlStartUn.lean`, given `RevNodup`) |
   | 9 | facts at the start | `alInfo` (`alEntry` 69, `alJoin` 608: `alJnRoll` 81, `alJnPreB`, `alJnRef` 512 with `alJnBrn` 207, `alJnBrz` 173, `alJnClq` 87) | 687 | `+put:by`, `+got:by`, `join-info`, `+jab:by` | `entryInfo`, `aliasStart`: done, `al_ent`, `al_jnroll` (`OptLinkAlStartInfo.lean`), the refinement `al_jnref` (`OptLinkAlStartRef.lean`, the `%brz` case with `brzInv_loob`), `al_info` (`OptLinkAlStartAll.lean`) |
   | 10 | `imms` and the two `=.` | `alImms` | 89 + 10 | `+got:by`, `join-imms` | `aliasStartImms`: done, `al_imms` (`OptLinkAlStartImms.lean`); with phase 9 and the two `=.`, **`al_start`** (`OptLinkAlStartAll.lean`: `GenRelL g info imms` on `g.rev o`) |
   | 11 | `bob` | `alBob` | 21 | `+got:by` | `HoonMap.get bl o`: done, `al_bob` (`OptLinkAlStart.lean`) |
   | 12 | parameters | `alPars` (`alEdges` 112, trap `alParT` 203) | 329 | `+zing`, `+turn`, `+murn`, `+get-jmps`, `+got:by`, `+put:by`, `param-info`, `+flop` | `aliasEdges`, `aliasPars`: done, `al_edges` (`OptLinkAlPars.lean`), `al_part`, `al_pars_perm`, `al_pars` (`OptLinkAlParsTrap.lean`; statement below) |
   | 13 | body | `alBody` (gate `alBodyG` 2336, one noun per op: `%imm` 154, `%mov` 46, `%inc` 181, `%con` 349, `%hed` 191, `%tal` 191, `%cel` 100, `%lob` 135, `%equ` 47, the hints 5 to 29, `%spy` 81, `%nok` 81, `%cal` 74, `%caf` 76, `%cam` 76, `%csl` 106, `%csf` 69, `%csm` = `alOpLast` 76) | 2409 | `+roll`, `+flop`, `rn`, `+get:ju`, `+put:ju`, `+put:by`, `+got:by`, `+jab:by`, `+int:in`, `+put:in`, `+turn` | `aliasOp`, `aliasBody`: done, `al_body`, `al_body_gen` (`OpOK` per case, `opOK_all`; the gate `al_bodyG`; the roll `body_fold`; `%imm`, `%con` for `pick = Ana.pickR FinMap`, the other cases for every `pick`) |
