   | 16 | walk, gate | `alWalk`, `alBodyCut` | | `al_prelude`, `al_end`, `al_turn` | `aliasWalk` by induction on `topo`, `aliasM`, then `AliasLink`: done, `al_walk`, `al_gate`, **`al_link`** (`AliasLink` for `P.alias = aliasM (Ana.pickR FinMap)`, no hypothesis), `opt_gate_alias` (`OptLinkAlWalkGate.lean`) |

   Linked so far: phase 1 (`al_rn`, `OptLinkAlRn.lean`), phase 2
   (`al_jreg`, `OptLinkAlJReg.lean`, with `InfoRel` and `infoN`), phase 3
   (`al_pinfo`, `OptLinkAlPInfo.lean`), phase 4 (`al_jinfo`,
   `OptLinkAlJInfo.lean`), phase 5 (`al_jimms`, `OptLinkAlJImms.lean`) and
   phase 6 (`al_revinit`, `al_prelude`, `OptLinkAlPrelude.lean`: the gate
   of `(bake silt (list @uwoo))` is built by running the mold `(list @uwoo)`
   on its default `~`, which returns `~` without calling `@uwoo`).  The
   state of the walk is `GenRel g N` (between blocks) and `GenRelL g loc
   imml N` (inside a block) of `OptLinkAlState.lean`, the subject `alWS`;
   the phases after 12 (the body, the end) state their subjects with them.
   Then phases 7, 8, 10 (`imms`) and 11 (`OptLinkAlStart*.lean`, nouns cut
   by `optlink.py alstart` into `OptLinkAlNounsStart.lean`) and, of phase
   9, the entry trap and the `+roll` of `join-info` (`al_ent`,
   `al_jnroll`).  The order of `pre`: the Hoon's list `preH` (`al_pre`) has
   no duplicates and the members of `g.rev o`, so with `RevNodup`
   (`aliasM_inv`) it is a permutation of `g.rev o` (`al_pre_perm`); each
   phase is linked against the model run on `preH`, and `aliasStart_perm`,
   `aliasStartImms_perm` with `infoMapRel_of_aInfoEq`, `immsRel_of_immsEq`
   move the result to `g.rev o`.  Phases 9 and 10 are done as one theorem,
   `al_start` (`OptLinkAlStartAll.lean`), with the refinement `al_jnref`
   (`OptLinkAlStartRef.lean`: its `%brz` case reads `is-loob`, which
   `InfoRel` fixes since `brzInv_loob` gives `loobImm = false`).  Left of
   phases 7 to 12: phase 12
   (`alEdges`, the trap `alParT`).  The
   loops of `join-info` and `join-imms` went through as `int_loop` did: one
   `nock_open` of the trap and one `simp` per constructor; the builders
   follow `intP_of_sets` (induction on `uniSz a + uniSz b`), the order
   facts of the four `+uni:by` branches closed by `grind` after
   `kin_cutR`/`kin_cutL`/`kin_node`.  The
   pattern that kept each step small: cut the body into named fields in
   `optlink.py` (the field nouns of `join-reg` are `alJRegF1` to
   `alJRegF8`), prove one fact per field with only that field unfolded
   and the case split it needs, and assemble with `nock_cons_of`.

   The relations (`OldRel` and `InfoRel` are defined, the rest to come):
   `HoonMap.toNoun` exactly for `new.gen` and the result; `OldRel` for
   `old.gen`; `InfoRel N x` for an `info-reg` noun `N` and an `InfoReg` `x`:
   the units and `is-cell` equal, `hed-of` and `tel-of` sets with the members
   of the lists (`SawRel`), and `is-loob` equal to `x.isLoob` up to
   `x.loobImm` (`isLoob' || x.loobImm = x.isLoob || x.loobImm`), which is
   what `join-info` leaves (header 2 of `Ska/OptAliasModel.lean`); a map of
   them (`IsMap`, the same keys) for `info-local` and each entry of
   `info.gen`; `imms` a `jug` with the members of `AImms` (an empty set
   absent); `rev.gen` by `JugRel` to `g.rev`, whose lists have no duplicates
   (`dedupN`, then `erase`).  What the comparison of the Hoon and the model
   found (no difference beyond the header of `Ska/OptAliasModel.lean`): the
   Hoon's `pre` is `+tap:in` of a set, the model's `g.rev o`, the same
   members in another order, so the link of phases 9, 10, 12 needs on the
   model side that `aliasStart`, `aliasStartImms`, `aliasEdges` with
   `aliasPars` give related results on two orders of `pre` (`joinReg`
   commutative and associative up to the members of the lists and
   `isLoob` up to `loobImm`, `paramInfo` of a permutation); the refinement
   after a `%brz` reads `is-loob`, where `InfoRel` alone does not fix it, so
   the walk must carry the invariant that a `%brz` end of `new.gen` has a
   test register with no `has-imm` in `info.gen` (true: `aliasFin` keeps or
   makes a `%brz` only then); `pick` is chosen as the root of the canonical
   set of the list (`isMap_ext`: the treap of a set depends only on its
   members), which meets `hpick`, so `AliasLink` is discharged for `P.alias
   = aliasM pick`; `NoLti` of the result is a model-side fact (an end is
   kept or becomes `%hop`, `%brz`, `[%bom ~]`).

   The converse (track H, 2026-11-27, `Compiler/OptLinkTotal*.lean`): that
   the fuelled model returns.  The fuelled passes are monotone in the fuel
   (`OptLinkTotalMono.lean`), so with the converses of `OptLinkFuel.lean`
   each returns one result past a bound on the fuel, which a loop needs since
   the driver runs every pass and loop with the same fuel.  The invariant of
   the structural loop is `StInv ρ L` (a rank `ρ` that falls along every jump,
   `Closed`, `ArityOK`, a block `0`, two-way ends that pass no arguments,
   labels in a list `L`) and its measure `mu ρ L` the triple (blocks, weight
   of the two-way ends, sum over the jumps of `ρ` of the target),
   lexicographic.  The weight (`twoW`) is `2` for a `%brn` end and `1` for
   the other two-way ends, because `+alias` reports a change when it turns a
   `%brn` whose test register is known to be a loob into a `%brz` (`aliasFin`,
   the case `hasImm = none`, `isLoob`), which keeps the blocks, the kinds of
   two-way ends and the targets.  With every two-way end counted `1`, `mu`
   stayed the same on that change while the loop went on: `xbBlocks` of
   `AuditOptTotal.lean` (`[%lob 1]` then `[%brn 1 1 2]`, two arguments).  The
   real compiler does the same (`tools/hoon/xbcheck.hoon`, 2026-11-27):
   `+alias` returns `folded=%.y` with `[%brz 0v1 [~ 0w1] [~ 0w2]]` and the
   `%lob` kept, a second `+alias` returns `%.n`, and `+optimize` returns
   after two rounds of `+optimize-once`.  With the weight, `mu` falls from
   `(3, 2, 17)` to `(3, 1, 17)` there (`#guard`).

   Each structural pass keeps `StInv` with the same `ρ` and `L`, does not
   raise `mu`, and lowers it when it reports a change (`hops_inv_step`: a
   merge drops the merged block, `hops_drop`; `rub_inv_step`: a fold turns a
   two-way end into a hop; `mid_inv_step`: a rewritten jump lands at a
   strictly lower rank, and the end keeps its kind and so its weight,
   `mf_facts`), so **`structLoop_total`** holds with no condition on the
   model.  The same holds for `+remove-dead-code` (`dead_step`).  The outer
   loop is proved under **`PassTotal P ρ L J`** (`OptLinkTotalOnce.lean`):
   `DriverHyp` at every fuel with `J` and `TopoInvR` (each pass keeps `J`,
   and `topo`, `rev` keep `TopoInvR`), `+alias` returns on blocks with
   `StInv`, `J` and `TopoInvR`, keeps `StInv`, does not raise `mu` and lowers
   it when it reports a change, `+trim-trace-hints` returns, keeps `StInv`
   and does not raise `mu`, and `+switch-chains` returns.  Then
   `optimizeF_total`, `optHalts_of` and `opt_gate_total_of`.

   `PassTotal` is discharged for the passes of the Hoon at `J = DInvR` by
   **`passTotal_of`** (`OptLinkTotalPass.lean`) up to two named facts.  The
   field `drv` is `driverHypR_of`.  For `+alias`, `aliasM_step` proves the
   rest where the model returns: its ends are the old ones renamed, folded
   to a hop on an old target, a `%brn` made a `%brz`, or `[%bom ~]`
   (`afin_facts`: old targets with the old arity, no arguments on a two-way
   end, weight and rank sum not above, the flag set only with a lower
   weight), block `0` is kept (`alias_zero`), and a change drops a block or
   lowers the weight of one (`aw_flag`).  `+trim-trace-hints` returns on a
   `topo` with `TopoOK` and keeps `StInv` and `mu` (`trim_step`, from
   `trim_shape`).  **`opt_gate_total`**: for the passes of the Hoon
   (`aliasM (Ana.pickR FinMap)`, `trimTraceHints`, `switchChains sfuel`), on
   blocks with `StInv ρ L` and `DInvR`, given `AliasRet` and `SwitchRet`,
   some fuel makes `optimizeF` return `s'`, and the pill's `+optimize`
   returns the noun of `s'`.

   Second session (2026-11-27).  `SwitchRet` is proved
   (`OptLinkTotalSwitch.lean`, **`switchRet_of`**): `switchChains sfuel`
   returns on blocks with `StInv ρ L` once `ρ o < sfuel` for every `o ∈ L`.
   The fuel bounds `+bb-topo` by `ρ 0`, and `chain-of` and `plains-of` by
   the rank, because each step follows the no side of a test, a jump.  The
   tree over `k ≥ 1` cases takes `2 (k - 1)` labels and depth `k`, and both
   rewrites give it enough (the tagged chain `2 (|chain| - 1)` labels,
   `freshIds` at least `2 (k - 1)`), with `k` at most the length of the
   chain, which is at most the fuel.  `DInvR` is not used.

   **`AliasRet` is false as stated** (`OptLinkTotalAlias.lean`,
   **`not_aliasRet`**), so `opt_gate_total` holds vacuously.  The one block
   `[%cel 1] [%bom ~]` has `StInv`, `DInvR` and `TopoInvR` (it defines no
   register, so `DefFirst` and `DefOnceR` hold), but with `n = 1` the model
   looks up the facts of register `1`, which neither the entry nor a
   definition gives, and crashes, as the Hoon's `got:by` does.  `DefFirst`
   speaks only of registers in `allDefs`, and the arguments of the entry
   (`entryInfo n`, registers below `n`) are read without a definition, as in
   `xbBlocks`.  The field missing from `DInvR` is **`ReadsIn n bl`**: every
   register a reachable block reads is in `allDefs bl` or below `n`.  Also,
   a `pick` that picks no member makes the model crash (`wPick` in the
   audit), so the corrected statement **`AliasRetN`** (open) is for a `pick`
   that picks a member, with `ReadsIn n`.  Restating `opt_gate_total` with
   it needs `PassTotal` at one `N = s.nargs` (the loop keeps `nargs`) with
   `J = DInvR ∧ ReadsIn N`, so every pass must keep `ReadsIn N` (a new
   obligation, open) and raw `+comp` output must have `ReadsIn nargs`
   (track D).

   The places where the model of `+alias` returns `none` are listed in the
   header of `OptLinkTotalAlias.lean`: the block of `o` (`TopoOK`), the
   facts, immediates and new block of every predecessor in `g.rev o`, a
   one-predecessor `%brn` or `%clq` with no target `o`, the facts of its
   test register, an edge with fewer arguments than parameters, the facts
   of an argument, of a register an op reads, and of the test register of
   an end, and a two-way end with arguments.  Proved: the op and end cases
   (`aliasOp_some`: an op returns once every register it reads has a fact
   under `old`, `aliasFin_some`: an end returns once a two-way end passes
   no arguments and its test register has a fact).  The walk invariant is
   checked with `#guard` at every step of the walk on `xaBlocks`,
   `xbBlocks` and `xhBlocks` (`walkInvB`): every predecessor of the next
   block is walked, a walked predecessor of an unwalked `o` has its new
   block, facts and immediates, jumps to `o`, and has a fact for the test
   register of a one-predecessor two-way end, the facts of a walked block
   refer only to registers with facts, and a kept walked block has a fact
   for `old r` for every `r` below `n` or defined on every path to it.
   Left for `AliasRetN`: the proof of that invariant by induction on the
   walk, which needs besides that every walked block that is kept is
   reachable and that `old r = r` until the one definition of `r` is walked
   (`DefOnceR`), and that the immediates refer only to registers with facts.

   **What the input must satisfy** (for track D).  By `stInv_of`,
   `StInv ρ (MapKeys.keys bl)` follows from `Acyclic`, `Closed`, `ArityOK`,
   a block `0` and `TwoNA`, and `opt_gate_total` asks `DInvR` (= `OptIn`)
   besides.  Of these, raw `+comp` output has by track D `Acyclic`, `NoLti`,
   `ParNodup` and the rest of `OptIn` (with `DownRk` and `ArgsAboveR` on the
   blocks of the generator).  It does not yet have `Closed` (every jump goes
   to a block, which `got` needs), `ArityOK` (every jump has the arity of its
   target, which `+eval-pass` needs), `TwoNA` (the two-way ends pass no
   arguments, the `?>` of `+remove-useless-branching` and of `aliasFin`) or
   the block `0` (the entry).

   **The input list of the final `opt_gate_total`** (third session,
   2026-11-27, for track D; the statement being proved on
   `dozreg/formalization-ska-h`).  For `s : Straight (TreapMap mugF)`, with
   `bl = s.blocks`, exactly: `Opt.Acyclic bl`, `Closed bl`, `ArityOK bl`,
   `(HoonMap.get bl 0).isSome`, `TwoNA bl` (these five give
   `StInv ρ (keys bl) bl` by `stInv_of`), `Opt.DInvR bl` (which holds
   `NoLti`), and `ReadsIn s.nargs bl` (`OptLinkTotalAlias.lean`: every
   register that a block reachable from `0` reads, by an op or by its end,
   `Opt.ReadAt`, is in `Opt.allDefs bl` or below `s.nargs`).  Nothing else:
   the fuel of `+switch-chains` is chosen above `ρ` on the keys
   (`switchRet_of`), and `pick` is `Ana.pickR FinMap`, which picks a member.
   The block `[%cel 1] [%bom ~]` with one argument (the witness of
   `not_aliasRet`) crashes the real `+alias` and `+optimize` too
   (`tools/hoon/undefread.hoon`: with `n-args = 2` `+alias` returns the
   block unchanged, with `n-args = 1` `+mule` of `+alias` and of `+optimize`
   gives `%.n`).  It is hand-built: it reads register `1`, which is neither
   an argument nor defined, and raw `+comp` output is expected never to do
   that, since `+comp` reads only registers it has written on the path or
   the `n-args` registers of the subject; `ReadsIn s.nargs` is that fact.

   Third session, further.  Every place of the list in the header of
   `OptLinkTotalAlias.lean` is closed at the level of one kept block, as a
   hypothesis on the state of the walk at `o`: **`aliasBlock_some`**
   (`OptLinkTotalBlock.lean`), from `aliasBody_inv` (`OptLinkTotalBody.lean`:
   the ops in order, with closed facts `AClo`, immediates with facts `IClo`
   and a set `S` of registers with facts under `old`), `aliasStart_inv`
   (`S1` .. `S5`), `aliasStartImms_inv` (`B3`), `aliasEdges_inv` (`E1`) and
   `aliasPars_inv` (`I1` .. `I3`, `OptLinkTotalStart.lean`).  Left for
   `AliasRetN`: the induction on `aliasWalk` that gives those hypotheses at
   every kept block from `W0` .. `W3` and the input.  Concretely, with
   `S r` = `r ∈ b.par`, or `r < n` and `r ∉ allDefs` at the entry, or `r`
   defined on every path to `o` before `o` (`DefFirst` splits every read into
   these and an earlier op of the block), the invariant must carry: a
   predecessor in `g.rev o` is walked and kept, reachable, with its new block
   jumping to `o` (`W0`, `W1`, from `RevSound`, `TopoOK` and `delJu` only
   removing); its frozen facts are closed with immediates with facts
   (`aliasBlock_some` gives that for the block just walked); a fact under the
   current `old` for every register defined on every path to it, kept since
   `old` changes only at the registers a later block writes, which by
   `DefOnceR` are defined only there, so not on every path to an earlier
   block (`W3`); the arguments of its jumps to `o` have facts and are at
   least `|par o|` (`afin_facts` with `ArityOK`, and the end's reads are in
   `S` by `ReadsIn`); `old` the identity on every register not yet written
   (`DefOnceR` again).  Then `PassTotal` at one `N` with
   `J = DInvR ∧ ReadsIn N`, the per-pass `ReadsIn N` obligations, and the
   restated `opt_gate_total` with the two instances are still to do.

   Fourth session (2026-11-27): **`AliasRetN` is proved**
   (`aliasRetN_of`, `OptLinkTotalWalkStep.lean`), for every `pick` that
   picks a member, on exactly the input list above (`StInv` gives `ArityOK`
   and `TwoNA`, `DInvR` gives `DefFirst`, `NoLti`, `DefOnceR`, `TopoInvR`
   gives `TopoOK` and `RevSound`; nothing new is asked of track D).  The
   walk invariant is `WInv` (`OptLinkTotalWalk.lean`), stated on the prefix
   `pre` of `topo` walked; it speaks only of blocks with facts, which are
   reachable, so `ReadsIn` is used only at reachable blocks (dropped blocks
   may read anything).  Its decidable form `winvB` holds at every step of
   the walk on `xaBlocks`, `xbBlocks`, `xhBlocks` and on all 4,080 raw
   outputs of `enumX` (`#guard` in `AuditOptTotal.lean`, among them
   `enumX[340]` with unreachable blocks).  The register facts last because
   `old` changes only at registers a later block writes, and such a
   register is neither written nor defined on every path at an earlier
   kept block (`later_dst`: by `DefOnceR` the path would pass through the
   later block, which `TopoOK` puts after).  Left for the restated
   `opt_gate_total` (the form `GateTotalN` of track D's `RawInvStX.lean`):
   the driver calls `+alias` at `s.nargs` on the output of the structural
   passes of each round, so `PassTotal` must be had at one `N = s.nargs`
   with `J = DInvR ∧ ReadsIn N`, which needs every pass of `DriverHyp`
   (`+remove-hops`, `+remove-useless-branching`, `+remove-empty-middle`,
   `+alias`, `+trim-trace-hints`, `+remove-dead-code`) to keep
   `ReadsIn N`, and the field `alias` of `PassTotal` stated at `N` only
   (open).  Checked in a scratch run: on all 4,080 raw outputs of `enumX`,
   with the model passes, `ReadsIn nargs` (on the reachable blocks) holds
   on the input of `+alias` and on the output of every round of
   `optimizeOnce` (up to six rounds), so that `J` is the right one.

14. **Raw `+comp` output meets the optimizer's invariant** (track D,
   2026-11-24, `Ska/RawInv*.lean`, `Ska/AuditRaw.lean`).  The driver theorem
   `optimize_keeps_optIn` of 12.8 asks `OptIn` of the raw blocks (the field
   `rawDInv` of `CompHypOf.lean`, stated there with the weaker `DInvS`, which
   `dInvR_dInvS` gives).  The inventory, field by field, before any proof.
   "The first fragment" is the call-free, `%6`-free fragment, the nomms on
   which `run` of `CompModel.lean` returns (`Frag` of `CompFrag.lean`: `%0`,
   `%1`, `%3`, `%4`, `%5`, cons, `%7`, `%10`); `FragX` of `CompFragX.lean`
   adds `%6`.  The final pass is `toStraightF`: on the fragments it takes its
   renaming path (`kc_run`, `toStraightF_eq`), which renames registers by the
   bijection `renReg r0 n`, moves the entry block to `0`, drops the
   bypassable blocks (no parameter, no body, a hop) and chases the jumps past
   them (`chase`); before it, `+coerce-lazy` (n-ary, `finishLF`) or `+kerf`
   (whole subject, `finishLU`) puts the prologue ops in front of the entry.
   A check of all eight fields by executable checkers (`RawInvCheck.lean`)
   on 5,200 enumerated nomms of `FragX` (both versions) and 120 with
   indirect calls (`finishLUC`; `enumC` has 120 nomms, not 360 as said here before): no field fails.

   * `parNodup`.  Parameters appear only on join blocks.  Even without `%6`
     there is one: `+forl` (cases `%3` and `%5` under a `%next` goal that is
     not none-equivalent, after `+collapse-lazy-atom`) emits `barg` with the
     one parameter `[r]`, and `if0`, `if1` hop to it with `[r0]`, `[r1]`
     (`[4 [3 [0 1]]]`: `0w2 [v2] hop 1`).  So "no `%6`, no parameters" is
     not so, but on the first fragment every block has at most one
     parameter and the field is trivial.  With `%6`: `+fork-need`'s join has
     the registers of the goal's sure need (`flattenNeed`, distinct by
     `Foot.dist`/`FootR.dist` of track C), `+sect` prepends fresh registers
     `tn`.  The final pass renames injectively (`renReg_inj`).
   * `passOK`.  The jumps with arguments: `if0`, `if1` pass `r0`, `r1`,
     allocated after the collapse register `r`, the parameter of `barg`;
     the fork blocks of `+fork-need` pass the fresh registers of
     `forkNeedGo` to the goal's registers; `+sect` (`lensHop`) passes the
     proxies' definitions `df` to fresh `tn`.  Mechanism: arguments are
     allocated after the target's parameters.  The final pass: `chase`
     returns a jump with arguments unchanged (one to a bypassable block
     fails the pass), renaming is injective.
   * `acyclic`.  Code is built backwards from the continuation: a block is
     emitted after the blocks it jumps to, so on the first fragment every
     jump goes to a lower label (`run_down`, `Down`, `CompDown.lean`).  With
     `%6` under `%next` the fork blocks `o0`, `o1` hop up to the join, which
     is allocated after them (`GrowU`, `upRk`, `downRk_of_growU`,
     `CompGrowU.lean`).  The final pass: `chase` follows hops downwards in
     the rank, the entry moves to `0` with the rank of its old label, and no
     kept jump can go to the old entry label (`chase` fails on it).
   * `downPar`.  No argument of a jump is a parameter of a block reachable
     from its target: `r0`, `r1` are allocated after every block reachable
     from `barg` exists, and those blocks' parameters were allocated before
     (`BelowIn`, `run_keeps`, `CompBelowRun.lean`); no block's parameters or
     end change after it is emitted on the first fragment (`Grow.keep`).  The
     final pass: a path of the output is a path of the raw blocks through
     the bypassed hops.  With `%6`, `+sect` changes the join's parameters
     after the fact (`jabBlock`), the place to look.
   * `trimBr`.  No trace hint op (`%hdp`/`%hde` of `%spot` or `%mean`,
     `%hmp`, `%hme`, `%hcp`, `%hce`) is emitted without `%11`, which `runL`
     and `runLC` do not compile, so `K` is empty everywhere and the field is
     trivial for every nomm the models compile (`brOp` keeps the empty
     stack on every other op).  Renaming keeps the op kinds.
   * `defFirst`.  The run for a goal `%next laz th` makes code whose paths
     from its entry to `th` define every register of `laz` that it does not
     hand up in its result's need, and which reads only registers it defined
     before or registers of its result's need; the prologue of the final
     pass defines the result's need at the entry from the input registers,
     which nothing defines.  Stated through liveness: `Live bl k r`, a path
     from `k` that reads `r` before any definition of it; `DefFirst` holds
     when no register with a definition is live at `0`.  Track C has the
     footprint of the writes (`Foot.wr`, `FootR.wr`) but no fact on paths.
   * `noLti`.  Syntactic: `+comp` never emits `%lti`, and `chaseFin` is
     `none` on it, so the output of the renaming path never has one,
     whatever the raw blocks.
   * `defOnce`.  Registers come from the counter `re`: each one is written
     by the arm that allocated it, or by the run whose goal names it, and a
     run writes no register of its result's need (`Foot.wr`), which the code
     before it writes (`+copy`'s moves, `+split`'s `con`, the prologue).
     The fills of `+crash-need` write fresh registers or the goal's, never
     the register they test since af5cc39 (the model has the `+murn`).
     Track C gives the set of written registers (`WroteIn`), not that each
     is written once.  The final pass: `+kern-need` and `+sure-loop` write
     the need's registers once each and fresh ones; bypassed blocks define
     nothing; renaming is injective.  `DefOnceR` follows from `DefOnce` over
     all blocks (`defOnceR_of_defOnce`), and the proof aims at that.

   What the final pass can break: nothing of the eight, given what the
   raw blocks of the run have; the facts to carry through it are the
   injectivity of `renReg`, that `chase` only skips empty hops, and that the
   prologue writes only the result need's registers and fresh ones.

   **Done (2026-11-24, track D, `Ska/RawInv*.lean`).**  On the first
   fragment (`Frag nm CGoal.done`: `%0`, `%1`, `%3`, `%4`, `%5`, cons, `%7`,
   `%10`; no call, no `%6`, no hint), with nothing baked, the raw blocks
   meet all eight fields: **`optIn_finishLF`** (n-ary, any input shape `ord`
   and cape), **`optIn_finishLU`** (whole subject), so `rawDInv_finishLF`,
   `rawDInv_finishLU` (`DInvS`, the form of `rawDInv` in `CompHypOf.lean`)
   and `optimize_keeps_finishLF`, `optimize_keeps_finishLU` (`+optimize`
   with the passes of the Hoon keeps that code).  No field is false; the
   checkers of `RawInvCheck.lean` agree on enumerated nomms of `FragX` and
   with indirect calls.  The proof in three layers:
   (1) the run-level invariant **`run_raw`** (`RawIH`): from a state with
   `SI` (labels below `bo`, blocks that jump down, registers below `re`, one
   definition per register over all blocks, no trace hint op, at most one
   parameter, jump arguments above the parameters at or below their
   target) whose definitions avoid the goal's registers, a run keeps `SI`
   and leaves live at its entry only registers of its result's need or
   registers live at the goal's continuation that the goal does not name;
   the arms each say what they define once, read first and leave live
   (`raw_split`, `copyNeed_raw`, `raw_copy`, `raw_collapse`, `raw_forl`,
   `raw_mede`, `raw_into`, ...), the facts of track C about the sub-runs
   (`run_keeps`, `run_foot`, `run_down`) are used as they are;
   (2) the prologue (`raw_sureLoop`, `kernLoop_raw`): it defines the run's
   need from the input registers and reads first only those;
   (3) `+to-straight` (**`optIn_toStraight`**): liveness, definitions,
   reachability and ranks pass through the bijective renaming and the
   chase of the bypassed hops.  `DefFirst` is stated through liveness
   (`defFirst_of_live`: no register with a definition live at `0`).
   A `%6` at the top (track C's class `Frag6`, the n-ary version):
   **`optIn_finishLF6`**, `optIn_finishLF_frag6`, `rawDInv_finishLF_frag6`,
   `optimize_keeps_finishLF_frag6` (`RawInvClosed*.lean`, `RawInvCut.lean`,
   `RawInvSix.lean`).  The robust liveness asked below was not needed in
   its general form: `run_closed` (the test's code jumps only into itself or
   to the two branch blocks), `run_above` of track C (that code mentions
   only registers from `gs.re` on), `RunOK.ext` of track C (a fork-free run
   leaves the blocks below its start as they were, so the branch blocks and
   the code of `a` and `b` are untouched by the run of `c`), `live_cut` and
   `liveAv_region` give it for this case: a register live at the entry is
   read on a path that avoids the branch blocks, so it is the test's own
   (then in the test's need, which the entry's `+sure-loop` defines), or it
   is live at a branch block, whose `+sure-loop` reads only inputs.

   **`FragX`, 2026-11-27 (`RawInvCheckX.lean`, `RawInvLiveK.lean`,
   `RawInvOutR.lean`, `RawInvTopX.lean`).**  Checked on 4,080 nomms with a
   `%6` below the top (`enumX`): no field of `OptIn` fails on either final
   pass, so no Hoon test was needed.  Three fields of `SI` are false there:
   `JDown` (the fork blocks hop up to the join, `[4 [6 [0 1] [0 1] [0 1]]]`),
   `Par1` (the join has two parameters, `[7 [6 [0 1] [0 1] [0 1]] [[0 3]
   [0 2]]]`) and, more to the point, the liveness cut at the lazy entries
   that the obstacle paragraph below proposes: in `[[0 3] [6 [0 6] [0 2] [0
   1]]]` the head's register is defined at the entry and read by the `%con`
   after the join, so it is live at both fork blocks though no lazy need
   holds it.  The spec that holds (`checkRunX`) follows paths from the entry
   through the lazy entries with kills: `LiveK bl K entry r` is false for
   every `r` when `K` gives each lazy entry the registers of its sure need;
   with the ranked forms `DownRk (upRk bl)` and `ArgsAboveR (upRk bl)` of
   `JDown` and `ArgsAbove` and distinct parameters per block in place of
   `Par1`.  Proved: the final pass from these facts,
   **`optIn_toStraight_prep`** (through `optIn_toStraightR`, `live_prep`),
   and the composition step **`liveK_prep`** for a parent's ops in front of
   a sub-run's lazy entries.  Step (i) of the run-level induction is done
   (2026-11-27, second session, `RawInvGenP.lean`, `RawInvFragX.lean`): the
   arm lemmas and `run_rawP` take `SIP X ρ lo` (jumps down in a rank, no
   parameter field) in place of `SI`, `run_raw` is its instance, and
   **`run_rawU`** runs the first fragment from a state whose blocks hop up
   (`SIP X (upRk g0.blocks) g0.bo`).  Left for `FragX`: (i') the run-level
   induction (`RawIHX`) itself.  Its first obstacle: `run_rawU` speaks of
   `Live` (no kills), but a first-fragment run whose goal's continuation is
   the code of a run with `%6` (the head `p` of a cons whose tail has the
   `%6`: `p` runs for the goal `q`'s entry) must hand up liveness with the
   kills of that code's lazy entries, `LiveK g1 K entry r → NMem need r ∨
   (LiveK g0 K th.there r ∧ ...)` for kills `K` that vanish from `g0.bo` on;
   with `Live` the kills are lost at the continuation.  So the liveness
   half of `run_rawP` should be restated over `LiveK K` with such `K` (the
   same proofs, with `LiveK` forms of `live_iff`, `live_unfoldRk`,
   `live_frameRk`, `live_prependRk`, `not_live_bom`, ...); this is done
   (2026-11-27, third session: `run_rawPK`, `run_rawUK`), and so is part of
   (i'): `RawIHX` is stated (`RawInvIHX.lean`, checked under `%next` and
   `%pick` by `checkGoalX`) and proved for the cases `frag`, `sevenFrom`,
   `seven`, `four` and `three` of `FragX`, and (fourth session,
   `RawInvIHXBin.lean`) cons, `%5` and `%10`.  For those `RawIHX` now asks
   at most 255 `%6` (as every `FragX` theorem of track C does) and returns
   the result's jump (no arguments, a label in `[1, g1.bo)`; checked by
   `checkGoalX`).  The second run's result may have forks, but the entries
   of the two results lie apart (`labs` of `runX_keeps`), so `+copy` is the
   copy of the sure needs (`copyL_apart_some`) and no `mergeForks` case
   arises; the moves of the copy block define the registers of both sure
   needs that the merged need does not keep, so the kills of the two roots
   (not entries of the result) do not touch a register live after it
   (`LiveK.monoB`).  (i') is done (fifth session, `RawInvIHXSix.lean`):
   `%6` (`rawX_sixP` through `sixBody_apart`, `rawX_sixN` through
   `sixNextSimple_apart`, a shared tail `rawX_sixTail`) and the induction
   **`runX_rawIHX`**, for every `X` with `XSix` (`XOK`, blocks-only, kept by
   the three blocks of `+fork-sure`); `RawIHX` now also asks a clean goal,
   since the frames of the branch runs come from track C's `runX_ok`.
   The sixth session (`RawInvXup.lean`, `RawInvCoerceP.lean`,
   `RawInvFinishX.lean`) put the parts together.  The concrete predicate is
   `Xup` (the label-order `ArgsAbove`, a block that hops up has no
   parameter, no jump with arguments targets a block that hops up), with
   instances `XOK` and `XSix` and `argsAboveR_upRk_of_xup`; for it
   `EmitOK` got the field `nup` and `XOK.put`, `XSix.fork` the fact that
   the old blocks jump below the new label (`SIP.jlt`).  `raw_coerceP` is
   `raw_coerce` from `SIP`, `coI_step` the one-entry step, and
   **`optIn_finishLFX_of`**, **`optimize_keeps_finishLFX_of`** give
   `OptIn` and `Opt.Keeps` of `finishLF` on every nomm of `FragX` with at
   most 255 `%6`, given `LazOK` of the top run's result: the sure needs of
   the root and of the entries of its lazy tree are pairwise disjoint and
   no block of the run defines their registers (both checked on `enumX`:
   `disjB`, `lazyOKB`; the `+coerce-lazy` prologues define each entry's
   need, so without them `Cross` fails).  The seventh session
   (`RawInvEApart.lean`) proved it: track C's footprint `FootR` gives that
   the run writes none of those registers (`wr`) and that the root's need
   is apart from each entry's (`apart`), and the one missing part, the
   entries' needs pairwise apart, is a separate induction **`runX_eap`** in
   the shape of `runX_foot` (the entries of two sub-runs lie in disjoint
   ranges of registers or in disjoint goals: `KeepX.out`, `FootR.esure`,
   `forkSure_spec`).  So **`lazOK_run`**, and **`optIn_finishLFX`**,
   **`optimize_keeps_finishLFX`** hold on every nomm of `FragX` with at most
   255 `%6` and nothing baked, with no hypothesis on the run; instances:
   a `%6` under a cons and nested `%6`s (`optIn_finishLFX_consSix`,
   `optIn_finishLFX_sixSix`).  The eighth session (`RawInvOutN.lean`,
   `RawInvKernX.lean`, `RawInvWholeX.lean`) did the whole-subject pass:
   **`optIn_finishLUX`**, **`optimize_keeps_finishLUX`** on every nomm of
   `FragX` with at most 255 `%6`, no hypothesis on the run (instances
   `optIn_finishLUX_consSix`, `optIn_finishLUX_sixSix`).  There `+kern`
   reads every lazy block other than the hop block of `+kerf` through a
   proxy, so `+to-straight` takes its map path (on all of `enumX`), which
   renames by `ren ∘ unproxy`, not injective; by `renameBlocks_ren` of
   track C its output is the renaming path of the unproxied blocks
   `mapVals (renBlob u)`, and those are the raw blocks plus the hop block
   with the walk's ops unproxied in front (`kernList_prep` over
   `walkLazy_flat`; a register read first after a renaming is the image of
   one read first before it, `opsRF_ren_back`), while unproxying fixes
   every defined register (no defined register is a key of `cond`), so
   `optIn_prepN` applies.  For the totality of the optimizer (Gaps 13,
   `StInv`): `Closed`, `ArityOK`, `TwoNA` and the block `0` hold of the
   output of both final passes on all of `enumX`, and the first three of
   the run's raw blocks (`#guard`s with `stB`, `okSt` in
   `RawInvCheckX.lean`).  The ninth session (`RawInvStX.lean`) proved the
   passage through the prologues and `+to-straight`, so that only a fact
   of the run is left.  `Closed` and the block `0` need nothing of the raw
   blocks: `+chase` fails on a label with no block and returns a block that
   is not bypassable, which the fold keeps, and the entry is kept at `0`
   (`stOut_ren`).  `ArityOK` and `TwoNA` come from the raw blocks, since a
   chased jump is the jump itself or the hop of a block it passed
   (`chase_rawR`) and in branch mode passes only hops without arguments
   (`chase_nil`).  So **`optInput_finishLFX_of`** and
   **`optInput_finishLUX_of`** give `StInv ρ (keys)`, `DInvR` (which holds
   `NoLti`) and `ReadsIn nargs` (the field of `AliasRetN`, since
   `not_aliasRet`) of both final passes on every nomm of `FragX` with at
   most 255 `%6`, given **`RunAT`**: the top run's raw blocks meet
   `ArityOK` and `TwoNA` (proved on `FragX` by `runAT_fragX`, see below;
   checked by `stB` on `enumX` and, under `%next` and `%pick` from states
   with continuation blocks, `atGoalB`).  `ReadsIn`
   comes from the liveness at the entry that `OptIn` already used
   (`readsIn_of_live`); it holds only of reachable blocks (`readsInB`: an
   unreachable fork block of `enumX[340]` passes a register nothing
   defines), as its statement asks.  The instantiation of track H's
   totality is the corollary `opt_total_finishLFX` (`opt_total_finishLUX`)
   from `GateTotalN`, the expected form of the restated `opt_gate_total`,
   with `AliasRetN` and `RunAT` the other hypotheses.  The tenth session
   (`RawInvAT.lean`, `RawInvATX.lean`) proved `RunAT` on `FragX`
   (**`runAT_fragX`**), so the `_at` forms **`optInput_finishLFX_at`**,
   **`optInput_finishLUX_at`** and **`opt_total_finishLFX_at`**,
   **`opt_total_finishLUX_at`** have no hypothesis on the run (the last two
   ask only `GateTotalN` and `AliasRetN` of track H).  The induction
   carries one relation `GA` between the states before and after a run:
   the blocks below the old `bo` keep their parameters and ends both ways,
   a block at a new label meets the arity of its targets in the final map
   (`BAT`) and jumps below its `bo`, and nothing lies above `bo` that was
   not there.  It composes because a new block's targets lie below the end
   of its part, so later parts keep their parameters; a step that makes no
   label is one (`ga_quiet`), and so is an `+emit` of a block that meets
   `BAT` where it is put (`ga_emit`).  Every emitted jump is `Fresh` (no
   argument, a target with no parameter: a goal jump, a block just
   emitted, a sub-run's result by `run_down` or by the induction) except
   two kinds: `+forl`'s `if0`, `if1` pass `[r0]`, `[r1]` to `barg`, whose
   parameter is `[r]` (`ga_forl`), and `+fork-need`'s fork blocks pass
   `flattenNeed n0`, `flattenNeed n1` to the join, whose parameters are
   `flattenNeed ned`, as many by `NCopy.length` (`ga_forkSure`); `+sect`
   keeps the blocks of `sixGenL` (`SameBlocks`).  No statement turned out
   false; the induction is proved, so `atGoalB` stays the check of its
   statement.  Left on track D: indirect calls, (b) below.  With indirect calls the
   facts of `RunAT`, of `StInv` and `ReadsIn` hold on all 120 nomms of
   `enumC` on both final passes (`RawInvCheckC.lean`), so the statements
   carry over; the proof needs the induction on `runLC` of (b), and for
   `RunAT` the `GA` of the arm of `%2` (`+kerf`'s hop, `+kern`'s ops, the
   `%nok` block).

   **Left, in this order.**  (a) `%6` (`FragX`; `Frag6` is done; see the
   paragraph above for what holds and what is proved).  Two obstacles, both met
   on the way here.  First, the liveness spec of a run is about the state
   at its end; with `%6` the final pass (and `+split`, `+copy` of the
   parents) put ops in front of lazy blocks inside a run's code after the
   run (the `yes`/`nuh` blocks of `%6`, every fork entry), so the spec must
   be robust: for every map that agrees with the run's blocks on its labels
   `[g0.bo, g1.bo)`, a register live at the entry is in the result's need
   or live at a goal target (or at a lazy entry).  It follows from the
   present spec with two facts more: the run's blocks jump only to its own
   labels or to its goal's targets (an induction like `run_down`), and they
   mention only fresh registers and the goal's (`run_keepsA` of track C);
   then a cut lemma (a path either stays in the run's labels or reaches a
   target) gives the robust form.  With it, `Frag6` (a `%6` at the top,
   under `%done`) is near: `runL_six_done` of track C splits the run, and
   the final pass's `+coerce-lazy` puts one `+sure-loop` at the entry and
   one at each branch block.  Second, under `%next` a `%6` returns a lazy
   need with forks, so every arm above it runs on `Lazy` (`splitLoop`,
   `copyLazyL`, `fromL`, `walkLazy` in `+mede`, `+collapse-lazy-atom` and
   `+kern` with proxies, `+fork`/`+sect` with `insertHop` and the threaded
   parameters, `lazyShut`), each needing its own `raw_` lemma over the lazy
   entries, and `toStraightF` takes the map path when proxies exist (the
   renaming `ren ∘ unproxy` is not injective, but proxies are never
   defined, only read, so `DefUniq` should still pass).  `+fork-need`'s join
   is where `ParNodup`, `PassOK`, `DownPar` and `Acyclic` (with `upRk`) get
   content: its parameters are the goal's need (distinct), the fork blocks
   pass fresh registers and hop up.  `Par1` and `ArgsAbove` must be replaced
   there (several parameters; arguments allocated after the join's
   parameters, ranked by `upRk`).  (b) Indirect calls: the arm of `%2`
   (`runLC_n2_none`: `+simple-next`, `+kerf` with `+kern` into the goal's
   need from a fresh `pro`, the `%nok` block, then the two runs and the copy
   as in `raw_binTail`) fits the present spec; nested calls need the
   induction on `runLC`, most simply by making the case lemmas take the
   runner as a parameter with its unfolding equations.  Estimate: `Frag6`
   took about 1300 lines (closure, cut, the top case); `FragX`, as much
   again as what is done here (about 5000 lines), most of it the lazy arms.
15. **The model-side lemmas of the link of `+alias`** (track E, 2026-11-27,
   `Ska/OptAliasPerm.lean`, `Ska/OptAliasBrz.lean`, `Ska/OptAliasPickRoot.lean`,
   `Ska/AuditAliasE.lean`).  The four facts that Gaps 13 asks of the model
   are proved, each on any input (`AliasLink` assumes only `NoLti` of the
   blocks).  (1) The order of `pre`.  Under a permutation of `pre` the
   results are not equal (`aliasStart_order`: `joinReg` keeps the order of
   its first `hedOf`), but they are related by `InfoEq` at each register
   (`aliasStart_perm`), by `ImmsEq` (`aliasStartImms_perm`) and, for the
   edges, by `List.Perm` (`aliasEdges_perm`), and the parameter loop maps
   permuted edges and related facts to related facts (`aliasPars_perm`,
   `aliasPars_edges_perm`, from `paramInfo_perm`).  `InfoEq` is exactly
   what `InfoRel` reads (`infoRel_of_infoEq`).  So the link of phases 9, 10
   and 12 proves the Hoon's nouns related to the model run on `preH`, the
   atoms of `~(tap in ...)`, and moves the relation to the model's
   `g.rev o`: `preH` is a permutation of it by `perm_of_nodup`, with
   `RevNodup` of the model (`aliasM_inv`) and the nodup of the tap list on
   the noun side (`tapList_set_nodup`).  The phases after read
   the facts only through `InfoRel` and the members of the sets, so the
   model's own order is never compared with the Hoon's.  (2) `%brz`.
   `BrzInv` holds at every state of the walk (`aliasM_inv`), and
   `brzInv_loob` gives `loobImm = false` for the facts the refinement after a
   single predecessor's `%brz` reads, where `InfoRel` (its last clause, or
   `InfoEq.isLoob`) fixes `isLoob` exactly.  (3) `pick`.  The choice is
   `Ana.pickR FinMap` (with the local instance `Ana.tkeyNat`): a set noun
   related to a non-empty list by `SawRel` is the noun of the `+silt` of the
   list (`isSet_ext`), whose root is that key (`sawRel_root`); it meets
   `hpick` (`pickR_mem`) and depends only on the members (`pickR_ext`).  So
   `AliasLink` is to be discharged for `P.alias = aliasM (Ana.pickR FinMap)`.
   (4) `NoLti`: `aliasM_noLti`, the first conjunct of `AliasLink`, with no
   hypothesis beyond `NoLti` of the input (`alias_nolti` of
   `OptAliasResult.lean` needed `TopoInvR`).
16. **The measure of the pass loop** (track F, 2026-11-27,
    `Ska/PassMeasure.lean`, `tools/hoon/passmeasure.py`).  Tried first to
    break it on the real compiler: an instrumented copy prints, per run, how
    each field of the entry changed, per pass loop its passes, and the members
    `+cull` drops.  Over 2,269 calls of `+ska-callgraph` (the formulas of
    `thread-a/b/c`, self-calls with swapped and rotated samples, mutual
    recursion, products run as formulas or called again, 1,300 random
    recursive cores, 21 library calls with `(ream '(add 1 2)')`): every pass
    loop ended, and no run went against the measure.  Open point (a) happens:
    151 runs changed only the map, 100 of them with the product `|+~` (an axis
    to `~`, `runDatum_dunno_prod` proves it is the only change there), 51 with
    a product that knows a cell (provenance lost under a `|` leaf of its cape,
    or spelled out along its cells, `1` to `[2 [6 7]]`).  Open point (b) never
    happened: 21 members culled, none analysed again.  The first component is
    corrected: `less-code` went from `[[& &] |]` to `[& |]` six times (same
    nouns, one cell fewer), so it rises in `CapeLe`, where `&` is above every
    cell (`capeLe_uni_right`), not in cape size.  Not proved: the map for a
    product that knows a cell (the measure: leaves of the fixed product's cape
    whose map is not `~`, then cells of the cape at which the map is an atom),
    the mask through `+app:ca` against `bus` (that the cape of the result
    rises in `CapeLe` and lies in a finite set below `bus`), the merge of
    `indi` and `seek`, (b) (a culled member restarts from the bunt entry; the
    data says no call site comes back to an identity it left, no invariant
    says so yet), and the sum over the component, so `CgHalts` is still a
    hypothesis.  The deepest stack seen at a new function was 366 (`ream`),
    31 on the random cores.
    Since (`Ska/PassMeasureK.lean`): `runDatum_measure` proves the step of
    an entry after its first run, on a tidy `bus`, with the old product not
    `%void` and normal and the old map `PiNorm`: `less-code` stays at most
    `kS bus` and rises in `kS`, else the product falls in `kS`, else the
    map falls in `MapLt` over the product's cape, else `indi` stays or is
    merged (`+lack:ca` of `+msg-ca` with the old one), and with `indi` the
    same `seek` stays or rises in `CapeLe`.  A first finding: the step of
    `less-code` needs `Tidy bus` (data `0` under every `|`); on
    `[[& |] [1 7]]` the mask `[& |]` keeps the `7`, `[& &]` drops it with the
    same cape, and a `want` of `|` brings it back (`less_untidy_example`), so
    an identity whose subject sock is not tidy could change forever if its
    `want` alternated.  Every sock operation seen keeps `Tidy`, but the
    model of the analysis does not carry it yet.
    Since (`Ska/PassMeasureT.lean`): no untidy sock occurs.  On the real
    compiler, over the same corpus (13,297 runs), every subject sock,
    product, `less-code` and callee subject sock was tidy and every map
    `PiNorm` (`passmeasure.py` checks it at every run).  No poke plants one:
    the analysis takes socks from outside only at `&+sub` of `%full` and from
    the memo of earlier pokes, which only `+ska-poke` writes; `%fast`
    templates (clammed with their junk by `+validate-ovum`) are normalized by
    `+register-template` and never become subjects, `%dire` writes only the
    compile caches (`tools/hoon/untidyin.hoon`).  In the model every sock
    operation keeps `Tidy` and `PiNorm`, so does every run of `fol-loop` with
    a table whose answers at tidy subjects are tidy (`folTopR_tp`), and every
    write of `GStepT` (`GStepR` with the tidiness side conditions) keeps
    `GraphTP` (`GStepT.tp`); `runDatum_measure_graph` drops the hypotheses
    `Tidy bus`, `NormSock old.prod`, `PiNorm old.map` on such a graph.
    `seek` as numbers (`Ska/PassMeasureS.lean`): with the first four
    components the same, a change of `seek` (by `+msg-ca`) lowers the pair
    (cells, `|` leaves) of its cape (`runDatum_seek_lex`).  For `indi`,
    `+lack:ca` of a normal `dark` is idempotent (`lack_idem`), so the old
    `indi` is its own `+lack:ca` of `dark` (`runDatum_indi_lack`); a number
    needs a weight relative to `dark` (cells of `indi` can grow: `|` under a
    `dark` of `[& |]` becomes `[| &]`).
    Since (`Ska/PassMeasureA.lean`, `PassMeasureI.lean`, `PassMeasureE.lean`):
    every arm of the state-passing analysis keeps `TInv` (graph `GraphTP`,
    memo `MemoTP`) from a tidy root and a tidy memo of earlier pokes
    (`allPT`, `skaCallgraph_tp`), over a copy of the bridge whose subjects
    are well formed and tidy (`bridge_stepQ`), and the write of `+run` is a
    `GStepT.run` (`runEnd_gStepT`).  `indi` is a number: `+lack:ca` of
    `dark` is monotone (`lack_mono`), the merge makes cells only within the
    old cape and `dark`, and `indiW` (a weight within that shape) falls at
    every change (`indi_merge_step`).  An old product `%void` is the top of
    the product order (`mergeProd_old_void`).  One entry: `entryM` (eight
    numbers, lexicographic, well founded) falls at every run that changes
    the five fields `changed` compares (`runDatum_entry_step`,
    `Ana.run_entry_step` on the analysis), so it changes finitely often
    (`entry_finite`), from an old entry that is `RunW` (`less-code` a mask
    of `bus`, `indi` normal and fixed by `+lack:ca` of `dark`).  `RunW`
    holds of the bunt entry and of every entry `+run` writes when the run's
    `indi` is normal.  A memo hit writes a `less-code` found under another
    subject, but only at an identity that is not pushed (`analyzeStep`
    returns it), so it is never run.  `passmeasure.py` now checks this at
    every run: over 13,297 runs (6,464 after the first) the old
    `less-code` was always a mask of `bus`, the old `indi` always fixed and
    the new one normal, and 680 runs had an old product `%void` (512 kept
    it, 168 left it).
    Since (`Ska/PassMeasureN.lean`, `Ska/PassMeasureP.lean`): **the pass
    loop ends on the model, with two hypotheses named** (`passOn_wf`).  A
    pass that goes on (`PassOn`; `passLoop_of_on`: it is the pass loop
    calling itself) raises `next` or leaves it, and with `next` the same the
    member loop runs on the same stack (`allN`: every arm keeps `next` or
    raises it, and `resolve`, `+run`, the member loop and `+analyze` keep
    the stack when they keep `next`), every member's value (`valE`: the
    tuple `entryM` of its entry, `none` once `+cull` drops it) stays or
    falls, and one falls, since `changed` is the change of the five fields
    (`runDatum_changed`, `memberLoop_le`, `passOn_inv`).  The measure is
    `B - next`, then the members' values pointwise; `passOn_wf` rules out an
    infinite sequence of passes by `ev_const` (a bounded `next` stops
    growing) and `fam_finite` (finitely many non-increasing values in a
    well-founded order, one falling at every step).  Named: `RunStkW` (a
    `+run` keeps every stack member's entry `RunW`; the writes give it by
    `runW_bunt`, `runW_runDatum`, open are the `indi` of `fol-loop` normal
    and the subject of a pushed identity not `%void`, which needs the
    guard of `callStep` carried through the copy of the bridge), and a bound
    `B` on `next` along the passes.  `next` counts pushes, and a push is at
    an identity not in the graph, so `B` follows from finitely many
    identities (Kruskal over `+he-sock`, argued in `Ska/AnaEnds.lean`) and
    (b) (a member that `+cull` drops is not pushed again); without (b) a pass
    could push an identity that `+cull` then drops, again and again, and a
    bound on the identities alone would not give `B`.  The data
    (`passmeasure.py --passes`): of 5,058 passes that reached `%pm-end`,
    2,446 went on, 947 with a push and 1,499 with `next` the same, each of
    those with every run kept or lowered, one lowered and no more members
    in the next pass; none against the measure (62 more passes returned a
    lowlink below the index, so they do not go on).
    Since (`Ska/PassMeasureW.lean`, `Ska/PassMeasureF.lean`): **`RunStkW`
    is proved** (`run_winv`, by `allPW`, every arm at every fuel): the
    invariant is `WInv` (every entry of the graph and of the memo of the poke
    has a normal `indi`, every stack member's entry is `RunW`).  `fol-loop`
    keeps `gen.indi` normal when its table's answers have a normal `indi`
    (`folR_ni`, with `normCape_distribute`); the subject socks given to
    `resolve` are not `%void` (the guard of `callStep`, now carried by `hAS`
    of the bridge copy; `msgSock_ne_void` for a `%he` turn), so a push writes
    a `RunW` bunt entry; a memo hit writes at an identity off the stack; `+pop`
    and `+cull` take members off.  `passOn_wf_W` is `passOn_wf` with only the
    bound `B` named (and `MemoNI` of the memo of earlier pokes).  **The pass
    loop returns at some fuel whenever each pass does** (`passLoop_returns`):
    if at every state the passes reach the pass stops on its own (it returns
    with a continuation that always fails) or goes on, each at some fuel, and
    `next` stays at most `B`, then the pass loop returns; by `Acc` of the
    passes (`passNext_acc`, from `passOn_wf_W` by dependent choice),
    `passOn_mono` and the fuel monotonicity of `AnaFuel.lean`.
    The bound `B`, in two parts.  (i) Finitely many identities are pushed
    from a root: on the model, for every root and memo there is a finite set
    of identities containing every identity that `analyzeStep` pushes in the
    run of `analyze env memoF n root (SkaState.init reg)`, at every fuel.
    `HeTurn`/`HeBound` give only that one `resolve` loop ends (each `%he` turn
    cuts `heMeasure`); the rest is Kruskal over `+he-sock` (a stack with more
    than `he-threshold` members of one formula generalizes, so the socks of
    one formula along the stack form a bad sequence) and finite branching,
    argued in `AnaEnds.lean`, not proved.  (ii) Point (b): an identity that a
    pass's `+cull` drops is not pushed again in a later pass of the same
    component.  **(b) is false on the real compiler** (2026-11-27,
    `tools/hoon/repush.hoon`, three `%full` pokes; the instrumented library
    of `passmeasure.py` prints a `%pm-new` for a mug that an earlier
    `%pm-cull` dropped; all three pokes return on the plain library).  Poke 1:
    a recursive `R` whose product is two shift registers that lose their last
    known slot one per pass; site `Y` calls `f` on `[[a x x |] K]` at pass 2
    only, site `X` at pass 5 only; `+cull` drops it at pass 3 and `X` pushes
    it again (the members of formula `f` on the stack fail `+hit-test` at it:
    the one at `[a x x x]` needs a slot it lacks, the ones at `[a x | |]`,
    `[a | | |]` made those calls indirect, so their `indi` says it knows
    more); 8 passes.  Poke 2: four sites, the identity pushed four times
    (passes 2, 5, 8, 11), 14 passes.  Poke 3: **one site pushes the same
    identity twice**: its subject is built from the product of `g` at an
    earlier site whose subject changes at pass 9; the fresh `g` restarts its
    shift register, so the site's subject goes back up from `[a x x |]` to
    `[a x x x]` and both are pushed again; 12 passes.  So a call site's
    subject does not only fall (a fresh entry's first product can know more
    than the entry it replaces), and the bound is not one push per (call
    site, subject) pair.  Search (`tools/hoon/passmeasure-repush.py`): the
    candidate shape worked at the first try; 4 multi-site variants (2 to 5
    sites: one identity pushed 2 to 5 times, 8 to 14 passes), 2 single-site
    variants (the first did not re-push because `g` did not read the slot
    that changed, so `+hit-test` sent the new subject to the old `g`; with
    that read, 2 pushes per identity, 14 and 16 passes) and 363 random cores
    (registers, 2 to 5 sites of a shared `f` that is itself a restarting
    shift register in 70 %, later sites built from earlier products): 13
    re-pushes, at most 2 pushes of one identity, at most 9 passes, every
    pass loop returned.  A chain of two restarting callees before the one
    site (mode `c`) pushes one identity 4 times (31 passes): the count can
    double with each level of the chain, so it is not bounded by the
    number of sites, but it stayed finite.  No poke whose pass loop did not
    return.
    Growth and non-termination on the real compiler (2026-11-27, step 1
    and 2 of track F; `passmeasure-repush.py c D N M..`,
    `passmeasure-tab.py`, `passmeasure-q.py`, `passmeasure-u.py`,
    `passmeasure-cyc.py`).  A chain of `D` restarting callees, `D` = 1 to
    8: the passes of the root's loop equal the slots of the registers
    (`N` + the `M_i`) within 5 % (15 passes for 14 slots up to 134 for 140
    at `D` = 8, 796 for 795 at `D` = 1), so the passes are linear in the
    size of the formula (cells), not exponential in `D`.  The pushes of
    the most pushed identity grow with `D` (2, 4, 8, 10, 12, 16, 18, 26
    with lengths falling by 3; 4, 8, 14, 24, 40 with Fibonacci lengths,
    which full doubling needs, `M_{k-1} > M_k + M_{k+1}`), always below a
    third of the passes: each push of one identity needs a cull between,
    and a cull ends a pass.  Wall time on the plain library is polynomial:
    about 0.6, 3.3, 22 and 75 s per `+ska-callgraph` call for one
    register of 195, 395, 795 and 1195 slots (`c 1 600 595`, the smallest
    input seen over 60 s; a power 2.5 to 3 of the size: the passes times
    the runs of a pass times the formula with its long axes).  That time
    is the plain iteration of a long register, not the re-pushes (one
    restarting callee, at most 2 pushes of one identity).  Search for a loop that does not
    return: 960 random cores with two shared formulas and feedback of
    site products into the root's registers (50 re-pushes, at most 18
    passes), 1280 random self-calls of one formula at many subjects (3690
    `%rec` takeovers, 69 back to an earlier target, 4 re-pushes, at most
    11 passes), 19 chains, and one period-3 takeover designed as a true
    cycle (`passmeasure-cyc.py`): every loop returned.  The designed cycle
    fails for two reasons that a proof can use: `fol-loop` analyses both
    branches of a `%6` whatever its test (the product is their
    `+double-int`), so a formula cannot dispatch on a known tag and every
    identity of it runs every site; and for a kid that passes `+hit-test`
    a `%rec` target's product, patched by `+patch-prod`, depends on the
    target only through precision (the parts it computed by indirect
    calls are unknown), so a value fed back must run to itself.  A point
    any measure must handle: a member is rerun only when `+dirty`, and
    its callee set is not among the five fields `changed` compares, so a
    member can change callees (a takeover) with its entry constant.
    Restated (`Ska/PassMeasureB.lean`): what `passOn_wf_W` and
    `passLoop_returns` use is only that `next` is bounded along each chain
    of passes; `next` grows by one at every push, so this is `FinPushes`,
    every chain of passes pushes finitely often (with (i): every identity is
    pushed finitely often along it), with no bound fixed in advance.  The
    old bound implies it (`finPushes_of_bound`), and `passOn_wf_fin`,
    `passNext_acc_fin`, **`passLoop_returns_fin`** go through with it.  What
    a proof of `FinPushes` needs: an entry present at two consecutive
    passes stays or falls, so an identity present from some pass on changes
    finitely often, and the root is never culled; then an eventual
    stabilisation along the evaluation order of each run (a site whose
    subject is eventually constant has an eventually constant callee once
    the `less-code` and `indi` that `+hit-test` reads stop changing, and that
    callee is then never culled), by induction over the sites in order and
    over the members reached.  The open point in that argument: the
    resolution depends on the stack, and a fresh push of another identity of
    the same formula, higher on the stack, can take over a site that
    resolved by `%rec`.
    How far this is from `CgHalts` (`+ska-callgraph` returns at some fuel):

    | loop | ends | where |
    |---|---|---|
    | `fol-loop` within one run | proved for formulas that do not inline (one level of inlining, not bounded by the weight) | `FolWeight.lean` (`folLoopR_at_weight`) |
    | `%he` turns of `resolve` | proved: each turn cuts `heMeasure` | `HeTurn.lean`, `HeBound.lean` |
    | the stack (finitely many identities) | argued (Kruskal over `+he-sock`), not proved | `AnaEnds.lean` header |
    | one entry | proved; `RunW` of every stack member proved | `entry_finite`, `Ana.run_entry_step`, `run_winv` |
    | the pass loop, passes | proved, with the bound `B` on `next` named, or finitely many pushes per chain | `passOn_wf_W`, `passOn_wf_fin` |
    | no culled identity pushed again (b) | false: `repush.hoon` (one site can push one identity twice); replaced by `FinPushes`, not proved; no loop that does not return in 2,260 searched cores, passes linear in the register slots | `PassMeasureB.lean`, `passmeasure-tab.py` |
    | the pass loop, fuel | proved, given that each pass returns at some fuel and `B`, or `FinPushes` | `passLoop_returns`, `passLoop_returns_fin` |
    | `+sweep`, `+cull` | structural over the members and their callee sets; fuel not bounded in Lean | `Analysis.lean` |
    | the member loop | structural over its list, each step a `+run` (the whole recursion) | `Analysis.lean` |
    | `+prune-callgraph` | proved on a closed graph | `pruneLoop_ends`, `pruneCallgraph_ends` |
    | the whole recursion (`+analyze` inside `fol-loop` inside `+run`) | not proved: needs a fuel bound from the above | |

    Still open: `FinPushes` ((i), and finitely many pushes of each identity
    along a chain, by the stabilisation above), the fuel of `+sweep` and `+cull` (a bound by the members and
    the largest callee set), and the fuel of `+run` inside the member loop,
    which is the whole recursion; so `CgHalts` is still a hypothesis.
17. **The pill link of the `+leap` family** (track I, 2026-11-27,
    `Compiler/CompLeap*.lean`, cut by `tools/gen/leap.py`).  Linked, each on
    every input or on a stated invariant, compared with the Hoon part by part
    with no departure: `+need-from` (`ndf_gate`) and `+need-cut`
    (`ndc_gate`) on every axis and need; `+pass-bomb` (`pbo_gate`) on every
    nomm; `+pass-then` (`pth_gate`), `+pass-edit` (`ped_gate`), `+pass`
    (`pas_link`) and `+pass-both` (`pbt_gate`) on lists of parts with no
    axis `0` (`PosL`) and nomms with no `%10` at axis `0` (`Bake.NoAx0`);
    `+lazy-leap` (`lzl_gate`) on lazy needs with `LAll CrLok` and positive
    `p` and `s`; and `+leap` itself (`lpx_gate`, and `lp6_leap` in the form
    of `comp_run_n6_k`, whose `hLp` is `lp6_leap_none`) on lazy needs with
    `LAll CrLok` and nomms with `Bake.NoAx0`.  The second condition is
    needed: on `[10 [0 v] q]` with `q` passing nothing and `v` passing a
    part, `+pass-edit` runs `(peg 0 p)` and crashes, while the model's
    `passEdit` is `some` (its `pegF` is total).  `+fol-loop` never makes
    such a nomm (it turns `[10 [0 v] q]` into `[0 0]`, the type of the nomm
    says `p.p != 0`, and `folLoop_noAx0` proves it of the model).  The first
    is needed as well: a look at `0` makes the `+gep` of the `+rep:in`
    crash, like the model's.  Positive `p` and `s` hold on the parts
    `+pass-both` returns (`PosL`), and `lzl_lall` keeps `LAll CrLok` for
    the `rec` the loop passes on.  Left for the users of `lp6_leap` (track
    A): `Bake.NoAx0` of the branches `q` and `r` of the `%6`.
    **Phase L of `%6` is linked** (2026-11-27, `Compiler/CompRunSixL.lean`,
    `Compiler/CompRunSixLInv.lean`).  The `?^  lep` branch has three parts:
    the push of `lep` (`cr6Lep`, 475 characters of Nock: `+leap`, then
    `[~ [%next rest then.goal] bys]`), the run of `$` on the subject with the
    goal (axis `118`) edited to `gol.u.lep` and the state put back (`cr6L0`,
    `cr6L1`), and the trap `cr6LB` over `bys`, which calls `+copy` (door arm
    `220590`, linked by `comp_copy_all`) on `nex` and `i.bys`, puts the state
    back and runs again with `t.bys`.  Each part is linked: `cr6l_head`,
    `cr6l_eq`, `cr6l_loop`.  The model takes the same steps in the same
    order (`sixNextLC` at fuel `2` on the rest, then `copyAll` in list
    order), so no departure was found.  The side conditions of each `+copy`
    (`CopyH`) hold because `+leap` keeps the invariant (`leapInv`): the rest
    and every `by` meet `LInv`, and no register is in two of them, since
    `+need-cut` splits the registers of a need and the turns and
    `+lazy-prune` only move or drop them.  The registers of the run's result
    are fresh or the rest's (`GRes`), and `copyL_inv` keeps that through the
    loop.  `comp_run_n6_l` takes `LeapTwice` (as `sixNextLC_fuel` does) and
    `Bake.NoAx0` of `q` and `r` as hypotheses.  It also takes the re-entry
    as the link `hRe` of the case on `[%next rest then]` in the same state,
    with its result `hReR`.  Phase S on a fork-free rest and phase K give
    them now (`comp_run_n6_l_nf`, `comp_run_n6_l_k`).  Phase F will give
    them through its own `CompRunAtR` theorem, and so will phase S on a
    rest whose forks and bonds ask nothing, once track A links them.
    `res_n6_l` gives what phase L returns (`GRes`).
    **`LeapTwice` is proved** (2026-11-27, `Ska/CompLeapTwice.lean`,
    `Ska/CompLeapTwiceRun.lean`): `leap_twice` on any lazy need whose
    branches pass parts with nonzero product axes, so `leapTwice_noAx0` on
    branches with `Bake.NoAx0`; no invariant of the lazy need is needed.
    The invariant of one round is `QuietL p s`: in every block, the part of
    the sure need at `p` is behind a register (`+need-cut` gives `~`), or it
    is `%none` and every look under `p` goes to `by` as the axis `1`.  It is
    exactly an empty `by` of `+lazy-leap p s`.  A pass at `p` leaves the
    rest quiet at `p`, and a pass at another `p'` keeps it: when that pass
    frees a part at `p` that was behind a register, `p` is under `p'`, so
    the pass also took every look under `p`.  Hence after the loop the rest
    is quiet at every pass, in any order, and the second loop finds nothing.
    Tested before the proof on the executable model (a computable set type,
    200,000 random lazy needs up to three levels of nested forks and bonds,
    with pass lists from 20 branches and random lists of up to four passes;
    182,001 first leaps gave a rest, 139,361 of them with forks or bonds
    left, no second leap).  The theorems that took `LeapTwice` are restated
    without it: `sixNextLC_fuel_tw` (the fuel `3` is the Hoon's unbounded
    recursion), `comp_run_n6_l_tw`, `res_n6_l_tw`, `comp_run_n6_l_nf_tw`,
    `comp_run_n6_l_k_tw`.
18. **Direct calls** (track J, 2026-11-27, `Ska/CompDirect.lean`, `tools/hoon/directdump.hoon`).  The map first, then what is proved and what blocks the first contract theorem.
   1. *How the model compiles a direct call.*  `callL` (`CompCall.lean`, the case `%2` of `runLC`) models `mono` `|` only, with no memo key, no ring and `sof` false: `+need-ord-alloc-regs` of the callee's n-ary need `bNed = env.need b` gives fresh registers `sub` (the arguments `flattenNeed sub`, preorder, `Clean` by `needOrdAllocRegs_clean`); under `%done` one block `[jmp b subV]`; otherwise `+simple-next`, `+kerf` (`out`, `pro`) and `[cal b subV pro]`, `hop out`; then `q` for an empty need (skipped by `safeFolFol`, which holds for the `[1 fol]` the analysis leaves and for every `[0 axe]`), `+past` over `p`, `p` for `%next (ofSure sub) callBlock`, `+copy` (`runLC_n2_direct`).  A pure callee whose product is not needed is dropped.  Baking does not enter `+comp` (the need of a baked variant is its bake, Gaps 12.6); inlining happens before (`+inlined`, and the analysis itself: `[2 [0 1] [1 [0 6]]]` has the nomm `[7 [0 1] [0 6]]`, first version of `directdump.hoon`), so only a callee that recurs shows a call in the raw blocks.
   2. *The finding: the whole-subject version of a direct call is not modelled* (by `finishLUC`; modelled now by `finishLUCM`, 7).  `finishLUC` runs `runLC`, which has no `mono`; the Hoon's `+compile-unary` runs `+run` with `mono` `&`, and a direct call then takes the callee's subject whole into one register, tests and splits it along `bNed` (`+mono-try-call`: `%clq`, `%hed`, `%tal`, `%mov`), calls the n-ary version on the parts (`%jmp`/`%cal`) and the whole-subject version on the whole when a part is missing (`%jsp`/`%csl`), and under `%next` the two call blocks hop with an argument to a merge block with a parameter (`par = [merged]`), so the goals' jumps have arguments.  On the real compiler (`directdump.hoon`, `%dt` = `[8 [1 R] [9 2 [0 1]]]`): eight blocks, `0w0 mov v0 v6`; `0w8 imm R v5`; `0w7 con v5 v6 v2`; `0w5 clq v2 4 2`; `0w4 hed v2 v4, tal v2 v3`; `0w3 mov v3 v1`; `0w1 jmp R-bell [v1]`; `0w2 jsp R-bell v2`; `%dn` (under `%4`): `cal R-bell [v3] v7` and `csl R-bell v6 v8` hopping with `[~ v7]`, `[~ v8]` to `0w3 (par v5) mov v5 v4`.  The model gives the two n-ary blocks (`dt_model`).  So a theorem about `finishLUC` of a nomm with a direct call speaks of code the Hoon does not produce; `runLC` with a direct call is exact for the n-ary version (`+compile-scc-in`), whose top is `finishLFC`, and no contract of `finishLFC` with calls exists yet.  The model's code is still correct on the tests: `dtRun` runs it with the interpreter model against an oracle that runs `+mink` on `R` and agrees with `+mink` on four subjects.
   3. *What the interpreter does* (`CompCallSem.lean`): `%cal`/`%jmp` ask the oracle `call st b false vs t gul` on the values of `subV`; `%csl`/`%jsp` ask `call st b true [v]`.  Forward `callStep_fwd`, `tailStep_fwd` (`CompCallStep.lean`); backward `callStep_bwd`, `tailStep_bwd` (here).
   4. *What a compositional theorem needs of the callee.*  Not that the callee's code meets `BellContract`: the contract is stated against every oracle with `OracleFwd`/`OracleBwd` whose needs include `N`, and the callee's `BellContract` is what makes the interpreter's own oracle meet `OracleFwd` (`Theorems.lean`, the induction on the interpreter's fuel `k`).  It needs: (a) `N b false = some bNed` (`CalleeNeeds`), so that the values passed are `argsFor (ν st) b false` of the callee's subject (`argsFor_of_alloc`); for the whole-subject version also `N b true ≠ none` (the pessimistic call by subject); (b) the facts of `Annot.direct` (the products of `p` fit `b.less`, the product of `q` is `b.fol`); (c) for the n-ary version only, `CalleeNec`: on a product of `p` that does not split by `bNed` (`fits_of_split`, `split_of_holds`: exactly where the registers cannot hold it), `+mink` on `b.fol` crashes at once with the trace as it is, since the caller's code crashes in `p`'s split before `q` and the call run.  The Hoon ensures (c) through `sof` (`+sound` of `made p sup`, else `+need-soft` against `+callee-sure` and `p`, `q` not `+hinted`), which the model leaves as the parameter `env.sof`.  The whole-subject version needs no (c).  (d) `Annot.direct` does not say that `q` returns, and `safeFolFol` skips `[0 axe]`, which crashes off the subject: a theorem with a skipped `[0 axe]` needs that `q` returns on the subjects (the Hoon only knows the formula when the sock knows that axis, so the analysis gives it).
   5. *The blocker of a direct call inside `CallTree7`.*  The relations of the class (`RunOKLB`, `RunOKLS`, `LOKS`, `RunOKGB`, `SemLB`, `SemGB`) quantify over every oracle with `OracleFwd lib Inv ν O n`, with no hypothesis linking `ν` to the needs the compiler used.  They are false for any run with a direct call: take `ν st b false = some NeedOrd.this` for the callee `R-bell` of `%dt` (need `[~ this]`); the code passes the sample `s` of the callee's subject `[R s]`, which is its split under `[~ this]` but no split under `this` of a subject of `R-bell` unless `s` itself fits `R-bell`'s sock, so `OracleFwd` puts no constraint on the answer.  A direct call needs those relations with a parameter `N` and the hypothesis `∀ st, Inv st → N ≤ ν st` in `sem` and `semB` (the existing relations are the case of the empty `N`, and the call-free and indirect parts lift to it by dropping the hypothesis).  The tops are ready for it: `finishTop_genB` already passes that hypothesis to `fwdPhi`/`bwdPhi`, so `finishLUC_contractGB` with `N` is its text with the hypothesis handed to `R.sem`.  The `%cal` form needs in addition (i) `kerfLB` with `N` and a call block lemma like `nokLB` (`callLB`: `p`'s `RunOKLS` under `%next (ofSure sub) (jmp0 o)` gives the inner run under `%next (lazy-from-reg pro) (jmp0 out)`, through `callStep_fwd`, `callStep_bwd`, `CalleeNec`), with one catch: `+need-ord-alloc-regs` runs before `+kerf`, so `sub` lies below the state from which `kerfLB` starts, and the registers that `RunOKLB`'s `K` keeps (`x < g0.re`) would include `sub`, which `p` writes; the lemma must start from the state before the allocation; (ii) a parent at the top (`[7 d [0 1]]` through `runOKGB_done`, or `runOKGB_sevenL`) with `N`.  The `%jmp` form at the top (`[2 p [1 k] b]` under `%done`) has no `K` (`GoalSemCM` of `%done` is `ReturnsC`) and no `+kerf`; for `p = [0 1]` its run is the allocation, `[jmp b subV]`, the `%next` of `p` returning its goal, and `+copy` with the empty need, and the contract follows from `finishTop_genB` with `tailStep_fwd`, `tailStep_bwd` and `CalleeNec`, but it is a theorem about `finishLUC`, so about code the Hoon does not produce (2).
   6. *Recursion* (a call to a bell of the same SCC), not attempted.  The compiler side needs no induction of its own: the caller's contract is against any oracle, and the recursion is the interpreter's fuel induction of `Theorems.lean` (`OracleFwd … (interp lib poke k) k` to `k + 1`, each call running its callee with less `+mink` fuel).  What it needs is (a) for the members of the SCC: the need `map-local`/`top` that `+callee-need` read during `+compile-scc-in` must be the need of the final code, `N b false` in `needOf poke st'` of the state the poke leaves (`Sound.dire_code`), so a statement that the fixed point of the SCC is reached when the code is emitted; and for the whole-subject version `N b true`.  `Compiler/SkaJoint.lean`'s joint induction on the depth of calls is for the analysis (`E j` running batteries with `nockW (E (j - 1))`), not for `+comp`.
   7. *The whole-subject version, modelled* (`CompDirectM.lean`, `CompRunLCM.lean`, `CompDirectMEq.lean`, `CompDirectMCheck.lean`, `CompDirectMTop.lean`, `tools/hoon/directdump2.hoon`).  This answers the finding of 2.  `monoTryLoop`/`monoTryCall` is `+mono-try-call` as a function of the callee's need with registers: at a cell `[h t]` of the need, fresh `r-t` then `r-h`, the tail's tree into the success block, the head's into the tail's, `[hed r r-h] [tal r r-t]`, and `[clq r split fail]`; at `this rn` a block `[mov r rn]`; `%both` as a cell with `[mov r rn]` after the split ops; `%none` the success block itself.  `monoBlocks`, `callDM`, `callLM env mono` are the case `%2` with `mono` (`callL` when `mono` is `|` or the call is indirect), and `runLCM env mono` (generated from the text of `runLC` by `tools/gen/runlcm.py`; `runLC`, `callL`, `finishLUC` are unchanged) passes `mono` to every run.  With `mono` `&` the case reads no `sof`: `+soft-new` is under `?.  mono`, and the call block `+mono-try-call` returns is fresh, so `+soft-bond` is not reached; so `callDM` is exact for every direct call with no memo key and no ring, sound or soft.  `finishLUCM` is `+compile-unary` with `runLCM env true`. The theorems of 9 and 11 apply unchanged: they assume nothing of the callee's code, only `env.need b = some bNed` (the need `+callee-need` read during the compile, `map-local` or `top` of `+compile-scc-in`) with `N b false = some bNed` (the needs of the contract, that the oracle's `ν` includes), `N b true ≠ none`, and `env.ring b = none` (a member of an SCC is not a hot jet, whose need `+callee-need` takes first). 20. **`+to-straight` with proxies: the map path** (track I, 2026-11-27,
    `Compiler/CompStraightMap*.lean`, nouns cut by `tools/gen/tsmap.py`).
    The map first.
    (1) Linked before this item: `+to-straight` (door arm `6892`, gate
    `tsBat`, 4716 cells) on its renaming branch only.  The pushes are
    linked with `cond` empty (`ts_pre`, `tsn_pre`: the third push,
    `proxies`, is `(A 1)` there), the arm `$` takes `&(run !proxies)`
    (`ts_dol`, `tsn_dol`), and the fold is `renameBlocks` (`ts_core_at`,
    `tsn_core`, `tsn_at`).  So the whole-subject final pass
    (`cu_finish_all`, `CompUnaryTop.lean`) is linked only where `cond` is
    empty after `+kerf` (`cu_cond_empty`: a pure nomm without `%6`).  On
    the model `+kerf` makes a proxy on every enumerated nomm with a `%6`
    (track D), so every whole-subject compile with a `%6` takes the map
    path.
    (2) What the branch with `proxies` `&` runs.  The `|^` core
    (`tsCoreBat`, 4392 cells) has thirteen arms.  The renaming branch uses
    `$` (battery axis `126`, 509 cells, of which its own branch is 163 and
    the map branch `tsMapBr` 295), `rewrite-input` (`14`, 217), `rer`
    (`12`, 135), `+bypassable` (`30`, 58), `+chase` (`13`, 78) and
    `+chase-fin` (`254`, 582), all linked.  The map branch uses, for every
    kept block, `rewrite-par` (battery `250`, core arm `378`, 58 cells:
    `?:  =(~ par)`, else `+spin` of layer 2 with the gate `(rer(gen gen)
    r)`), `rewrite-body` (`502`, core `758`, 65 cells: the same `+spin`
    with `rewrite-op`), `rewrite-op` (`2`, core `4`, 1605 cells: the 25-way
    `?-` on the op tags, one `=^` through `rer` per register field),
    `rewrite-fin` (`1006`, core `1518`, 823 cells: the 11-way `?-`, the
    branch jumps through `rewrite-jump` (`255`, core `383`, 38 cells:
    `(rewrite-args (chase & j))`), the hop through `rewrite-hop` (`124`,
    core `188`, 38 cells: `(chase | j)`), `%bom` through `+chase`) and
    `rewrite-args` (`1007`, core `1519`, 174 cells: `?:  =(~ args.j)`,
    else `+spin` over the units, `rer` on each `u.a`).  None of these
    seven is linked.  The branch is `[input (count-args input) =<(new
    (~(rep by blocks) gate))]`: the first accumulator is `[~ B]` with `B`
    the current `gen` (the example of `_gen` is `[0 14]`; the formula
    `[7 [9 170 0 0xfffff] 9 2 0 1]` next to it is the bunt of `blob`, arm
    `170` of the types core, for the pair `[k b]`), and the gate does `=.  gen  gen-acc`, the test `&(!=(0 k)
    (bypassable & b))` (on yes `[new gen]`), else three `=^` (par, body,
    fin) and `[(put:by new k [par1 body1 fin1]) gen1]`.  Every register
    goes through `rer`, whose `=?  r  proxies  (unproxy r)` slams the gate
    `unproxy` (`tsUnBat`: `+get:by` of `cond.gen` at `r`, `def.u.e` on a
    hit) and which, with `run` `&`, returns `[(ren r) gen]` and leaves
    `gen`.  The pushes differ from the linked ones only in `proxies`: `&`
    (`A 0`) when the noun of `cond` is a cell.
    (3) The model's map path is `toStraightF` with `hasProxies cond`
    (`Ska/CompStraightMap.lean`): `rewriteInput`, then `mapBlocks` with
    `rewriteBlob` (`rewritePar`, `rewriteBody` through `rewriteOp`,
    `rewriteFin` through `rewriteJump`, `rewriteHop`, `rewriteArgs`), each
    register by `rer cond proxies run ren` in the state monad `RM`.  On a
    run of inputs `rer` reads and writes no state, and `toStraightF_run`
    (through `mapBlocks_stateless`) makes the map path `renameBlocks bs
    (proxRen cond true ren)`, whose `keepBlock` renames by `proxRen` and
    chases with `chaseFin`.  The whole-subject compile has the one input
    `sub`, so `run` is `&` there and only this stateless case occurs: the
    state `[re-gen m]`, and so the bunt `B`, is never read.  The stateful
    case (`run` `|`) occurs only in the n-ary final pass when a part of
    the input was baked or coerced; it is not in this item.
    (4) The plan: link each rewrite gate in any core whose `rer` returns
    `[f r G]` with `G` the core's own `gen`, against `renPole f`,
    `renJmp f` after `chase`, and `chaseFin bs f`; then the gate of the
    fold against `renameStep bs f`, the branch by `rep_gate_list`, `$`
    with `proxies` `&`, the pushes with any `cond`, and `+to-straight` on
    `tsSample` for every `cond` (`TsAt` without its hypothesis), which
    ends `+compile-unary` for code with proxies.
    L, 2026-11-27 (track I, the map path), `Compiler/CompStraightMap.lean`,
    `Compiler/CompStraightMapNouns.lean` (`tools/gen/tsmap.py`),

    `tsmArgBuntF1` is an autocons `[[7 .. 9 2 0 1] [0 30]]`, whose product
    is the cell `[~ gen]`: no core met it, so every lemma on `MapCoreS`
    held vacuously.  Restated as `some (C (A 0) G)` (`tsm_args` spins from
    `[~ gen]`, as `tsm_par` does); every proof went through unchanged.
    L, 2026-11-27 (track I, the pill link closed),
    `Compiler/CompStraightMapPre.lean` (`tools/gen/tsmpre.py`),
    `Compiler/CompStraightMapAt.lean`, `Compiler/CompStraightMapSix.lean`
    (`tools/gen/tsmsix.py`).  The third push is `?:(=(~ cond.gen) | &)`,
    so `proxies` is `&` exactly when the noun of `cond` is a cell, which
    is `hasProxies cond` (`hasProxies_cell`; without proxies `cond` is
    empty, `hasProxies_none`).  The states take the noun `PX` of
    `proxies` (`tsS5P`, `tsS7P`, `tsCoreStP`, `tsStP`); the pushes that
    do not read it are copied from `CompStraightN.lean` with `PX`
    (`tsnp_r_layer1`, `tsnp_pre2`, `tsnp_ren`, `tsnp_pre3`,
    `tsnp_pre3_nil`), pushes 1 to 5 with `cond` a cell (`tsnp_pre1`), and
    `tsnp_pre` is the gate up to the call of `$` with `proxies` `&`.  The
    two bunts are kernel facts on a core whose rest is fifteen free nouns
    then `K` (`tsm_rep_bunt_sp`: `[[0 *blob] [~ [0 0]]]`, the blob bunt
    being `[~ ~ %bom ~]`, `tsm_blob_bunt_eq`; `tsm_arg_bunt_sp`: `[~ [0
    0]]`): no slot there returns a free noun whole, so `evalFuel` reduces
    with them free, in under a second; the real core is that spine once
    `ctx` is `[c2 c6 K]` (`ctx_of_slot7`).  With `tsm_unproxy`,
    `tsnp_ren` and the slots, `tsm_core` gives the core (`tsmp_core`:
    `renameBlocks bs (renReg r0 n-in ∘ unproxy cond)`), the gate with
    proxies is `toStraightF` by `toStraightF_run` (`tsmp_at`), and on
    every `cond` (`ts_at_all`: `tsn_at` when `hasProxies cond` is false).
    So track A's `TsMapAt` holds (`tsMapAt_holds`), its tops hold with no
    hypothesis on `+to-straight` (`cu_gate_scc_one_six_ts`,
    `cu_gate_scc_one_six_fol_ts`: only `FinSure` and the state nouns
    left), the gate of `+compile-unary` on `SixOk` code is `finishLUC`
    whatever proxies `+kerf` leaves (`cu_gate_six_ts`, track A's
    `cu_gate_six` with `Q` true), and the whole-subject `TsAt` of
    `CompUnaryTop.lean` holds in every context of `+comp`
    (`ts_at_this`, `ts_at_proved`).  `TsOn` asks a run of inputs (its
    premise `isRun .. = true`), so the stateful case (`run` `|`, the
    n-ary pass with a baked or coerced input) is not needed by
    `TsMapAt`; it is still not linked.
